**Service handle** captured once via `Initialize(ICampService)` from `IoC.InitializePatchStatics`, the single post-registration block (moved out of `FieldCampIoC` when registration-time eager resolves were banned: resolving `ICampService` mid-registration materialized its contributor collection before Refuge registered, review round A HIGH). Widget construction and the sprite memo live in `CampNameplateIconPresenter`; the patch file is a 68-line guarded delegate. Sprite names are vanilla atlas entries; a missing entry degrades to an invisible icon by design.

## Patch75_Refuge

**Targets:** `ClanPartiesVM.RefreshPartiesList()` (public, Postfix) + `ClanPartyItemWithPartyVM.UpdateProperties()` (Postfix, #994) + `PlayerEncounter.DoMeeting()` (public static, Prefix). Three patch classes, one category.

**Feature:** Refuge, `Main/Features/Refuge/Hooks/RefugeClanScreenPatch.cs` + `RefugeClanRowRefreshPatch.cs` + `RefugeEncounterPatch.cs`. **Status:** ACTIVE.

The clan-screen postfix appends every refuge (ready, building, orphan-adopted) to the Garrisons list as `ClanPartyItemWithPartyVM` rows (selection wired via AccessTools to the private `OnPartySelection`). The `UpdateProperties` postfix re-applies the refuge row decoration (hidden wage slider, "(being raised)" suffix on a building refuge) after every refresh, because vanilla's `UpdateProperties` resets `Name` and the wage state; it shares that method with Patch17's TroopWeight postfix and touches different fields. The encounter prefix is click-to-manage: when the LOCAL player meets their own ready refuge, the meeting conversation is swapped for the refuge menu (`return false`); suppressed while enlisted, because Enlistment owns encounter policy (INV-D1). Co-op disposition: ReviewedSafe (player-local UI redirect, no shared mutation skipped; `CoopVetoClassificationTests`).

**What is deliberately NOT here:** the source module's two combat patches (`SimulateHit`, `ApplyDamageReductions`). Both targets are TAOM-owned model slots, so the defender damage reduction rides the model chain instead: `TaomCombatMechanicsModel.ApplyDamageReductions` (real-time) and `TaomCombatSimulationModel.SimulateHit` (auto-resolve), both consulting `IRefugeDefenseService`. One decision, two consumers, no Harmony ordering accident.

## Patch76_UncapturableHeroes

**Targets:** `Hero.CanBecomePrisoner()` (public instance, Postfix) + `TakePrisonerAction.Apply(PartyBase, Hero)` (public static, Prefix). Two patch classes, one category.

**Feature:** UncapturableHeroes (#513), `Main/Features/UncapturableHeroes/Hooks/Hero_CanBecomePrisoner_Patch.cs` + `TakePrisonerAction_Apply_Patch.cs`. **Status:** ACTIVE.

Sauron and the Nine are never taken prisoner; they escape as fugitives instead. **The escape is free and that is the whole design.** `MapEvent.CaptureDefeatedPartyMembers` gates capture on `CanBecomePrisoner()` at `MapEvent.cs:1983`, and when that gate fails the hero is still in the defeated member roster, so vanilla's own fall-through at `MapEvent.cs:2004-2008` applies `MakeHeroFugitiveAction`. The postfix writes the veto; the engine writes the escape.

**Do not try to use `CampaignEvents.CanHeroBecomePrisonerEvent` instead.** `Hero.cs:2010-2012` returns `true` unconditionally for every hero that is not `MainHero`, *before* the dispatcher is reached, so that event never fires for an AI lord. The method has to be patched. `CanBecomePrisoner()` has exactly two engine call sites: the battle gate above, and `EncounterGameMenuBehavior.cs:1892` (player voluntary surrender, `MainHero`-guarded on our side).

The prefix covers the capture routes that never consult the gate at all. The one that matters is `PrisonerCaptureCampaignBehavior.cs:67` (a hero inside a settlement that changes hands, or whose host declares war). It targets the PUBLIC `Apply` rather than private `ApplyInternal`: the only caller `Apply` misses is `ApplyByTakenFromPartyScreen`, already unreachable for a hero because `PlayerEncounter.DoCaptureHeroes` (`PlayerEncounter.cs:1611`) strips every hero out of `RosterToReceiveLootPrisoners` first. Three guards carry it: `MainHero` defers, an already-`IsPrisoner` hero defers (`MakeHeroFugitiveAction` never removes anyone from a `PrisonRoster`, so converting would leave him Fugitive and still held), and a failed escape returns `true` so vanilla capture proceeds rather than stranding him.

**Both hooks must fail open.** `PatchShield.ShieldFinalizerWithResult` swallows `Missing*`/`TypeLoad` and the patched method then returns `default(bool)`, which for the postfix means `false` = every hero in the game uncapturable. Each body is wrapped in its own try/catch that leaves the vanilla answer alone.

Does NOT block death: a hero who dies in the battle still dies (`MapEvent.cs:1977` skips the whole block for `DiedInBattle`/`DiedInLabor`), and that is deliberate. Co-op disposition: ReviewedSafe (lore-fixed identity from compiled roster plus shipped data; `CoopVetoClassificationTests`). The `MapEvent` fall-through premise is pinned by an IL test in `UncapturableHeroesBindingTests`, because if it ever moves the failure is completely silent in game.

**Three drift hazards this category is unusually exposed to, all pinned by `UncapturableHeroesBindingTests`.** (1) Harmony binds the prefix's parameters BY NAME, so a TaleWorlds rename of `capturerParty`/`prisonerCharacter` would silently feed the prefix nulls and turn the seam into a no-op that resolving-by-type would not catch; a `ParameterInfo.Name` assertion covers it. (2) A `Missing*`/`TypeLoad` raised while JITting the postfix fires BEFORE its own try/catch is entered, so the patch cannot self-guard; it reaches `PatchShield`'s finalizer, which returns `default(bool)` = `false` = every hero uncapturable, and because `PatchShieldPolicy.CompiledProtectedOwnerPrefixes` contains `"TAOM"` the shield refuses to unpatch, making that state persist for the session. Every engine member the postfix touches, including `KillCharacterActionDetail.None`, therefore has a binding assertion. (3) The postfix carries `[HarmonyPriority(Priority.Last)]` so another mod's postfix cannot re-grant capture after TAOM denies it; the reverse stays open, since the guard never flips `false` to `true`.

**Known behavioural interaction, deliberate:** `LordConversationsCampaignBehavior.cs:3072-3076` / `:3145-3149` ("You are my prisoner now.") are not `IsPrisoner`-gated, so a protected hero escapes after the player picks the line. Both pass `MainParty` as the captor, so the escape message fires immediately and resolves it narratively. Not gated, on purpose.

## Patch77_PlayerSwitcher

**Targets:** `BodyGeneratorView` constructor (public instance, Postfix, bound by ARITY) + `BodyGeneratorView.OnFinalize()` (public instance, Postfix). Two patch classes, one category.

**Feature:** PlayerSwitcher (#514), `Main/Features/PlayerSwitcher/Hooks/Patch77_BodyGeneratorView_Constructor.cs` + `Patch77_BodyGeneratorView_OnFinalize.cs`. **Status:** ACTIVE.

Attaches the hero picker to the character creation face generator, so the player can take over an existing lord. The panel itself does nothing; the handover runs from an `ICharacterCreationContentHandler` at priority 1100 and needs no Harmony at all.

**Bind by arity, never by a `Type[]`.** The 1.4.8 constructor takes THIRTEEN parameters; the predecessor mod pinned a 12-type attribute and 1.4.8 added `FaceGenHistory`, so that attribute matches nothing and throws at `PatchCategory` time, which bricks startup. `TargetMethods()` yields the single declared constructor and `Prepare()` refuses to bind when more than one exists. `AccessTools.Constructor(typeof(BodyGeneratorView))` with no type array is NOT a substitute: Harmony normalises a null parameter array to `Type.EmptyTypes` and looks for a parameterless constructor, which does not exist. `PlayerSwitcherBindingTests` asserts both the count and the arity against the installed DLLs.

**The `ActiveState is CharacterCreationState` guard is load-bearing.** The barber screen and the multiplayer face generator construct the same view, and an unguarded postfix would load the picker into both.

**Clearing the selection on construction IS the selection lifecycle.** The view is rebuilt on every entry to the face generator stage, so leaving to the culture stage and returning resets it by itself; no patch on `ExecuteDone`, `ExecuteCancel` or `ResetFaceToDefault` is needed. The `OnFinalize` postfix deliberately does NOT clear, because it fires when leaving the stage in EITHER direction and the selection must survive advancing forward.

**`IsPickerHidden` returns before the clear (#704).** While Kysaro's faction screen is the picker, the postfix attaches nothing, and it must not clear either: the faction screen sets this feature's selection for a hero taken over there, and its stage skip leaves the face generator in place when the stage list is not vanilla's, so a clear here would cancel that handover. `FactionUIBindingTests` pins the check in the source.

**Sprite teardown is conditional on purpose.** `SpriteCategory` carries an `IsLoaded` bool and no reference count, so `ui_clan` is unloaded only when this patch was the one that loaded it. An unconditional `Unload()` would blank another consumer's icons with no error and no crash.

Applied from `SubModule.cs` inside try/catch alongside `Patch9_RaceFilter`; a failure disables the feature for the session rather than taking startup down. Full design: [player-switcher.md](../features/player-switcher.md).

## Patch78_PlayerSwitcher_CareerFastPath

**Target:** `CharacterCreationManager.StartNarrativeStage()` (public instance, Postfix)

**Feature:** PlayerSwitcher (#514), `Main/Features/PlayerSwitcher/Hooks/Patch78_CharacterCreationManager_StartNarrativeStage.cs`. **Status:** ACTIVE.

Sends a player who has already picked a lord straight to the career menu, past the six backstory menus (parent, childhood, education, youth, adulthood, age selection) whose answers that player never keeps. Those answers grant skills, attributes and traits to the character-creation hero, and Patch77's handover deletes that hero at finalize, carrying across only the career. The career menu is the one genuine choice left in the stage, which is why the walk stops there instead of running to the end.

**One call site, and it is why there is no flicker.** `StartNarrativeStage` is called exactly once in the shipped game (v1.4.8): from `SandBox.GauntletUI.CharacterCreation.CharacterCreationNarrativeStageView`'s constructor, BEFORE it builds `CharacterCreationNarrativeStageVM` and before `LoadMovie`. The walk therefore finishes while no UI exists to render the menus it passes through. Verified by decompiling the installed `SandBox.GauntletUI.dll`; the `E:\Decompiled_Bannerlord\` dump covers only core `TaleWorlds.*` assemblies and contains no call site at all, which is a trap worth remembering when grepping for callers of a UI-adjacent method.

**Postfix, not prefix.** Vanilla has to set `CurrentMenu` to the head of the chain first; the walk moves forward from wherever vanilla left it.

**Selecting before advancing is mandatory, not tidiness.** `TrySwitchToNextMenu` opens with `SelectedOptions[CurrentMenu].OnConsequence(this)`, an indexer, so a menu nothing was chosen for throws `KeyNotFoundException`. The walk drives the same public transition a real click drives (`GetSuitableNarrativeMenuOptions` → `OnNarrativeMenuOptionSelected` → `TrySwitchToNextMenu`), which both avoids that throw and leaves `SelectedOptions` fully populated for the review stage and the trait XP pass. It needs no reflection: `CurrentMenu`'s private setter and `ModifyMenuCharacters()` are never touched.

**A zero-option menu aborts the walk.** Vanilla's own `CanAdvanceToNextStage()` returns `true` for a menu with no options, so advancing one is the crash above waiting to happen. The walk stops and logs instead, leaving the player in the ordinary backstory flow.

**Gated solely on `IPlayerSwitchSession.HasSelection`**, matching `PlayerSwitchContentHandler`. An ordinary character creation never enters the walk. The gate re-evaluates on every entry to the stage, because the view is rebuilt each time, so deselecting restores the full questionnaire and re-picking restores the fast path. A hero taken over on Kysaro's faction screen (#704) sets the same selection, so the walk serves that takeover unchanged.

Applied from `SubModule.cs` in its **own** try/catch beside Patch77's: the fast path is a convenience, so losing it must not disable the picker, and a Patch77 failure must not stop this binding. `PlayerSwitcherBindingTests` pins all five engine members the walk calls. Full design: [player-switcher.md](../features/player-switcher.md).

## Patch79_TooltipDiagnostics

**Target:** `MapInfoItemVM.ExecuteBeginHint()` (public instance, Postfix) and `GauntletInformationView.OnShowTooltip(Type, object[])` (private instance, Postfix)

**Dedicated server (ADR-013, B6c):** not applied (`SubModule.ApplyTooltipProbes`).

**Feature:** DevConsole, `Main/Features/DevConsole/Hooks/Patch79_MapInfoItemVM_ExecuteBeginHint_Probe.cs` + `Patch79_GauntletInformationView_OnShowTooltip_Probe.cs`. **Status:** ACTIVE, SCAFFOLDING (remove once the map bar tooltip question is answered).

Two probes that make silent tooltip failures visible. Diagnostic only: neither changes behaviour, both swallow their own exceptions, and both rate limit per key.

**Why probes and not reading a log.** Both failure paths this targets are silent by construction. In v1.4.8 the map bar's tooltips stopped being fixed properties on `MapInfoVM` and became list items (`PrimaryInfoItems` / `SecondaryInfoItems` of `MapInfoItemVM`); the `HintWidget` in the item template fires `ExecuteBeginHint` on the list element, and Gauntlet resolves that by walking the binding path and invoking it null-conditionally, so a failed resolution does nothing at all. Separately, `GauntletInformationView.OnShowTooltip` has three silent exits: a requested type missing from `RegisteredTypes`, a throw inside `Activator.CreateInstance(value.TooltipType, ...)`, and a throw inside `LoadMovie` after the view model was already constructed. The last two share one catch that downgrades to `Debug.FailedAssert`. `Debug.FailedAssert` reaches only `rgl_log`, and there is no `rgl_log` on this install, so without these probes none of the three is observable.

**Probe B reads two private fields on purpose.** `OnHideTooltip()` nulls `_dataSource` and `_movie` at the top of every call; the success path assigns `_dataSource` after construction and `_movie` only after `LoadMovie` returns. So after the call, both set means built, `_dataSource` alone means the movie failed (exit three), and neither means nothing was constructed (exits one and two). The first cut read only `_dataSource` and would have reported a movie failure as success; the deep review caught it (RCA `rca-tooltip-diagnostics-2026-09-04.md`, F2). Both `FieldInfo`s resolve once in `Initialize`, never in the postfix (`harmony-patches.md` bans uncached reflection on a per-hover path), and a failed resolution logs a warning rather than leaving the probe quietly useless.

**Read the two together:** Probe A silent means the hover never reached the view model, so the binding path is broken. Probe A firing plus a FAILED line from B names the failing stage: CONSTRUCTION (unregistered type, constructor threw, or not a `TooltipBaseVM`) or MOVIE (the prefab failed to load). Both quiet on a missing tooltip points past this layer, at rendering or the widget tree.

**Rate limited per key, deliberately not per latch.** `TooltipProbeLog` keys hovers on `ItemId` and builds on type plus outcome (a value tuple, so the steady-state membership check allocates nothing), so hovering gold cannot silence food, and a tooltip that fails and later succeeds reports both. A single global latch is the defect already present in `SpecialResourceMapBarMixin`, where the first failure is logged and every later different failure is invisible for the life of the process.

Applied from `SubModule.cs` in `OnSubModuleLoad`, in its own try/catch, for the Patch62 reason: both targets run from the main menu onward, well before any game init.

## Patch80_KingdomVoteDeadlock

**Target:** `KingdomDecisionsVM.RefreshWith(KingdomDecision)` (Prefix, seam A; and a second Postfix, seam D, `Priority.Last`) + `KingdomDecisionsVM.HandleDecision(KingdomDecision)` (Postfix) + `DecisionItemBaseVM.ExecuteFinalSelection()` (Postfix, `Priority.Last`) + `DecisionItemBaseVM.ExecuteDone()` (Prefix, seam E; protected, named by string)

**Dedicated server (ADR-013, B6c):** not applied (`SubModule.ApplyKingdomVoteDeadlockFix`). All five classes target view models a server never builds; the campaign concludes an election without them, and co-op clients keep the fix.

**Feature:** Diplomacy, `Main/Features/Diplomacy/Hooks/`. **Status:** ACTIVE. Issues #547 (seams A to C), #550 (seams D and E).

Stops a kingdom decision window that can never be closed. Player reports: the vote resolves, the verdict shows, and the popup stays with no usable control, forcing a Task Manager kill. It is not a crash and not a freeze; the campaign keeps ticking and the portrait encyclopedia links still work.

**The vanilla defect (v1.4.8, verified against the installed engine).** `KingdomDecisionsVM.RefreshWith` is the only method that builds the popup and it never calls `ShouldBeCancelled()`. It constructs the item view model, whose `InitValues()` runs `KingdomElection.StartElection()`; that sets `IsCancelled = true` and stops, and the window opens regardless. On Done, `KingdomElection.ApplySelection()` is `if (!IsCancelled) { ... }`, a silent no-op, so `KingdomDecisionConcluded` never fires and `IsKingsDecisionOver` stays false. `KingdomDecisionPopupWidget` therefore never gets the `false → true` edge that starts its five-second auto-close timer (that timer, not a click, is what normally closes the popup), so `ExecuteDone` never runs and `IsActive` is never set false. Meanwhile `ExecuteFinalSelection` has already set `_finalSelectionDone`, which forces `CanEndDecision = false` and disables the popup's only button. `GauntletKingdomScreen` also holds `NavigationHandler.IsNavigationLocked` while the window is active, which is why it reads as a frozen game.

**How a ballot goes stale mid-sequence.** `AllianceCampaignBehavior.OnWarDeclared` queues one `ProposeCallToWarAgreementDecision` per allied kingdom. Voting yes runs `StartCallToWarAgreement` → `DeclareWarAction.ApplyByCallToWarAgreement` synchronously, which re-enters `OnWarDeclared` and can drag the next ballot's `CalledKingdom` into the same war; that flips its `IsAllowed()` to false, so `ShouldBeCancelled()` becomes true. TAOM amplifies the frequency rather than causing it: `TaomAllianceModel` sets `MaxNumberOfAlliances => int.MaxValue`, so a single war declaration can queue many back-to-back ballots.

**Three seams, because there are three holes.** A (`RefreshWith` Prefix) refuses to build a window for a stale ballot; it covers both call sites, including `KingdomManagementVM.ForceDecideDecision` (:696, wired to the Settlement/Clan/Policy/Diplomacy tabs plus `OnGrantFief` :711 and `OnConfirmAbdicateLeadership` :805), which checks nothing at all. B (`ExecuteFinalSelection` Postfix) force-closes any window whose election is already cancelled. `IsCancelled` is written only in `Setup()`/`StartElection()`, which run once per item view model, so a window cannot go stale after it opens: seam B is not a race guard, it is the backstop for when seam A never got to judge (binding unresolved, staleness check faulted, or a future path reaching the item VM without passing through `RefreshWith`). C (`HandleDecision` Postfix) repairs the queue: vanilla's own bail-out sets `_shouldCheckForDecision = false` without recording the ballot, so every later decision that session is silently never offered, and reopening the Kingdom screen is the only reset, which is exactly the workaround players found.

**Why suppressing is safe.** Vanilla's hourly `KingdomDecisionProposalBehavior.UpdateKingdomDecisions` already deletes every decision for which `ShouldBeCancelled()` is true and raises `KingdomDecisionCancelled`. TAOM only declines to display what the engine is about to discard, and deliberately does not remove it, so the pruner keeps ownership of removal and the event fires once.

**Seam B closes through `_onDecisionOver`, not `ExecuteDone`.** `ExecuteDone` opens with `KingdomDecisionMaker.GetChosenOutcomeText()`, which dereferences the election's `_chosenOutcome`, null on a cancelled election. Calling it would trade the hang for an NRE. `Patch80KingdomVoteDeadlockBindingTests` pins that premise, along with `ApplySelection` still reading `IsCancelled`, both patch-target parameter names (Harmony binds by name), and the five members reached by reflection.

**Seam D (2026-09-15, #550): the election that was over before the window existed.** A second mechanism, independent of staleness. When the player is not a supporter of the election (`Supporter.IsPlayer => Clan.Leader.IsHumanPlayerCharacter`, which is false whenever `Clan.PlayerClan.Leader != Hero.MainHero`), `KingdomElection.StartElection()` takes `ReadyToAiChoose()` and applies the outcome synchronously, firing `KingdomDecisionConcluded` while `DecisionItemBaseVM`'s constructor is still running (listener at :358, `InitValues()` at :360, `StartElection()` at :399 on v1.5.3). The view model's own handler sets `IsKingsDecisionOver = true` before it is bound and `IsCancelled` stays false, so seams A to C never fire. The popup's close is the widget's five-second timer, started only by the false-to-true edge of `KingdomDecisionPopupWidget.IsKingsDecisionDone` (:24), which is reused across decisions and never reset: the first such window closes on the timer, every later one in the same screen visit binds already-true and stays open with navigation locked. `KingdomDecisionsVM_RefreshWith_AutoResolved_Patch` postfixes `RefreshWith`, matches `CurrentDecision._decision` to the ballot this call built (so a seam-A refusal or an older item is a no-op), and when `IsActive && IsKingsDecisionOver` already hold runs vanilla's own `ExecuteDone` through the cached `MethodInfo`. That is safe precisely where seam B's is not: `ReadyToAiChoose()` assigns `_chosenOutcome` before it fires the event. `ExecuteDone` hides the popup, shows the outcome inquiry whose OK runs `OnDecisionOver`, clears the concluded listener and nulls the selection; nothing is re-implemented (lessons/harmony-il.md, "Substituting for a vanilla method inherits every one of its responsibilities"). `ExecuteDone` is deliberately kept OUT of the binding's `IsReady` so losing it on an engine bump disables seam D alone, not the three #547 seams. Both `RefreshWith` call sites on v1.5.3 are covered: `HandleDecision`'s inquiry callback (:222) and `KingdomManagementVM.ForceDecideDecision` (:703). A hit logs at warning level, because TAOM's own route into this state is closed at the source: the Player Switcher now promotes a taken-over non-leader to clan leader and repairs older saves at session launch ([player-switcher.md](../features/player-switcher.md) "Clan leadership follows the player"). A warning in a player's log therefore names a route nobody has traced yet.

**Seam E (2026-09-15, Codex review 113 F1): the widget timer outlives a seam D close.** The bind pushes `IsKingsDecisionOver` into `KingdomDecisionPopupWidget`, whose change-gated latch arms `_kingDecisionDoneTime` (:24-30, :64-66), and only its own `ExecuteFinalDone` (:50-54) resets it; hidden widgets still receive late updates. Seam D closes before the five seconds elapse, so the widget's `FinalDone` (bound to `ExecuteDone`, `KingdomDecision.xml:16`) reaches whatever item is bound when it fires: the same closed item, where the duplicate inquiry is rejected by `GauntletQueryManager.CreateQuery` (`HasSameContentWith` is true because the outcome lambda captures only `this`, so the two delegates compare equal; the `Debug.FailedAssert` is log-only in the shipping client), or the NEXT decision's live item if the player opened one inside the window, where `GetChosenOutcomeText()` dereferences a null `_chosenOutcome` inside `OnLateUpdate`. `DecisionItemBaseVM_ExecuteDone_Patch` prefixes `ExecuteDone` and runs vanilla only when `IsActive && IsKingsDecisionOver`. Both hold on every legitimate call: the timer arms only on the true edge of `IsKingsDecisionOver`, `IsActive` is cleared by nothing but `ExecuteDone`, and seam D checks the same pair before invoking. `ExecuteDone` has exactly one caller on v1.5.3 (the widget event), so the guard changes nothing else. `HarmonyPatchBindingTests` resolves the string-named protected target; `Patch80KingdomVoteDeadlockBindingTests` pins the target name and the two state reads.

## Patch81_MarriageAlignment

**Target:** `RomanceCampaignBehavior.CheckNpcMarriages(Clan)` (private instance, Transpiler)

**Feature:** MarriageAlignment, `Main/Features/MarriageAlignment/Hooks/Patch81_MarriageClanDraw.cs`. **Status:** ACTIVE (#542).

Narrows the AI's random partner-clan draw to alignment-compatible clans. **This patch does NOT block anything.** The Free/Evil marriage block lives in `TaomMarriageModel.IsCoupleSuitableForMarriage`, which is the chokepoint every path funnels through. This patch only stops a blocked Free lord from wasting the day's draw.

**Why the block alone is not enough.** Vanilla picks with `Clan.All[MBRandom.RandomInt(Clan.All.Count)]`: one uniform draw over EVERY clan per lord per day, then `continue` if unsuitable. The clan pool is 53% Evil, 25% Free, 16% Neutral, so with the block and no steering a Free lord's draw lands on a usable clan only ~41% of the time. Gondor, Rohan and Dale would marry at roughly 40% of their current rate and run short of heirs. A correct block with no compensation is a slow demographic bug, not a neutral trade-off.

**The splice.** Both `Clan.All` reads (the indexer receiver and the `.Count` argument) are mutated in place to `Ldarg_1` with a `call CandidateClansFor(Clan)` inserted after each. Vanilla's `.Count` and indexer then operate on the filtered pool with no further IL change, and every vanilla filter after the draw (war, clan relation, romance state, the model) still runs. Mutating the existing instruction rather than replacing it preserves any labels attached to it. `Clan.All` appears on exactly ONE source line in the whole class, so the anchor is unambiguous; `MarriageAlignmentBindingTests` pins both that count and the `Ldarg_1` assumption (instance method, first parameter `Clan`) against the installed engine.

**Self-bails** if it does not find exactly two `Clan.All` reads: logs through `IModLogger` and returns the instructions untouched. Cross-alignment marriages stay blocked in that case; only the steering is lost. `CandidateClansFor` additionally returns `Clan.All` unchanged on four degenerate paths (null clan, toggles off, Neutral considering culture, any exception) and on an empty filtered pool. An empty pool matters because `MBRandom.RandomInt(0)` returns 0 and the indexer would then throw. There is deliberately no `Campaign.Current` guard: `Clan.All` IS `Campaign.Current.Clans` and vanilla reads it unconditionally at the same point, so a successful read is itself the proof.

**Pool cache** lives in `MarriageClanPoolCache`, keyed on the considering clan's culture and stamped by `MarriageClanPoolStamp` on `Campaign.UniqueGameId` plus clan count plus campaign day. The id is a STRING, not the `Campaign` object, so a finished campaign's object graph is not pinned alive by a static field (`plans/001-cross-campaign-singleton-resets.md`). Clan culture is only ever assigned at clan CREATION, which moves the count, so the count is what covers culture churn; this is NOT the CultureConversion feature, which converts settlement cultures. The stamp is a pure class with its own tests because it is the only real decision here. The helper runs twice per lord per day, so the `IMarriageAlignmentService` resolve is cached too.

Applied from `SubModule.cs` in `OnSubModuleLoad` alongside `Patch59_CaravanTrade`, the nearest precedent (also a private `CampaignBehavior` method). Once per process, which is what a transpiler wants. See `docs/features/marriage-alignment.md`.

## Patch82_MapEventObserverInvariant

**Target:** `MapEvent.SimulateBattleSetup(FlattenedTroopRoster[])` (public instance, Prefix)

**Feature:** MapEventGuard, `Main/Features/MapEventGuard/Hooks/Patch82_MapEventObserverInvariant.cs`. **Status:** ACTIVE (#551).

Restores the engine's own pairing of `MapEvent.BattleObserver` with `MapEvent.TroopUpgradeTracker` before the simulation dereferences the tracker. This is a backstop, not the fix: the path that reached it was TAOM's enlistment battle-end gate, corrected in the same change.

**The invariant, and why the engine never re-checks it** (verified against installed v1.4.8). `MapEventSide.AllocateTroops` :552 and `AllocateTroop` :590 both call `_mapEvent.TroopUpgradeTracker.AddTrackedTroop(...)` with no null check, gated only on `BattleObserver != null`; `ApplySimulatedHitRewardToSelectedTroop` :1050 and :1056 do the same behind an early `if (BattleObserver == null) return;` at :1040. Four unguarded dereferences across three methods, leaning on one pairing.

**How the pairing breaks.** `MapEvent.RemoveInvolvedPartyInternal` :855-858 nulls the tracker outright whenever the removed party is `PartyBase.MainParty`, and only a rejoin (`AddInvolvedPartyInternal` :636) or a save reload (`OnAfterLoad` :530) rebuilds it. The observer has exactly one writer in the whole game, the `BattleSimulation` constructor, which assigns it and only then indexes `SelectedTroops[(int)_mapEvent.PlayerSide]`. `BattleSideEnum.None` is `-1`, so a main party with no `MapEventSide` makes that line throw `IndexOutOfRangeException` with the observer already attached, and its one clearer (`PlayerEncounter.LeaveBattle` :1990) never runs on that path.

**Removing the main party is also what puts the event back in reach of the tick.** `MapEventManager.Tick` :59 skips only `MobileParty.MainParty.MapEvent`, so one detach supplies both halves: the null tracker and the tick that dereferences it. That is why crash bundle 31942985 shows a pure-vanilla stack with no TAOM frame anywhere on it.

**The repair clears the observer rather than rebuilding the tracker.** The observer is a `BattleSimulation` whose constructor threw, so `PlayerEncounter.Current.BattleSimulation` was never assigned and nothing else holds it: there is no scoreboard left to feed. Rebuilding the tracker would invent state for a battle the player is provably not in, since the tracker is null precisely because the main party was removed. The prefix tests the tracker first (non-null for every ordinary AI battle, so it returns immediately in the common case), reads and writes `BattleObserver` through reflection cached in `Initialize`, and no throttle is needed because the repair makes its own condition false.

**Fail-quiet by construction.** `IsReady` is false when `BattleObserver` does not resolve, and the prefix then does nothing at all, so an engine rename degrades to "the guard is not installed" rather than throwing inside every simulated battle in the world. The body is wrapped anyway, because this runs ahead of vanilla simulation for every live map event.

Applied from `SubModule.cs` in the standard `OnGameInitializationFinished` batch beside `Patch65`: the target runs off each map event's simulation timer under `MapEventManager.Tick`, which `Campaign.Tick` drives, so no map event can tick before the campaign exists. `Patch82MapEventObserverInvariantBindingTests` pins the target, both accessors of the internal `BattleObserver`, the public reference-typed `TroopUpgradeTracker`, the continued existence of `MapEventSide.AllocateTroops`, and all three registrations. See `docs/features/enlistment.md` for the chain that produced it.

## Patch83_StaleCharacterRepair

**Target:** `MBObjectManager.PreAfterLoad()` (public instance, parameterless, Postfix)

**Feature:** StaleCharacterRepair, `Main/Features/StaleCharacterRepair/Hooks/Patch83_StaleCharacterRepair.cs`. **Status:** ACTIVE (crash bundle 065939b6, 2026-09-05).

Makes a character the save restored under an id that current ModuleData no longer defines INERT, by filling every field on it that the engine dereferences unguarded. Without it, loading such a save is a hard `NullReferenceException` inside `Campaign.OnGameLoaded` that the player sees as a failed load.

**Why the fields are null.** A save-restored object is built by `FormatterServices.GetUninitializedObject` (no constructor, no field initializer) and self-registers via `MBObjectBase`'s `[LoadInitializationCallback]`; ModuleData XML then upgrades those registered instances IN PLACE through `MBObjectManager.CreateObjectFromXmlNode`'s `GetPresumedObject`. A character whose id was removed is never reached by that second pass. `CharacterObject` persists exactly two fields (`_heroObject` :20, `_originCharacter` :23) and `Init()` :402-414 restores four more, so everything else stays at default.

**Four fields, not one, and this is the part that matters for triage.** The reported crash was `BasicCharacterObject.GetSkillValue:292` (`DefaultCharacterSkills.Skills.GetPropertyValue`), but that is merely the first null the load path reaches. `UpgradeTargets` is an auto-property INITIALIZER (`CharacterObject.cs:314`) and so is also null, and `PartyCharacterVM.cs:1113` reads `.Length` on it: a stale troop in any roster crashes the party screen. `BodyPropertyRange` is null and `BasicCharacterObject.cs:221` reads `.HairTags` on agent spawn. `_basicName` is null and `ToString()` calls `Name.ToString()`. Repairing only the skills would move the crash somewhere with a stack naming nothing about save staleness. `_culture` and `Level` are deliberately NOT invented.

**How a leaderless party reaches the skills read.** `SkillHelper.GetEffectivePartyLeaderForSkill:78-94` returns `party.MemberRoster.GetCharacterAtIndex(0)` when a party has no leader hero, so garrisons and militia hand a plain TROOP to models expecting a leader. `DefaultPartyMoraleModel.GetMoraleEffectsFromSkill:206-213` does null-check the character, which is why the crash is a null field inside it rather than a null character, and why the report names the inlined `CharacterObject.GetSkillValue` frame.

**The seam is forced by ordering, and this cannot be a campaign behavior.** `Campaign.OnGameLoaded` :679-695 runs `base.ObjectManager.PreAfterLoad()` at :683, `AfterLoad()` at :687, the crashing `CampaignObjectManager.AfterLoad()` at :688, and dispatches `OnGameEarlyLoaded` / `OnGameLoaded` only at :691-692. Both load events are after the crash. `PreAfterLoad` and `AfterLoad` each have exactly one call site in the whole engine; `PreAfterLoad` is strictly earlier and also covers `CampaignObjectManager`/`IssueManager`/`QuestManager.PreAfterLoad` at :684-686. Fires once per SAVED-game load only: `OnGameLoaded` is reached from `DoLoadingForGameType` only inside the `GameLoadingType.SavedCampaign` branch (:1663-1670).

Applied from `SubModule.cs` in `OnSubModuleLoad` beside `Patch58_SkipCampaignIntro`, and **guarded** (`try`/`catch` + `LogError`, the Patch61/Patch62 shape) unlike Patch58: this category binds an engine method by name AND the adapter reflects four engine members, so an unguarded rename would throw out of `OnSubModuleLoad` and take the rest of module init with it.

**Repair, not a read guard.** The read sites are per-agent per-hit combat paths and per-frame UI paths. Idempotent and broken-only: each field is filled only when null, and `TryMakeInert` re-checks before writing so a character another mod repaired in between is left alone.

**Every binding here fails quietly**, which is what the tests are for. `Patch83StaleCharacterRepairBindingTests` pins the target and its arity, all four reflected members and their types, the `MBCharacterSkills`/`MBBodyProperty` construction, `RegisterPresumedObject`, the category string and `ResetForUnload`. See `docs/features/stale-character-repair.md`.

## Patch84_SiegeAftermathMenuGuard

**Target:** `SiegeAftermathCampaignBehavior.menu_settlement_taken_player_participant_on_init(MenuCallbackArgs)` and `menu_settlement_taken_player_army_member_on_init(MenuCallbackArgs)` (both private instance, Prefix)

**Feature:** MapEventGuard, `Main/Features/MapEventGuard/Hooks/Patch84_SiegeAftermathMenuGuard.cs`. **Status:** ACTIVE (#557, crash bundle d7d9f7d3, 2026-09-07).

Two classes, `Patch84_ParticipantMenuGuard` and `Patch84_ArmyMemberMenuGuard`, sharing one category. If a stack ends in either menu init, this is the owner.

**The crash.** `menu_settlement_taken_player_participant_on_init` (installed v1.4.8) opens with `Settlement currentSettlement = _besiegerParty.CurrentSettlement;` and then reads `currentSettlement.GetName()`. Neither is null-guarded, even though the next line guards the character chain as `LordPartyComponent?.Owner?.CharacterObject ?? CharacterObject.PlayerCharacter`. The army-member sibling is worse: its FIRST unguarded dereference is `_besiegerParty.Army` at :325, one line ahead of its own `_besiegerParty.CurrentSettlement` at :326, and `_besiegerParty.CurrentSettlement.Culture` again at :349. Three sites, all unguarded. Gating on the party being present covers the whole method rather than any one line.

**Why `_besiegerParty` can be null.** It is a SAVED field assigned in exactly one place: inside `OnMapEventEnded`'s `if (mapEvent.GetMapEventSide(Attacker).IsMainPartyAmongParties())` block. When the main party is not among the ending event's parties, that whole block is skipped, so `_besiegerParty` keeps whatever the save held and `_wasPlayerArmyMember` is never set. `menu_settlement_taken_on_init` routes on that unset flag into the participant menu, which dereferences the null on its first line. On a campaign with no earlier player-participating siege, the saved value is null.

**How a player reaches it.** An enlisted soldier on the winning attacker side of a siege who is not a member of the capturing army. Two independent readings agree the main party had already left the event: vanilla must have skipped the block, since otherwise `_wasPlayerArmyMember` would be true via `MainParty.Army.LeaderParty != MainParty` (TAOM's battle army is always led by the commander, never the main party, per `ArmyMembershipAdapter.CreateArmyLedBy`); and TAOM's `EnlistmentBattleBehavior.OnMapEventEnded` reached `OnCommanderBattleEnded` through the commander disjunct with `IsMainPartyInMapEvent` false. `MapEventSide.Parties` IS `_battleParties`, the same collection `MapEvent.InvolvedParties` tracks, so the two views cannot disagree.

**IT IS TAOM's OWN DETACH, and the listener order is why.** The first write-up of this patch claimed the reverse and said ordering ruled the detach out; that was wrong, corrected 2026-09-07 during `/deep-review`. `MbEvent<T>.AddNonSerializedListener` (installed v1.4.8, :24-30) HEAD-INSERTS into a singly-linked list and `Invoke` :32-35 walks from the head, so registration is **LIFO: last registered is first invoked**. TAOM adds its campaign behaviours in `SubModule.OnGameStart` after SandBox has registered its own, so TAOM's `EnlistmentBattleBehavior.OnMapEventEnded` runs BEFORE vanilla's on the same dispatch. It calls `ServiceBattleService.OnCommanderBattleEnded` -> `ArmyMembershipAdapter.LeaveArmy` -> `MainParty.AttachedTo = null`, which `MobileParty.SetAttachedToInternal` :1780-1783 answers with `HandleMapEventEndForPartyInternal` + `Party.MapEventSide = null`, whose setter runs `MapEventSide.RemovePartyInternal` and drops the main party from `_battleParties`. Vanilla then reads `IsMainPartyAmongParties()` against a list the player is already out of. Same seam as #551, different victim. **Patch84 stays regardless:** fixing the ordering removes the path TAOM creates, but does not make vanilla's two menus null-safe for any other route into the same state.

**The repair reuses vanilla's own localisation keys.** `Decide` is pure and returns one of three verdicts: `RunVanilla` when BOTH of vanilla's dereferences would succeed, `RepairWithSettlement` when a settlement still resolves (`_besiegerParty?.CurrentSettlement ?? Settlement.CurrentSettlement`), `RepairWithoutSettlement` otherwise. The repair rebuilds the menu body from `{=C2KeQd0a}` and `{=hvQUqRSb}`, copied verbatim from the engine so the shipped translations keep resolving, and falls back to `TextObject.GetEmpty()`, which is how vanilla itself degrades the army-member menu for combinations it does not handle. **No new player-facing strings, so no `/localize` pass.** The army-member aftermath flavour sentence is deliberately not reproduced: it branches on `_playerEncounterAftermath` and `_wasPlayerArmyMember`, which were never assigned on this path either.

**Guarding only the party would move the crash one line down**, onto `currentSettlement.GetName()`. Both terms are in the verdict for that reason, and a test pins the case.

**Fail-quiet by construction.** `IsReady` is false when `_besiegerParty` does not resolve, and both prefixes then defer to vanilla untouched rather than replacing every siege aftermath menu in the game with a fallback. A prefix that throws logs at ERROR before deferring, because deferring here means letting the original crash happen.

Applied from `SubModule.cs` in the standard `OnGameInitializationFinished` batch beside `Patch82`: both targets are game menu inits, which cannot open before the campaign exists. `Patch84SiegeAftermathMenuGuardTests` holds 9 tests, 5 of them direct on the pure `Decide` and the rest pinning the behaviour type, the `_besiegerParty` field and its type, both targets with arity and overload counts, and all three registrations for BOTH classes. See `docs/features/map-event-guard.md`.

## Patch85_EnlistedDetachDeferral

**Target:** `PlayerEncounter.FinalizeBattle()` (public instance, parameterless, Postfix)

**Feature:** Enlistment, `Main/Features/Enlistment/Hooks/Patch85_EnlistedDetachDeferral.cs`. **Status:** ACTIVE (#557).

Moves the enlisted battle-end army detach OUT of the `MapEventEnded` dispatch. This is the root-cause fix that Patch84 guards the symptom of; both ship.

**Why the old placement was wrong.** Campaign-event listeners are LIFO: `MbEvent<T>.AddNonSerializedListener` (installed v1.4.8, :24-30) head-inserts into a singly-linked list and `Invoke` :32-35 walks from the head. TAOM registers its behaviours in `SubModule.OnGameStart` after SandBox has registered its own, so `EnlistmentBattleBehavior.OnMapEventEnded` runs BEFORE vanilla's `SiegeAftermathCampaignBehavior.OnMapEventEnded`. `ServiceBattleService.OnCommanderBattleEnded` used to call `ArmyMembershipAdapter.LeaveArmy` there, which sets `MainParty.AttachedTo = null`, which `MobileParty.SetAttachedToInternal` :1780-1783 answers with `HandleMapEventEndForPartyInternal` + `Party.MapEventSide = null`, dropping the main party out of `MapEventSide._battleParties`. Vanilla then read `IsMainPartyAmongParties()` against a list the player was already out of, skipped its assignment block, and left `_besiegerParty` null for its own aftermath menu to dereference. Same seam as #551.

**Why THIS target.** `PlayerEncounter.Finish` runs `FinalizeBattle()` :1071 (inside which `MapEvent.FinalizeEvent` dispatches `MapEventEnded` to every listener) and `FinishEncounterInternal()` :1072 (which grants the post-defeat escape, `TeleportPartyToOutSideOfEncounterRadius` + `SetDoNotAttackMainParty(2)`, ONLY when `MainParty.AttachedTo == null`) as CONSECUTIVE statements. A postfix on the first lands after every vanilla listener has read the party list and before the escape is decided. That window is one statement wide and is the only place both constraints hold at once. The later `if (InsideSettlement && AttachedTo == null ...) LeaveSettlement()` at :1082 also still sees the detach.

**Stateless by design.** There is no "detach owed" flag to arm, persist, reset per session, or leak across a save. `IServiceBattleService.FlushArmyLeaveAfterBattle` re-derives every term from live state, so a missed arm, a save taken mid-battle, or a co-op host handoff cannot leave a stale intention. That also makes it safe on the legacy save-upgrade `FinalizeBattle` call at :609.

**The #551 guard travels with it.** `FlushArmyLeaveAfterBattle` no-ops when `IEncounterAdapter.IsMainPartyInMapEvent` is true, because `FinalizeBattle` can run while the player is inside another live map event and detaching there would tear him out of it. In that case `EnlistmentReconciler`'s `noBattleAnywhere && IsInArmy` sweep detaches him once it resolves, which is the intended path rather than a fallback.

**Fail-open with a standing backstop.** The postfix swallows its own exceptions: a missed detach degrades to "detached a tick late" because the reconciler sweep already exists for battles that resolve without the normal edge. Co-op gated on `ICoopSessionProvider.IsAuthority`, matching the gate on the path it defers from. Resolvers (`Func<T>`) rather than instances, so the patch batch does not pull enlistment construction forward.

Applied from `SubModule.cs` in the standard `OnGameInitializationFinished` batch beside `Patch84`: the target is campaign-runtime and cannot run before a campaign exists. `Patch85EnlistedDetachDeferralBindingTests` pins the target with arity and overload count, the continued existence of `FinishEncounterInternal`, the LIFO shape of `MbEvent`'s listener list, and all three registrations; `ServiceBattleServiceTests` covers the behaviour. See `docs/features/enlistment.md` and `docs/features/map-event-guard.md`.

## Patch86_HideoutBossFight

**Target:** `Helpers.MapEventHelper.GetPriorityListForHideoutMission(List<MobileParty>, out int)` (public static, Prefix, skip original) and `SandBox.Missions.MissionLogics.Hideout.HideoutAmbushMissionController.SpawnRemainingTroopsForBossFight(List<MatrixFrame>, int)` (private instance, SandBox.dll, Prefix, original still runs; `ref spawnCount` plus three injected fields)

**Feature:** BanditManagement, `Main/Features/BanditManagement/Hooks/Patch86_HideoutBossFight.cs` (category, logger, service resolver), `Patch86_HideoutAssaultBossFight.cs`, `Patch86_HideoutAmbushBossFight.cs`. **Status:** ACTIVE (#564).

Two classes, one category (the Patch84 shape). The hideout boss fight is exactly 1 boss + N bodyguards, N being the MCM `Hideout Boss Bodyguards` (0-10, default 4), on both the daytime assault and the night sneak-in, independent of `Enable Bandit Scaling`.

**Why the template was not enough.** The `*_boss_party_template` sizes the boss PARTY parked in the hideout, not the fight. Assault route: `MapEventHelper.GetPriorityListForHideoutMission` (:147-172, sole caller `SandBoxMissions.OpenHideoutBattleMission` :1142) puts `min(floor(0.8 × total), FirstFightMax)` troops in phase 1 and everything else, highest level first, in the boss phase; `HideoutMissionController.SpawnBossAndBodyguards` (:789) then spawns every troop phase 1 did not take, because `PartyGroupTroopSupplier.NumTroopsNotSupplied` is the initial count minus what was allocated. Before it, `HideoutCampaignBehavior.ArrangeHideoutTroopCountsForMission` (:606-656) trims the hideout to `FirstFightMax + BossFightMax` but line 615 excludes boss parties from the trim, so a 40-97 man boss party (the old template plus Patch39) overflowed straight into the boss phase, and `TaomBanditDensityModel` scaled `BossFightMax` up to 2.5x on top. Sneak-in route: `HideoutAmbushMissionController.InitializeTroops` (:751-778) supplies every troop into the private `_allEnemyTroops`, sentries pop from it (`pop / 8`, :595-598), and `SpawnRemainingTroopsForBossFight` (:478-555) pads the list UP to `Clamp(pop / 2, 4, 20)` (:507-511) then spawns every element (:528-542): the clamp is a floor, so the night fight was boss + everyone who was not a sentry.

**Assault prefix (skip original).** Replicates vanilla's roster build (healthy total :149, per-party flatten :152-156, wounded removed first :157, heroes and `Culture.BanditBoss` troops all held back and counted after that pass :158, highest-level regulars next :163, phase-1 roster returned :171) and replaces the :150 formula with `firstPhaseTroopCount = healthy - bossPhase`, where `IHideoutBossFightService.PlanAssault` clamps `bossPhase = heroesAndBosses + N` to `[1, healthy - 1]` (`HideoutMissionController.InitializeMission` :590 asserts phase 2 < total; `SelectBossAgent` returns `val ?? val2` and the caller derefs it, so an empty boss phase is an NRE). Vanilla's four `Debug.Print`s become one TAOM info line. Exact on every hideout: on an existing save a fat boss party lands in phase 1 once, never in the boss fight. **Fail-open is safe at this call site**: nothing above the decision mutates campaign state (the roster is a fresh copy, the parties are only read), so the catch hands the call back to vanilla's own split and logs.

**Ambush prefix (original still runs).** Injects `____allEnemyTroops`, `____overriddenHideoutBossAgentOrigin` and `____allEnemyTroopTypesCache` (four underscores: Harmony's three plus the field's own; `HarmonyFieldInjectionNamingTests` checks the count, `ReflectionSiteBindingTests` the members, `Patch86HideoutBossFightBindingTests` the types), trims the list to the N highest-level troops (roster order on ties, parity with the assault split) and sets `spawnCount = N`; vanilla then pads from the type cache when fewer remain (only requested when that cache is non-empty, because `GetNewRandomEnemyTroop` :473 derefs `GetRandomElement` on it) and spawns the boss from his own origin (:513-527), which is never in the list. Selection first, mutation last (`Clear` + `AddRange`), so a throw leaves vanilla's list intact. `spawnFrames` is not declared, so no `MatrixFrame` parameter and no deferred-category question.

**The model half.** `TaomBanditDensityModel.NumberOfMaximumTroopCountForBossFightInHideout` returns `1 + N` unconditionally (its only consumer is the :609 trim), so the hideout is trimmed to `FirstFightMax + (1 + N)` and phase 1 keeps its pre-#564 size. `Boss Fight Curve` now scales the first fight only. The eight boss templates are one `1/1` boss plus soldier stacks summing to min 3 / max 4 (`HideoutBossPartyTemplateTests`), and `tools/rebalance_template_power.py` no longer recognises them.

**No master toggle by design.** N is a design choice, not scaling, so `Enable Bandit Scaling` off leaves the boss fight at boss + N; a player cannot restore vanilla's 6..11. Both prefixes return `bool` and are `ReviewedSafe` in `CoopVetoClassificationTests`: the assault target is a pure roster-ordering function for the local mission (the campaign-side trim flows through the GameModel value, N is fingerprinted), the ambush target mutates `MissionLogic` instance state only. Leftover unspawned troops never block a clear: `PlayerEncounter.DoEnd` (:1814-1833) walks every surviving party out on `AttackerVictory`, the same path vanilla's duel branch relies on.

Applied from `SubModule.cs` in the standard `OnGameInitializationFinished` batch beside `Patch40`: both targets run inside a campaign mission. `Initialize` takes a resolver (`Func<IHideoutBossFightService>`), `ResetForUnload` clears it. `Patch86HideoutBossFightBindingTests` pins both targets with parameter NAMES (Harmony binds by name) and the `out` flag, the injected field types, every engine member the assault body touches, the IL fact that the campaign trim still reads the boss-phase cap, that both prefixes still call the service, and the three-place registration; `HideoutBossFightServiceTests` (33) covers the behaviour. See `docs/features/bandit-management.md` "Hideout boss fight: boss + N".

## Patch87_ReturnToArmy

**Target:** `PlayerTownVisitCampaignBehavior.game_menu_return_to_army_on_consequence(MenuCallbackArgs)` (private instance, Prefix, skips the original on exactly one row)

**Feature:** ReturnToArmy, `Main/Features/ReturnToArmy/Hooks/Patch87_ReturnToArmy.cs` (prefix, logger, `ResetForUnload`) and `ReturnToArmyRules.cs` (the pure decision). **Status:** ACTIVE (#566).

**The trap.** Vanilla's consequence (installed v1.4.8, :368-376) switches to `army_wait_at_settlement` and calls `PlayerEncounter.LeaveSettlement()` + `Finish()` only for a village; one method serves `town_return_to_army` (:63), `castle_return_to_army` (:142) and `village_return_to_army` (:158). `game_menu_town_town_leave_on_condition` (:994-1006) hides "Leave" for every non-leader army member, so in a town or castle that option is the member's only exit. It is fine while the player is MERGED into the army (`AttachedTo != null`): the army rests here and `LeaveSettlementAction.ApplyForParty(leader)` (:13-26) finishes the player's encounter when the leader moves. For a member who is in the army but NOT attached (`Army != null`, `AttachedTo == null`) it is a dead end: the wait menu's tick (`PlayerArmyWaitBehavior` :181-196) re-routes through `DefaultEncounterGameMenuModel.GetGenericStateMenu()`, which answers `town_wait_menus` in a foreign-faction fortification (:295-298, "You are waiting in X", whose only button returns to the town menu) and `army_wait_at_settlement` in a same-faction one (:291-294, a wait nothing ends, since `Army.Tick` :379-398 merges only a party marching at the leader). A Player Switcher takeover inherits the lord's `Army` untouched and a lord marching to join an army is exactly this state; vanilla reaches it through the "we will wait for other parties around X, then follow us" line (`LordConversationsCampaignBehavior` :3510-3514).

**The prefix** reads `MainParty.Army`, `.AttachedTo` and `.CurrentSettlement.IsVillage`, hands them to `ReturnToArmyRules.Decide`, and runs vanilla for every row but one: not in an army or its leader (unreachable, the option's condition hides it), merged into the army (wait with it), village (vanilla already leaves). For an unattached member in a town or castle it replicates vanilla's own `game_menu_settlement_leave_on_consequence` (:1054-1068) in order (gate position, `LeaveSettlement`, `Finish`, `SetMoveModeHold`, `SignalAutoSave`), drops the `AttachedParties` loop consciously (parties attach to the leader, who never sees this option), writes nothing to `Army`, and returns false. Auto-marching to the leader (`MobileParty.SetMoveEngageParty`) was declined by the reporting user; restoring "Leave" for members would change the attached case too.

**Error paths differ on purpose.** A throw before the first mutation defers to vanilla, whose wait menu is a dead end but not a crash, and logs an error. A throw after it does NOT: `LeaveSettlement` has already nulled `CurrentSettlement`, which vanilla's :371 dereferences unguarded, so the prefix skips vanilla and logs `[ReturnToArmy] the leave sequence threw after mutating`. `ReviewedSafe` in `CoopVetoClassificationTests`: what is skipped is a menu switch, what runs instead is the player's own vanilla Leave on the local main party, and the condition reads replicated engine state only.

Applied from `SubModule.cs` in the standard `OnGameInitializationFinished` batch beside `Patch84`: the target is a menu consequence, which cannot run before the campaign exists. `Patch87ReturnToArmyTests` (12) covers the decision on all sixteen inputs and pins the target (private instance, one `MenuCallbackArgs`, one overload), its condition, call presence in the vanilla body, every engine member the leave sequence touches, the three-place registration and, by IL scan, that the prefix still calls `Decide` and the full leave sequence. The IL tests pin call presence only: at every engine bump re-read the vanilla body by hand and re-derive the decision table, because a refactor that keeps the calls but changes which branch leaves would pass them and be silently suppressed on the owned row (Codex review 98, no P1/P2). See `docs/features/return-to-army.md`.

## Patch88_LordPartyTemplate

**Target:** `Clan.DefaultPartyTemplate` getter (public instance, Postfix, swaps `__result`); `HeroSpawnCampaignBehavior.SpawnLordParty(Hero hero, bool isNewGame)` (private instance, Prefix + Finalizer, scope only); `LordPartyComponent.InitializationArgs.InitializeLordPartyProperties(MobileParty mobileParty, Hero owner)` (public instance on the nested args type, Prefix + Finalizer, scope only)

**Feature:** LordPartyTemplates, `Main/Features/LordPartyTemplates/Hooks/Patch88_LordPartyTemplate.cs` (the postfix, the thread-static ambient owner, `Initialize`, `ResetForUnload`), `Patch88_SpawnLordPartyScope.cs`, `Patch88_InitializeLordPartyPropertiesScope.cs`, and `LordPartyTemplateResolution.cs` (the pure decision). **Status:** ACTIVE (#580).

**Why a getter.** The engine has no per-hero party template. Both reads that draw a lord's roster go through the clan: `InitializeLordPartyProperties` (installed v1.4.8, `LordPartyComponent.cs:38`) takes `owner.Clan.DefaultPartyTemplate` for the initial roster on every creation path, and `SpawnLordParty` (:265) reads `Owner.Clan.DefaultPartyTemplate` again to weight the new-game top-up. `Clan.DefaultPartyTemplate` (`Clan.cs:112-122`) is the clan binding else the culture default. A postfix on that one getter, live only while a lord spawn is in flight, covers both reads without a transpiler and without re-deriving the fill formula. Faramir shares his clan with Denethor and Boromir and Sauron shares his with Herumarth and Naktharil, so rebinding a clan was never an option.

**The scopes.** Each scope prefix saves the previous ambient owner in Harmony's per-invocation `__state` and sets its hero; each finalizer restores `__state`. Restore, not clear: the two scopes nest (`SpawnLordParty` reaches the component initializer through `MobilePartyHelper.SpawnLordParty`), so a clearing inner finalizer would blank the outer scope before the top-up read. Both finalizers return `__exception` untouched, because Patch65 finalizes `SpawnLordParty` too and swallows `InvalidOperationException` on purpose; every finalizer on a method writes one shared exception slot, so a non-transparent neighbour would decide what Patch65 decided. The initializer scope exists because `CreateLordParty` is also reached from `RebellionsCampaignBehavior`, `CompanionRolesCampaignBehavior` and StoryMode, none of which pass through `SpawnLordParty`.

**The postfix** returns on its first line when no ambient owner is set, which is every read outside a lord spawn (`Clan.HasNavalNavigationCapability`, `ClanVariablesCampaignBehavior`, bandit and caravan-ambush spawns). Otherwise `LordPartyTemplateResolution.Resolve` decides: the clan being read must be the owner's own clan and the owner must be in `lord_party_templates.json`. A hit resolves the template through `MBObjectManager.GetObject<PartyTemplateObject>`; an id that does not resolve leaves vanilla's answer in place and warns once per hero. The rebel branch reads `Culture.RebelsPartyTemplate`, a different getter, and the player's clan takes the position-only path at `LordPartyComponent.cs:32` before any template is read, so neither is touched.

Applied from `SubModule.cs` in the standard `OnGameInitializationFinished` batch beside Patch65, for the same reason: the new-game dispatch (`OnNewGameCreatedPartialFollowUpEvent` to `TrySpawnHeroesAndParties(isNewGame: true)`) runs after `Campaign.OnInitialize`, and a lazier hook would leave the new-game roster on the clan binding. `Patch88LordPartyTemplateTests` (12) pins the getter, both targets with their parameter NAMES (Harmony binds `hero` and `owner` by name), that both vanilla bodies still call the getter, every engine member the postfix body references, the category literal on all three classes, the three `SubModule.cs` lines, call presence in the postfix and both scopes, finalizer transparency and `ResetForUnload`. Not pinned offline: that Harmony hands `__state` from a prefix to a finalizer, which is on the in-game list. See `docs/features/lord-party-templates.md`.

## Patch_MissionTime_SetMovementOrder

**Target:** `Formation.SetMovementOrder(MovementOrder)` (Postfix ×2)

Shared deferred category for `Formation.SetMovementOrder(MovementOrder)` postfixes. Applied once from `OnMissionBehaviorInitialize` (one-shot static guard) because `MovementOrder.cctor` reads `Mission.Current.CurrentTime` — null in `OnSubModuleLoad`/`OnGameInitializationFinished`. Currently houses Patch31_SmartCavalryAI's charge handler (prefix + postfix), its Patch31b sibling on `Formation.SetTargetFormation` (kept in the same category so the pair applies together), and Patch35_CompanionTactics' `CancelStanceOnMove` postfix. **Any future patch with `MovementOrder` in its postfix signature must use this category.**

## Late_ActionSetOverride

**Target:** `ActionSetCode.GenerateActionSetNameWithSuffix` (Prefix)

Race-aware action-set name generation for custom races. The Prefix replaces the vanilla name build: a null `Monster` returns `as_human[_female]<suffix>`; otherwise the result is `as_<monsterId>[_female]<suffix>`, where `monsterId` prefers `Monster.BaseMonster` when present and falls back to the full `StringId` (matching vanilla's preference order) — so TAOM's custom-race monsters resolve to their own `as_<race>*` action sets (the LOTRLOME_Armory `as_<race>_facegen` requirement in `docs/features/character-creation.md` depends on this resolution). Returns `false` (skip original) on success; any exception falls through to vanilla. Applied in the late `OnGameInitializationFinished` batch alongside `Late_Transpiler` (one-shot guarded). Historical note in-file (Phase 9b #151): Harmony 2 attribute patches require `public static class` — this was TAOM's one non-static outlier, since fixed. Owning feature: HeroRace — see `docs/features/hero-race.md`.

## Late_Transpiler

**Target:** `BodyGeneratorView.RefreshCharacterEntityAux` (Transpiler)

CharacterSelection face-generator action-set injection. The transpiler finds the `Newobj` for `AgentVisualsData`'s parameterless ctor and inserts, right after it, a call to the patch's own `GetActionSet(BodyGeneratorView)` + `AgentVisualsData.ActionSet(...)` — so the body-generator preview is built with a race-appropriate `_facegen` action set (`BodyGen.Race` → `FaceGen.GetBaseMonsterFromRace`, null → human fallback, then `MBGlobals.GetActionSetWithSuffix(monster, isFemale, "_facegen")`) instead of the human default. Degrades gracefully (Phase 9b #160): if any of the three lookups (ctor / `ActionSet` method / `Newobj` IL match) fails, it logs the specific gap and returns the original instructions unchanged — previously such a mismatch threw out of `PatchCategory` during `OnGameInitializationFinished` and bricked startup. Applied in the late `OnGameInitializationFinished` batch (one-shot guarded). See `docs/features/character-selection.md`.

## Patch89_MapLoadDiagnostics

**Target:** `Campaign.RealTick` (Postfix heartbeat, `TargetMethod`); `_Lifecycle`: `GameStateManager.PushState` / `PopState` / `CleanAndPushState` / `CleanStates`, `GameState.OnInitialize` (base binding, deliberate: `MapState` does not override it, so the label reports the real instance type), `MapState.OnActivate`, `LoadingWindow.EnableGlobalLoadingWindow` / `DisableGlobalLoadingWindow`; `_MapScreen`: `MapScreen.OnInitialize` / `OnFrameTick`; `_SceneReady`: `SceneView.CheckSceneReadyToRender` / `ReadyToRender`

Map-load diagnostics: a `[MapLoad]` heartbeat every 5 seconds on the campaign map (frames, fps, tick ms, a per-type party census on emit frames only, campaign time, loading-window state and the WHOLE game-state stack) plus a sequence-numbered lifecycle trace: every state push, pop, clean and initialize with the resulting stack, the map state and map screen seams bracketed ENTER/EXIT, the first completed map frame, and every raise of the global loading window, and every lower that actually took it down (a Prefix captures `IsLoadingWindowActive`, because the engine calls the lower every frame on several screens), with its managed caller chain. Written on the archived v1.5.0 line for the map-load stall and it answered the question on its first run: `MapScreen.HandleIfBlockerStatesDisabled` lowers the window only once `SceneView.ReadyToRender() && CheckSceneReadyToRender()` has held while its ready-frame counter reaches 3 (the fourth consecutive ready frame lowers it), and a terrain baked by pre-1.5 tools never satisfied it (not a TAOM defect; the v1.5.2 Modding Kit re-bakes it). Four categories applied separately from `OnSubModuleLoad`, each in its own try/catch, because Harmony aborts a category at its first failing class and a drifted binding must not take the working heartbeat with it. `MapLoadDiagnosticsBehavior` resets the singleton heartbeat and the static trace clock on every session launch, so a second campaign in one process starts its own timeline. Numbered 89 on the v1.5.x line because Patch66 is `Patch66_Enlistment` on trunk. `Main/Features/MapLoadDiagnostics/`. See `docs/features/map-load-diagnostics.md`.

## Patch90_PreloadBodyGuard

**Target:** `PreloadHelper.WaitForMeshesToBeLoaded` (Prefix, returns `true`; receives `_uniqueDynamicPhysicsShapeName` by `____` injection)

Runtime guard against the infinite mission load on a missing collision body (#601; mechanism #352, #599). Vanilla's wait is a do/while with no exit that counts, every pass, each registered body name that `PhysicsShape.GetFromResource(name, true)` returns null for, and null means "not loaded yet" and "no such body" alike. The prefix repairs the precondition instead of replacing the method: it polls only the body names with the same 1 ms sleep for a bounded 5 s budget (a healthy load resolves every name on the first pass and pays nothing measurable), removes what is still null from the set, logs each drop at ERROR as `[PreloadGuard]` with the repair command, and lets vanilla's loop run against a set that can now empty. `_uniqueMetaMeshes` is never touched and the prefix never returns `false`. The item whose body was dropped has no collision shape for that mission: a visible, logged defect in place of a frozen game. Six callers, all main thread: `MissionPreloadView` (campaign battles and sieges), `ArenaPreloadView` (tournaments; the player's kit is first in that set), `MissionCustomBattlePreloadView`, `MissionMultiplayerPreloadView`, `NavalShipsPreloadView`, `GauntletEducationScreen.OnFrameTick`. Applied in `OnSubModuleLoad` beside Patch89 (the education screen and custom battle call the wait before any campaign initialises), own try/catch. Decision logic lives in `PreloadBodyGuardService` (pure, 7 tests); `PreloadBodyGuardBindingTests` pins the private field's name and type on the installed engine so a rename un-injects as a red test, not as a silently unguarded load. Feature doc: [preload-body-guard.md](../features/preload-body-guard.md). **ACTIVE** (2026-09-15).

## Patch91_MissionTickStall

**Target:** `Mission.TickAgentsAndTeamsImp(float, bool)` and `MissionState.TickMissionAux(float, float, bool, bool)` (private, bound by name with argument types). Prefix + void finalizer on each; signatures identical on v1.4.8 and v1.5.3.

Battle-freeze probes (#634). A player's battle froze with the heap flat, no exception and no crash report. That is the shape of an asynchronous agent tick that never finishes: the next frame's `[MBCallback] Mission.OnPreTick` spins in `WaitTickCompletion` (`while (!tickCompleted) Thread.Sleep(1)`) before any `OnPreMissionTick`. The agent-tick prefix records the calling thread and a UTC timestamp in `MissionTickStallProbe.AsyncAgentTick`. The frame prefix does the same in `MissionTickStallProbe.MissionFrame`, but only while `CurrentMission.CurrentState == Continuing`: a teardown frame (`CheckMissionEnd` -> `EndMissionInternal` inside `Mission.OnTick`) is not a frozen battle. Each void finalizer clears its probe and runs whenever the method throws, so a tick that throws never reads as in flight (Harmony 2.4.2's `MethodCreator` rethrows the original exception while every finalizer on the method is void, as on the agent tick, which PatchShield excludes; on the shielded `MissionState.TickMissionAux` PatchShield's value-returning finalizer makes it `throw`, and `RethrowStackPreserver` keeps the trace).

`MissionTickStallWatchdog` polls the probes from a 1 s timer. For a tick in flight past 10/20/40 s it suspends that thread and logs its managed stack as a `[MissionStall]` ERROR, one stack per thread (an agent tick inline inside the frame in fast-forward is covered by the frame's sample). The capture is shared with `ExitStallSampler` through `ThreadStackCapture`. It stands down while the mission-exit window is open, so two samplers never suspend one thread. A stack in the agent tick is the stuck work; a stack in the frame shows the main thread, waiting in `WaitTickCompletion` or stuck in a callback or `OnMissionTick`. Composes with Patch37's finalizer on `Mission.Tick`.

MCM toggle "Enable Battle Freeze Sampler", default ON, independent of the master diagnostics toggle. Applied in `OnGameInitializationFinished` beside Patch43, inside the once-per-process guard, own try/catch. Cost per healthy frame: two `DateTime.UtcNow` reads, one `CurrentState` read and six volatile writes. The agent-tick prefix and finalizer also call the Patch97 profiler's `OnAgentTickEnter`/`OnAgentTickExit`, a static null check when the profiler is not installed. **ACTIVE** (2026-09-22).

## Patch92_TrollFormationSpacing

**Target:** `Formation.UnitDiameter` getter (postfix); the three public `Formation.GetUnitPositionWithIndexAccordingToNewOrder` overloads and `Formation.GetUnitSpawnFrameWithIndex` (prefix plus void finalizer, a thread-static scope). `Main/Features/TrollBruteForce/Hooks/Patch92_TrollFormationSpacing.cs`.

Trolls stood inside each other in formation (Mike, 2026-09-25). The engine spaces every foot unit for a 0.76 m human: `UnitDiameter` is `GetDefaultUnitDiameter`, `BipedalRadius` (0.38, `managed_core_parameters.xml`) times two, whatever the Monster. The postfix raises it to the width `TrollFormationSpacingTracker` stored for the formation in `TrollFormationSpacingStore` once trolls are a tenth of it, vanilla's share for horses (`CalculateHasSignificantNumberOfMounted`): the widest troll's measured shoulder width times its `AgentScale` (`TrollBruteForceConfig.ShoulderWidthByMonster`: cave troll 0.75 m, so about 1.43 m at 1.9; hill troll 2.43 m, so about 2.7 m at 1.11; capped at 4 m). A first cut used 0.76 m times body size, which fits the cave troll but gave the hill troll, twice as broad for its height, 1.78 m. `LineFormation` reads `owner.UnitDiameter` for every slot, file count and rank depth; MixedFormations reads it through `IFormationAdapter.UnitDiameter` (`LayoutPositioner.UnitPitch`).

The order preview, the deployment placement and the spawn frames lay a formation out on a team-less copy (`new Formation(null, -1)`), which no store holds, so the first build still placed trolls at human width and their capsules pushed them to touching (every neighbour 1.6 m apart). The scope patch names the real formation in a thread-static (`TrollFormationSpacingStore.LayingOut`) while its layout runs, and the postfix gives a team-less copy (`__instance.Team == null`) that formation's width; `Patch92BindingTests` pins the four scope targets and the public `Formation.Team` field the postfix reads. The store (`Main/Features/TrollBruteForce/TrollFormationSpacingStore.cs`, a `ConcurrentDictionary<object, float>`) is keyed by reference through `ReferenceIdentity` (`Main/Core/Collections/ReferenceIdentity.cs`): `Formation.GetHashCode` is `Team.TeamIndex * 10 + FormationIndex` and threw a NullReferenceException on the preview's copy in the first build.

The tracker refreshes from `TrollBruteForceMissionBehavior.OnMissionTick` twice a second on the main thread. When a formation's width changes it calls `Formation.OnUnitAddedOrRemoved()` and `Arrangement.OnFormationFrameChanged(updateCachedOrderedLocalPositions: true)`. The `true` is what `LineFormation.UpdateLocalPositionErrors` passes (v1.5.3 `LineFormation.cs:2310`); the engine's own frame change in `Formation.SetPositioning` passes the default `false` (`Formation.cs:926`). With `true`, `BatchUnitPositionAvailabilities` recomputes the cached local slot positions from the current unit width and then the global slots from them. With `false` it still rebuilds the global slots on every call (`owner.BatchUnitPositions`, `LineFormation.cs:2205`, sits outside the dirty check), but from the cached local positions, which keep the human width unless something already marked them dirty. A re-issued Move to the same spot recomputes neither, and left every troll on the human-width slots. While `Mission.IsTeleportingAgents` is on (the field deployment), it also replays the tail of vanilla's `Formation.OnMassUnitTransferEnd` (v1.5.3 `Formation.cs:1958-1966`): `ApplyActionOnEachUnit` with `ForceUpdateCachedAndFormationValues`, then `SetHasPendingUnitPositions(false)`. The trolls were placed at human width before their width was known and nothing else moves them during deployment; after it they walk to the wider slots. The getter also runs on the AI thread and the TWParallel workers, so the postfix only reads the concurrent map. It has no try/catch: beyond the store it reads only the public `Formation.Team` field, which cannot throw. PatchShield's shield pass skips all five targets (`PatchShieldPolicy.ExcludedTargetMethods` lists `Formation.get_UnitDiameter`, `GetUnitPositionWithIndexAccordingToNewOrder` and `GetUnitSpawnFrameWithIndex`), so none carries a shield finalizer on its per-unit calls.

Two width reads bypass the patch: the custom-width order preview measures its copy's occupation width through `Formation.GetLastSimulatedFormationsOccupationWidthIfLesserThanActualWidth`, which `OrderController.SimulateNewCustomWidthOrder` calls outside the scope (`OrderController.cs:1287`), and the deployment plan sizes a formation's spawn area through `DefaultDeploymentPlan.GetFormationSpawnWidthAndDepth`, which uses the static `Formation.GetDefaultUnitDiameter` (`DefaultDeploymentPlan.cs:593-607`). Two more reads use that fixed human width and place no troll in single player: `StrategicArea.GetAgentFrame` (`StrategicArea.cs:473-474`; a strategic area takes only ranged agents, `IsAgentEligible` at `:280-282`, and no troll carries a ranged weapon) and multiplayer's `SpawningBehaviorBase` (`SpawningBehaviorBase.cs:209`). Feature doc: [troll-brute-force.md](../features/troll-brute-force.md). Applied in `OnSubModuleLoad` after Patch30. **ACTIVE** (2026-09-25).

## Patch93_CreatureBandits

**Target:** `Mission.SpawnTroop` (prefix, skips the original for a creature); `CommonAIComponent.OnHit` (prefix); `Mission.CanAgentRout` (postfix); `Agent.GetPrimaryWieldedItemIndex`, `Agent.GetOffhandWieldedItemIndex` and `Agent.GetMissileRange` (prefixes). `Main/Features/CreatureBandits/Hooks/Patch93_CreatureBandits.cs`. Mission's private `CreateAgent(Monster, ...)` (transpiler, postfix, finalizer), `Main/Features/CreatureBandits/Hooks/Patch93_CreatureBanditWeaponState.cs`.

Creature Bandits (#692): hostile bandit-only creatures that fight with no rider, first the Mirkwood spider brood. A creature troop is a hidden troop whose Horse slot holds its creature. The spawn prefix turns it into a riderless creature for the enemy side of a field battle: `CreatureBanditSpawner` calls the engine's free-mount `Mission.SpawnMonster`, then sets the troop's origin (so `BattleAgentLogic` counts its death and `IsSideDepleted` ends the battle), the troop as `Character` (so the first kill does not NRE in `TroopUpgradeTracker.CheckUpgradedCount`), the tuned hit points (`CreatureBanditTuning`, MCM "Creature Bandits"; the `Character` setter overwrites health from the troop), the team, clears the flight flags (`RunsAwayWhenHit`, `CanWander`, `CanGetScared`, `CanRear`), applies route A and raises `OnAgentBuild`, which `SpawnMonster` does not. The battle spawn loop ignores the returned agent (v1.5.3 `MissionBattleSideSpawnContext.cs:375-389`), and skipping the original skips `SpawnTroopWithAgentBuildData`'s `Character.IsHero` read and weapon wield. Every other spawn, any failed spawn, and a banner bearer's spawn (`bannerItem` set: `BannerBearerLogic.SpawnBannerBearer` is the one caller that uses the returned agent) fall through to vanilla. No formation: a riderless mount in one crashed twice in June (`rca-spider-troop-2026-06-04.md:28, 61`).

`CommonAIComponent.OnHit` panics any riderless AI mount on a hit of 1 or more (`CommonAIComponent.cs:198-201`); the prefix skips it for a creature bandit (its base is empty). Morale is the second route: `CommonAIComponent` panics an agent whose morale falls below 0.01 when `CanPanic()` agrees (`CommonAIComponent.cs:84-94, 174`), and `CanPanic()` first asks `BattleMoraleModel.CanPanicDueToMorale`, which both TAOM morale models (`TaomBattleMoraleModel`, `TaomCustomBattleMoraleModel`) refuse for a creature, so that route needs no patch. The `CanAgentRout` postfix refuses the rout that would fade a creature out at the map edge, where SandBox never counts a mount (`BattleAgentLogic.cs:147`); `CreatureBanditMissionBehavior` counts one that slips through (only a creature route A left Mountable). The postfix is used instead of the engine's `CanAgentRout_AdditionalCondition` event, a multicast `Func<Agent, bool>` that returns only its last subscriber's answer. The three weapon-state guards answer `None` and `0` for a creature that has no native weapon state: a mount-built agent gets one only when `CanWieldWeapon` is in its creation flags, so they matter only when route A was skipped (the June spider's closed set, `rca-spider-troop-2026-06-04.md:30, 62`; the 2026-09-28 spike logged no guard answer). The guards and the rout postfix run for every agent, check a managed field first, and are on `PatchShieldPolicy.ExcludedTargetMethods`. Every check is `CreatureBanditAgents.Is`: a riderless, non-humanoid agent whose `Character` is a creature troop, which no vanilla agent is. It reads `IsHuman`, never `IsMount`, because of route A below.

**Route A (targeting).** Soldiers never target a riderless creature: native `IsEnemy` (`FUN_1805d52e0`) takes a `Mountable` agent's side from its rider, so a riderless one is nobody's enemy, and the soldier's melee and ranged candidate builders (`FUN_18068b1e0`, `FUN_18068b750`) accept only enemies. Clearing `Mountable` fixes that, but the candidate scorer (`FUN_18068c9d0`) then reads the creature's native weapon state unchecked, and the engine allocates that state only for an agent created with `CanWieldWeapon` (`FUN_1805b89c0`). So the transpiler passes `monster.Flags` on its way into `CreateAgentInternal` through `CreatureWeaponStateScope.OnCreationFlags`, which adds `CanWieldWeapon` only while the spawner has armed the scope around its own `SpawnMonster` call. The postfix strips the flag from the new agent before the build: live, it sends the creature into the unarmed melee lookup, whose actions `as_spider` does not have. The spawner's last wiring step, after the team step, unlists the creature from the loose mounts and clears `Mountable` (`CreatureRouteAUnmount`), but only when the weapon state exists, the flag is not live, the team is set and the Monster's pelvis bone resolves; otherwise the creature stays untargetable and `spawn-wired` logs `routeA=skipped:<reason>`. Evidence and the skeptic reviews: [creature-bandits-roadmap.md](../research/creature-bandits-roadmap.md), "Targeting". `CreatureBanditsWiringTests` binds every prefix and postfix parameter against the installed engine.
