Feature: [creature-bandits.md](../features/creature-bandits.md). Applied by `CreatureBanditsModule` at process load. **ACTIVE**: route A passed its Custom Battle spike (2026-09-28, console spawns); the campaign spawn path has not run in game.

## Patch94_CreatureBroodCampaign

**Target:** `SandBox.View.SandBoxViewHelpers+MobilePartyVisualHelper.GetHumanAgentPartyVisual` (prefix, resolved by name); `EncounterGameMenuBehavior.game_menu_encounter_meeting_on_init` (prefix); `BanditInteractionsCampaignBehavior.GetMemberAndPrisonerRostersFromParties` (private, prefix, resolved by name, #694). `Main/Features/CreatureBandits/Hooks/Patch94_CreatureBroodCampaign.cs`.

The campaign-map side of Creature Bandits (#692). A bandit party's map icon is its visual leader, the troop at roster index 0, drawn as a rider (the helper) on its Horse-slot mount (built separately in `MobilePartyVisual.AddCharacterToPartyIcon`, v1.5.3 lines 1128-1167). For a brood the leader is the pale broodmother, so the prefix returns no rider visual and the icon is the spider alone; the method guards every use of the rider, as it must at sea, and the helper draws a banner only for a leader hero's clan banner (`SandBoxViewHelpers.cs:112-118`), which a brood lacks. The same method carries BannerColorPersistence's transpiler; a prefix composes with it. Meeting a bandit party opens a conversation with its highest-tier troop through `PlayerEncounter.DoMeeting`, which the `encounter_meeting` menu calls until the meeting is done, after which the same init starts the battle and opens `encounter` (`EncounterGameMenuBehavior.cs:2245-2271`); the second prefix marks the meeting done first for a brood or, since #694, a Wild Trolls band (`CreatureBanditRules.IsCreatureBandClan`), so spiders and bandit trolls go straight to attack or leave. The icon prefix stays spider-only: a troll band's leader is a humanoid troll, drawn by vanilla with its race's `as_hill_troll_map` action set (the bands are hill trolls only for now; `CreatureBanditLiveDataTests` checks both exist: on v1.5.6 a missing id does not throw, it returns set 0 after the native log line "Action set "%s" could not be found, using default action set!", and index 0 passes `IsValid`, so the map would get a human set, whose effect is untested; see [animation system map](bannerlord-animation-system-map.md) section 7). The third prefix (`Patch94_CreatureBandNoJoin`, #694) closes a recruitment path the prisoner rule never sees: with the Roguery perk Partners in Crime, telling a bandit party to serve under the player (`OpenRosterScreenAfterBanditEncounter`, v1.5.3 lines 420-460) hands the player every party joining the encounter on the bandits' side, a nearby brood or troll band included (bandit clans are not at war with each other), and destroys them. The prefix drops both clans from the list this method reads (its only call, line 448), which is the list the destroy loop walks (455-460), so the bands stay on the map and out of the roster while the looters still join. Applied by `CreatureBanditsModule` at game init (SandBox.View and campaign menu types). **ACTIVE, PARTLY SMOKED**: on 2026-09-29 Mike met a console-spawned troll band on the map (then an armoured cave troll leading), and no parley fired; the hill-troll icon, the no-join prefix and every brood encounter are still unsmoked.

## Patch95_FactionUI

**Target:** eight patch classes in `Main/Features/FactionUI/Hooks/`: `GauntletLayer.LoadMovie(string movieName, ViewModel dataSource)` (prefix, postfix, finalizer); `UIResourceManager.Refresh()` (postfix); `VideoPlayerView.PlayVideo(string, string, float, bool)` (prefix); `VideoPlaybackState.SetStartingParameters` (prefix); the private `MBMusicManager.ActivateMenuMode` (prefix, may skip); the `LoadingWindowViewModel.LoadingImageName` setter (prefix); the `BodyGeneratorView` constructor (prefix, bound as the type's only constructor, the Patch77 way); the static `BodyGeneratorView.InitCamera(Camera camera, Vec3 cameraPosition)` (prefix, postfix).

**Dedicated server (ADR-013, B6c):** not applied, core-target classes included (`SubModule.InitializeFactionUi`): those three only theme menu music and video.

Kysaro's themed front end, merged from his TAOM_FactionUI module (#704). The load prefix swaps a vanilla movie for a themed one when `FrontEndMovieService` matches both the movie name and the view-model type (main menu, version line, loading window, and the face generator, backstory, review, banner, clan-naming and options stages; the face generator and banner editor only inside character creation, so the barber and the campaign banner editor stay vanilla), after registering the image groups it needs; a themed prefab missing from the install falls back to vanilla with one warning. If the themed movie throws while building, the finalizer removes what the failed build attached to the layer's root (the movie's constructor attaches its root widget before the prefab is instantiated, v1.5.3 `GauntletMovie.cs:56-57`), gives the hold back and loads vanilla's movie, so a broken prefab costs the theme, not the screen. The faction screen is TAOM's own movie with no vanilla one behind it (`MovieSwap.HasVanillaFallback`), so there the finalizer rethrows and `FactionScreenLauncher` shows the faction map; the launcher also checks the prefab is installed before loading it. A themed movie that threw is not tried again until restart (`FrontEndMovieService.LoadFailed`, and a latch in the launcher): the failed `GauntletMovie` stays subscribed to the widget and brush factories (v1.5.3 `GauntletMovie.cs:52-53`, undone only by `Release`, which nothing can call on it), so each retry would retain another. A runtime UI resource refresh reloads every movie through the private `LoadMovie`, past this patch; the image tables are restored by the `Refresh` postfix, but the effects classes keep the replaced widgets (cosmetic, and possible only while the main menu is active, where the engine allows a runtime module load). The postfix records which movie object holds which image groups and starts each themed screen's live effects.

Release is not a patch. A screen's layer releases its movies through `GauntletLayer.OnFinalize` -> `ClearContext` -> `movie.Release()`, never through the public `ReleaseMovie`, so the first build's `ReleaseMovie` prefix never saw a themed screen close and no image was ever freed (found by the review, removed). `FactionUITicker` instead asks each held movie whether it is released (`IGauntletMovie.IsReleased`) once per tick, and `FrontEndSpriteService` frees a group's textures with the engine's own UI-texture release (`Texture.ReleaseImmediately`) 60 ticks after nothing holds it. The `Refresh` postfix exists because `UIResourceManager.Refresh` replaces the sprite table, the font factory and the brush factory; its runtime caller runs it between releasing and rebuilding the open movies, and only when the native side loads a module at runtime (`GauntletUISubModule.OnNewModuleLoad`), so the postfix puts back every image, font and brush before a themed screen is rebuilt.

The constructor prefix runs before `InitCamera` (v1.5.3 `BodyGeneratorView.cs:156`, before its movie at :185): it names the face generator as the camera's owner and, inside character creation only, copies a pending pick's look and gear onto the player and swaps `dressedEquipment` for that gear. The pick's name and skills wait for the hero-preset finalize handler at priority 1060 (Option A, Mike 2026-10-01), so backing out of a pick leaves nothing invisible behind. Patch77 (Player Switcher) postfixes the same constructor; the two never act together, because the faction screen hides Player Switcher's panel (`SetPickerHidden`) for any character creation in which it is shown. A hero taken over on the faction screen skips the face generator altogether (`CharacterCreationStagesAdapter`, from the 1060 handler's `OnStageCompleted`), so this prefix sees only copied picks, or a takeover whose stage list was not vanilla's. The `InitCamera` prefix moves the camera by the owning stage's offsets from `facegen_camera.json`, and the postfix widens its vertical field of view; the backstory, review and options views load their movie before `InitCamera`, the face generator after, which is why the owner is set from two places.

The music prefix skips `PsaiCore.MenuModeEnter` on the main menu while the menu video plays its own audio, and marks the mode Menu so `Update` does not retry; `ActivateMenuMode`'s only caller, `Update`, sits inside its `!_systemPaused` branch (v1.5.3 `MBMusicManager.cs:566`), so skipping it drops no pause gate. Kysaro's matching `DeactivateMenuMode` prefix is not ported: vanilla's body is `MenuModeLeave(); CurrentMode = Paused;` and `Logik.MenuModeLeave` ignores the command when psai never entered menu mode. Leaving the main menu lifts the silence from `FactionUITicker`, in place of his prefix on the per-frame `MBMusicManager.Update`. Known limitation, shared with his module and unverified in game: after a round trip through another music-menu state (Custom Battle's setup), the mode is already Menu when the main menu returns and `ActivateMenuMode` fires only from Paused, so the theme may play under the video. The loading window gets its fonts and stone bars registered before its movie is built, in place of his two `LoadingWindowWidget.OnLateUpdate` postfixes, and the image-name prefix acts only while the window is enabled. No target is per frame, so nothing joins `PatchShieldPolicy.ExcludedTargetMethods`. Applied in `OnSubModuleLoad`, the early batch: the splash and main menu render before any campaign, and the engine builds the loading window on its first application tick (`GauntletUISubModule.OnApplicationTick`). `FactionUIBindingTests` pins every target's parameter names (the constructor's thirteen included), the `Refresh` shape, the reflected members (catalogued in [reflection-sites.md](taleworlds-api-snapshot/reflection-sites.md)), the `MusicMode` values, the category on all eight classes, and that SubModule applies, initializes, ticks and resets the patch. Feature: [faction-ui.md](../features/faction-ui.md). **ACTIVE.** Mike's first in-game look (2026-10-01) was on the build before the review fixes; the fixed build is not yet deployed or smoked.

## Patch96_TournamentRewards

**Target:** two classes in `Main/Features/TournamentRewards/Hooks/Patch96_TournamentRewards.cs`: `SandBox.Tournaments.MissionLogics.TournamentBehavior.GetMaximumBet()` (postfix); the private `TournamentCampaignBehavior.game_menu_tournament_join_current_game_on_consequence(MenuCallbackArgs args)` (prefix, may skip).

Tournament rewards (2026-10-02). The bet postfix replaces vanilla's `const` 150 (Deep Pockets x2) with the MCM cap, 0 meaning a 50,000,000 ceiling below the `OverallExpectedDenars` overflow; every reader of the cap (the bet slider, button and text, `TournamentVM.cs:207, 922, 1066`, all in the tournament UI) goes through this method, and on a fault vanilla's cap stands, with one warning logged because the UI reads the cap repeatedly. The hero count the model needs is noted by no patch: `TournamentRewardsBehavior`'s `TournamentFinished` listener notes it per town (the winner panel asks the model again, by town, after the event) before vanilla's handler asks `GetRenownReward` and `GetInfluenceReward`; both callers of the event reach it (`TournamentBehavior.EndCurrentMatch` for a played or watched tournament, `TournamentManager.ResolveTournament` off-screen). That order is the engine's, not TAOM's: an `MbEvent` runs its last-registered listener first and TAOM's behavior is appended after vanilla's, which the behavior's doc comment traces through the v1.5.3 sources (re-read it on an engine bump). An `MbEvent` also invokes its listeners with no try, so the listener catches and logs its own faults rather than skip vanilla's award. A prefix on the private `OnTournamentFinished` did this job until 2026-10-04; the listener replaced it because that prefix was one more string-named private target in a category Harmony stops at its first failing class (`PatchCategoryApplier.cs:65`). The join prefix skips vanilla's Join, shows the prize and skill dialogs, and on the final pick re-enters the whole vanilla method through a `[ThreadStatic]` bypass (vanilla: switch to the town menu, open the mission, `OnPlayerJoinTournament`), so nothing vanilla does is re-implemented and no safety gate is dropped; closing a dialog leaves the player in the join menu. Its catch returns `true` before any dialog shows: vanilla's Join is a safe default there, being the unpatched game. Classed ReviewedSafe for co-op (`CoopVetoClassificationTests`). No target is per frame. Applied at GameInit through `TournamentRewardsModule`. `TournamentRewardsBindingTests` (a `BindingVerification` class, so the engine-bump gate runs it) pins the bet target, the join prefix's parameter name, the module's category on both classes and the private `TournamentGame.Prize` setter the join adapter invokes, which is also a Category B row in [reflection-sites.md](taleworlds-api-snapshot/reflection-sites.md); `TournamentRewardsWiringTests` pins the module's declarations and the shared reward service. Feature: [tournament-rewards.md](../features/tournament-rewards.md). **ACTIVE**, not yet smoked in game.

## Patch97_MissionTickProfiler

**Target:** `public void Mission.OnTick(float dt, float realDt, bool updateCamera, bool doAsyncAITick)` (transpiler) and the `[MBCallback] internal void Mission.OnPreTick(float dt)` (transpiler, bound by name with its argument type). `Main/Features/MissionPerf/Hooks/Patch97_MissionTickProfiler.cs`. Since plan 041 also two attribution transpilers in `Patch97_MissionAttribution.cs`: `public Agent Mission.SpawnAgent(AgentBuildData, bool, Equipment, ItemObject)` and `[EngineCallback] internal void ManagedScriptHolder.TickComponents(float dt)`.

The tick profiler (2026-10-02): per-behaviour tick time and main-thread allocation, the engine phases managed code can see, and one `[Hitch]` line per slow frame. Four call-site swaps, each stack-identical (an instance `call`/`callvirt` becomes a static `call` of a `MissionTickProfilerHooks` helper taking the instance first, mutated in place so labels stay): in `Mission.OnTick` the one `callvirt MissionBehavior::OnPreDisplayMissionTick(float32)` and the one `callvirt MissionBehavior::OnMissionTick(float32)`; in `Mission.OnPreTick` the one `call Mission::WaitTickCompletion()` and the one `callvirt MissionBehavior::OnPreMissionTick(float32)`. The frame boundary (one frame is one mission tick) was this category's `OnPreTick` prefix until plan 041 moved it to `Patch98_HitchProbe`, which the installer applies right after this category, so the profiler measures only with Patch98 installed. The attribution transpilers (plan 041) swap, in `Mission.SpawnAgent`, both `callvirt MissionBehavior::OnAgentBuild(Agent, Banner)` for `MissionAttributionHooks.TimedAgentBuild` (2 sites), and in `ManagedScriptHolder.TickComponents` the four `call TWParallel::For(int, int, float, ParallelForWithDtAuxPredicate, int)` for three `TimedParallelBlock` and one `TimedOccasionalBlock` by occurrence plus the one `callvirt ScriptComponentBehavior::OnTick(float)` for `TimedScriptTick` (5 sites, 4 when the open delegate over the protected internal virtual does not bind); the `attribution install` line reports the counts, and a mismatch leaves that method vanilla with one warning. `WaitTickCompletion` (17 bytes of IL with a loop) is timed at its call site through a cached open delegate rather than patched: whether the JIT inlines it is unverified, and an inlined copy would bypass a prefix on it. The transpilers soft-fail (`TickProfilerTranspiler`): a site not found as many times as its swap has helpers, or a helper that does not fit its target, leaves the whole method vanilla with one `[TickProfiler]` warning, and a rerun on its own output bails the same way. The agent-tick phase comes from Patch91's bracket on `Mission.TickAgentsAndTeamsImp`.

Threads: `Mission.OnTick` and `Mission.OnPreTick` run on the main thread; the agent-tick pair runs on the asynchronous AI thread or inline, so its totals are `Interlocked` and the main-thread id decides whether it is subtracted from `otherMs`. Installed by `MissionTickProfilerInstaller.InstallIfEnabled` inside the once-per-process game-init block, right after Patch91, and only when the MCM toggle "Enable Tick Profiler" (Battle Load Diagnostics, default OFF, `RequireRestart = true`) reads on there: game init precedes every mission, so nothing that calls `Mission.OnPreTick` has run and no earlier-compiled caller holds an inlined copy. `SubModule.cs` never names the category. Off, no patch exists. The install is a startup fact: each mission start re-reads `OnTickSites` and, through `Harmony.GetPatchInfo`, the patch methods the profiler needs (Patch97's `Mission.OnTick` transpiler, Patch98's `Mission.OnPreTick` prefix, Patch91's agent-tick prefix and finalizer), and a missing one (another mod's transpiler took an anchor, or PatchShield stripped TAOM's patch: the strip removes Patch97's transpiler, so nothing rewrites `OnTickSites` afterwards) means the profiler times no behaviours for that mission and writes one `[TickProfiler]` warning naming it (`MissionTickProfilerHealth`, `HookHealth`); with the hitch probe on, the default, the mission still measures in probe mode unless the missing hook is Patch98's frame boundary. While a mission measures, every tick re-reads only `OnTickSites` (a field), so a later rewrite that lowers it stops the mission at once; a strip mid-mission is found at the next mission start, because patch info is deserialized per read and each patch method resolved by a scan of the loaded assemblies. Of the three targets (`Mission.OnTick`, `Mission.OnPreTick`, `Mission.TickAgentsAndTeamsImp`) only `TickAgentsAndTeamsImp` is on `PatchShieldPolicy.ExcludedTargetMethods`, for every player: it carries Patch91's bracket, and a swallow there would skip the body that sets `tickCompleted`, so the next `WaitTickCompletion` would spin forever. Given up, for any owner's patch on it: the swallow of a MissingMethod, MissingField or TypeLoad exception (no other type was ever swallowed) and the strip of the offending patch; an exception escaping the asynchronous agent tick reaches the native job thread. `Mission.OnTick` and `Mission.OnPreTick` were on the list too from plan 028, for cost alone, until maintainer decision D13 (2026-10-03) took them off once plan 034 had made PatchShield's finalizer cheap (the figures are in `PatchShieldPolicy`'s `ExcludedTargetNamespacePrefixes` comment); PatchShield wraps them like any other patched method. A foreign mod's load-time patch on either is covered from a process's first game, TAOM's own (Patch97, Patch98) from the second game start, since they apply in the first game's late batch, after both passes. The shield classifies by exception type only, so any MissingMethod, MissingField or TypeLoad exception escaping the method is swallowed, a mission behaviour's included, and the rescue strips the prefixes, postfixes and transpilers of every unprotected owner on the method without finding out which patch threw; owner `com.taom.mod` is not protected (FOLLOW-UP L1), so a swallow on either method costs TAOM's own Patch97 transpilers and Patch98 prefix there too; Patch98's finalizers are never stripped and stay. The kept exclusions on `TickAgentsAndTeamsImp` and `WaitTickCompletion` (Patch98's target) stop a swallow only at those two methods, and their callers `Mission.OnTick` (the synchronous agent tick) and `Mission.OnPreTick` (the wait) are shielded, so the exception is swallowed one level up. An exception that escapes `OnTick` after it clears `tickCompleted` (`Mission.cs:3756`) and before the agent tick sets it again (`:3629`), swallowed by any catcher above, leaves the next `WaitTickCompletion` spinning; no list entry prevents that, and the re-shield did not cause it (read from the v1.5.3 decompile, not seen in a game; for the PatchShield follow-up plan). Throw positions, catchers and the remedy: the feature doc, "PatchShield". PatchShield writes one diag.log line per patched method it skips by name. A `[Hitch]` line is written for the first 100 hitch frames of a mission; later ones are counted in `[TickSummary]`. `MissionTickProfilerBindingTests` runs both rewrites against the installed IL and walks the targets through `IsExcludedTargetMethod` both ways (the agent tick excluded, the two tick methods not). Composes with Patch98's brackets on `Mission.OnTick` and `Mission.OnPreTick` (a `Priority.First` prefix and a `Priority.Last` finalizer around this category's rewritten bodies). Feature: [mission-perf-heartbeat.md](../features/mission-perf-heartbeat.md). **ACTIVE** (off by default), not yet smoked in game.

## Patch98_HitchProbe

**Target:** six whole-method brackets in `Main/Features/MissionPerf/Hooks/Patch98_HitchProbe.cs`, each a `void` prefix at `Priority.First` and a `void` finalizer at `Priority.Last`, taking only Harmony's `__state` (`out` on the prefix, `ref` on the finalizer): `private void Mission.WaitTickCompletion()`, `[MBCallback] internal void Mission.OnPreTick(float dt)`, `public void Mission.OnTick(float dt, float realDt, bool updateCamera, bool doAsyncAITick)`, `[EngineCallback] internal void ManagedScriptHolder.TickComponents(float dt)` and `public Agent Mission.SpawnAgent(AgentBuildData agentBuildData, bool spawnFromAgentVisuals, Equipment agentSpawnEquipment, ItemObject formationBannerItem)` (the only overload), and `MissionScreen`'s explicit `void IMissionSystemHandler.UpdateCamera(Mission mission, float realDt)` (named `TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade.IMissionSystemHandler.UpdateCamera`, `private final virtual`; it runs `CameraTick` and the native `MBWindowManager.PreDisplay()` inside `Mission.OnTick` and feeds the `cameraMs` column, subtracted from `missionTickMs` in probe mode and from `otherMs` in full mode; main thread; shielded by PatchShield like `Mission.OnTick`, from a process's second game start, since its category applies after PatchShield's second pass; the engine calls `PreDisplay()` only while the mission is Continuing and `!MissionEnded`, so after a battle's result `cameraMs` times `CameraTick` alone). None of the first five is virtual. The sixth is its own category, `Patch98_HitchProbe_Camera`, applied by `HitchProbeInstaller` only after `Patch98_HitchProbe` applies, so a failure of it costs only `cameraMs`: the category apply logs `[PatchApply] ... FAILED` and joins the game-initialization notice (a red chat line), a `camera bracket not installed` WARNING says `cameraMs` reads 0.00 and that time stays in `missionTickMs` (probe) or `otherMs` (full), and the install line leaves `MissionScreen.UpdateCamera` out of its targets. If `MissionScreen` itself cannot be loaded, TAOM skips the camera class at load (a `[PatchApply] ... SKIPPED` ERROR, named in the startup notice): the category then applies nothing, no `camera bracket not installed` WARNING is written, and the install line still lists `MissionScreen.UpdateCamera`; at an engine bump, `CameraTarget_IsMissionScreensSealedExplicitUpdateCamera` fails first.

The hitch probe (plan 041, 2026-10-02): every player's `taom_debug.log` says where each slow battle frame went, with a `[HitchDetail]` line beside plan 028's `[Hitch]` (spawn, scene script and clip-loading share, the raw brackets) and per-window `[ScriptProfile]`, `[SpawnProfile]` and `[AnimLoad]` lines plus a `[TickSummaryExtra]` per mission. The `OnPreTick` prefix is the frame boundary for both the probe and the Patch97 profiler (it moved here from Patch97), then samples `MBAnimation.IsAnyAnimationLoadingFromDisk()` once per frame when its measured median cost is within 20 us. The priorities make each bracket enclose every other patch on its method (Patch23 on `SpawnAgent`, Patch97 on `OnTick`); a `void` finalizer closes the bracket when the method throws and leaves the exception alone (Harmony keeps `rethrow` only while every finalizer on the method is void; on the four shielded targets PatchShield's value-returning finalizer makes it `throw`, and `RethrowStackPreserver` keeps the trace). No transpiler.

Threads: `WaitTickCompletion`, `OnPreTick` and `OnTick` run on the main thread once per mission frame; `SpawnAgent` runs once per spawned agent, on the main thread per the thread table, which the hook checks (an off-main call is counted, not timed, with one WARNING line); `TickComponents` runs once per ticking scene per frame on a thread native picks, so its bracket is `[ThreadStatic]` and `Interlocked`, and the first measured call names its thread once. `SpawnAgent` brackets nest (a depth counter times the outermost call). Wait self-check: in `probe` mode, if the `WaitTickCompletion` bracket did not run inside any of the process's first 30 measured `OnPreTick` calls (the 17-byte method inlined by the JIT, or skipped by another patch), one WARNING line says `waitTickMs` reads 0 and `preTickMs` includes the wait; with Patch97's wait swap live, the bracket records nothing so the wait is counted once.

Installed by `HitchProbeInstaller.Install`, called from `MissionTickProfilerInstaller.InstallIfEnabled` after the Patch97 work or its skip, inside SubModule's once-per-process game-init block; `SubModule.cs` never names the category. MCM toggle "Enable Hitch Probe" (Battle Load Diagnostics, default ON, `RequireRestart = true`; on a dedicated server the default is off unless `taom-server-settings.json` sets it, [server-settings](../features/server-settings.md)); the category also installs when "Enable Tick Profiler" is on, since it holds the frame boundary. Modes: both toggles off, nothing installed (`probe off:` line); the probe alone, `probe`; the profiler on, `full`. Measured cost of the managed brackets, six dummy methods including the camera: 0.70 to 0.75 us per simulated frame (four runs on 2026-10-15; a same-session A/B against the five-bracket tree at `caefaa1f3` read 0.646, 0.665 and 0.767 us for five brackets and 0.836, 0.775 and 0.715 us for six, so the camera bracket's own cost is inside run-to-run noise, about 0.1 us or less) against a 50 us (0.5% of a 10 ms frame) target (`HitchProbeOverheadBenchmarkTests`, opt-in; the native clip-loading call is stubbed there, so the figure says nothing of it: the `anim-loading sample` line is the median of 32 calls at the first measured mission, a one-time eligibility check for sampling within 20 us, not a continuing budget, and the walk's safety against the engine's own writers is UNVERIFIED). A finalizer that runs with no prefix before it in its own call (PatchShield's rescue strips an unprotected owner's prefixes, postfixes and transpilers and never finalizers, and `com.taom.mod` is unprotected; a prefix before ours that throws also keeps ours from running, while one that returns `false` does not) records nothing, is counted, and writes one WARNING per method per process, with every such call counted in one WARNING at each measured mission's end: each call carries its own `ProbeState` from its prefix to its finalizer in Harmony's `__state` (started at 0 by Harmony in every call, set as the first statement of each enter hook, closed by the finalizer; the only code ahead of an enter hook is the frame boundary at the top of the `OnPreTick` prefix, which catches its own faults), so a prefix that returned early because nothing was measuring still pairs with its finalizer, a nested call that lost its prefix cannot close the outer call, and a rerun of the finalizers after a later one throws records and warns about nothing. PatchShield: `Mission.WaitTickCompletion` and `ManagedScriptHolder.TickComponents` are on `PatchShieldPolicy.ExcludedTargetMethods` for every player (the wait because a swallow would skip its loop and let `OnPreMissionTick` overlap the agent tick; the script tick as built); given up, for any owner's patch on the two, the missing-API swallow and the strip of the offending patch. `Mission.OnPreTick`, `Mission.OnTick` (plan 028) and `Mission.SpawnAgent` (plan 041) were excluded for cost until maintainer decision D13 (2026-10-03) took them off once plan 034 had made PatchShield's finalizer cheap (the figures are in `PatchShieldPolicy`'s `ExcludedTargetNamespacePrefixes` comment); they are shielded like any other patched method, with the loss that implies: a missing-API exception escaping `SpawnAgent` strips Patch23's prefix and Patch97's spawn attribution transpiler along with Patch98's prefix, and the kept exclusions on `WaitTickCompletion` and `TickAgentsAndTeamsImp` (the agent tick Patch91 brackets) do not stop a swallow one level up, at their shielded callers `OnPreTick` and `OnTick` (`TickComponents`, the third kept exclusion, has no such caller: native reaches it through a shim in the `ManagedCallbacks` namespace, which PatchShield excludes; see the feature doc). `HitchProbeBindingTests` resolves the six targets, checks none is overridable (the camera target is final), pins the camera target to `MissionScreen`'s interface slot and `Mission.OnTick`'s single call to it, and walks them and the two attribution transpilers' targets through `IsExcludedTargetMethod`, pinning both halves of the split; `HitchProbeWiringTests` pins the priorities and the frame boundary's placement. Feature: [mission-perf-heartbeat.md](../features/mission-perf-heartbeat.md). **ACTIVE** (on by default), not yet smoked in game.

## Patch99_XmlMergeFastPath

**Target:** `TaleWorlds.ObjectSystem.MBObjectManager.CreateMergedXmlFile(List<Tuple<string, string>> toBeMerged, List<string> xsltList, bool skipValidation)` (prefix at `Priority.Last` that may skip the original; `void` finalizer). Class `MBObjectManager_CreateMergedXmlFile_Patch` in `Main/Features/XmlMerge/Hooks/`.

Module-XML merge fast path (plan 042, 2026-10-03). The engine's merge loop converts the whole document merged so far between `XmlDocument` and `XDocument` for every file and compiles every XSLT on every call, so a type's merge grows with the square of its size: about 28 s of a new campaign's 50.2 s loading screen and 14 s of a custom battle's load (rgl logs, 2026-10-02). For validated merges (`skipValidation == false`: every `LoadXML` from `Campaign.OnInitialize` and `CustomGame.OnInitialize`, GameText, BannerIcons; about 30 to 40 calls per load, never per frame) the prefix hands the call to `XmlMergeService`, which runs the same loop with the same engine helpers in the same order (the private `CreateDocumentFromXmlFile` through a cached delegate, the public `MergeElements`, `ToXDocument`, `ToXmlDocument`) but keeps one `XDocument` across files and compiles each XSLT once per process (`XsltTransformCache`, re-validated by last-write time and length). Responsibilities of the skipped original: each file loads and validates through the engine's own loader (the rgl `opening` lines and validation messages are unchanged); each entry's XSLT applies to the document so far before that entry's file, and index 0's never; each file merges through `MergeElements`, or is appended when its XSD path is ""; the round trip of the accumulated document between merges is the one step dropped, proven output-neutral on every type of the live install by `XmlMergeLiveEquivalenceTests` (the gate, run for `Campaign` and `CustomGame`); the result is the identical document on that module set (two inputs no installed module carries can differ without an exception: the feature doc's "Known limits"). Unvalidated merges (`GetMergedXmlForNative`, `CoreParameters`, the custom battle's `CustomBattleScenes` read, TAOM's `ObjectManagerAdapter` and `MonsterSizeCatalogAdapter`) keep the engine's code. The prefix stands aside (`reason=foreign-patch`) while another owner than `com.taom.mod` and PatchShield has a prefix, transpiler, inner prefix or inner postfix on the target, or any patch on `ApplyXslt`, `MergeTwoXmls`, `ToXDocument` or `ToXmlDocument`, read from all six of Harmony's collections. Any exception lets the original run on untouched arguments (the unpatched game's outcome, with the PatchShield caveat below; the fast path writes nothing outside its own documents); when that original then succeeds, the finalizer turns the fast path off for the session with one WARNING. The finalizer is `void`, so it never replaces the engine's exception, and Harmony keeps `rethrow` only while every finalizer on the method is void. **PatchShield: plan 040 put `CreateMergedXmlFile` on `PatchShieldPolicy.ExcludedTargetMethods`, so this patch adds no entry of its own, and the two ship together** (feature doc, "Shipping order"). With that exclusion, the only configuration that ships, PatchShield never attaches to the method and every finalizer on it is `void` (Patch99's and plan 040's Patch100), so Harmony keeps `rethrow` and the engine's exception leaves with its own trace. Without it (were the entry ever removed), PatchShield's second pass (the first game init) attaches its value-returning `ShieldFinalizerWithResult`: Harmony then uses `throw`, `RethrowStackPreserver` keeps the engine's trace, and the finalizer swallows `MissingMethodException`, `MissingFieldException` and `TypeLoadException` into a null document, which `MBObjectManager.LoadXML` loads as an empty type where the unpatched game lets the exception propagate. Applied at ProcessLoad through `XmlMergeModule` (a game's merges run long before the late batch); the per-game `[XmlMerge] summary` line comes from one call in `SubModule.OnGameInitializationFinished`. Classed ReviewedSafe for co-op (`CoopVetoClassificationTests`). `XmlMergeBindingTests` pins the target's signature and parameter names, every member the adapter reaches, and, through `IlCallScanner`, that `CreateMergedXmlFile` calls exactly `CreateDocumentFromXmlFile`, `ApplyXslt` and `MergeTwoXmls` and `MergeTwoXmls` exactly `ToXDocument`, `MergeElements` and `ToXmlDocument`, and the ordered call sequence of those two, `ApplyXslt`, `ToXDocument` and `ToXmlDocument`: a red IL test after an engine update means the fast path must be re-proven before it ships again. `ReflectionSiteBindingTests` pins the private loader. Feature: [xml-merge-fast-path.md](../features/xml-merge-fast-path.md). **ACTIVE**, not yet smoked in game.

## Patch100_LoadTimeStamps_LoadXml

**Target:** `MBObjectManager.LoadXML(string id, bool isDevelopment, string gameType, bool skipXmlFilterForEditor)` (prefix plus void finalizer, bound with its argument types); the static `MBObjectManager.CreateMergedXmlFile(List<Tuple<string, string>> toBeMerged, List<string> xsltList, bool skipValidation)` (void finalizer only). `Main/Features/LoadTimeStamps/Hooks/MBObjectManager_LoadXML_StampPatch.cs` and `MBObjectManager_CreateMergedXmlFile_StampPatch.cs`.

The always-on `[LoadXml]` stamp (plan 040). The engine's log attributes about 28 s of a 50 s new-campaign load to the module XML merge, and `taom_debug.log` said nothing about it. The `LoadXML` prefix starts a call on a thread-local stack in `LoadXmlStampService`; the `CreateMergedXmlFile` finalizer marks the end of the first merge of the call in flight on that thread with its merged file and XSLT counts; the `LoadXML` finalizer logs one INFO line per call (`id`, `files`, `ms`, `xslt`, `merge_ms` from entry to the merge's end, `objects_ms` the rest, `result`) and adds it to the summary that `SubModule.OnGameInitializationFinished` writes for every game initialization. Every engine caller of `LoadXML` in a campaign or a custom battle runs before TAOM's `OnGameInitializationFinished`: inside the game type's `OnInitialize`, which ends by calling `GameManager.OnGameInitializationFinished` (v1.5.3 `Campaign.cs:1471`), or, for SandBox's `MusicInstruments` and `MusicTracks`, in SandBox's own `OnGameInitializationFinished` (`SandBoxSubModule.cs:113-114`), which runs before TAOM's because SandBox loads first. So each summary covers that load. A merge outside `LoadXML` (GameText, the native merges, TAOM's own `ObjectManagerAdapter` and `MonsterSizeCatalogAdapter`) finds no call in flight and gets no line. Both finalizers are `void`, so Harmony rethrows the original exception with its stack (`LoadXmlStampPatchShapeTests` pins the type, message and throw site), and every forwarder in `LoadTimeStampsHooks` swallows its own faults; a fault in the stamp warns once per process. Toggle on or off, a crash thrown through an XML load shows a TAOM-patched frame (`com.taom.mod`'s observe-only replacement of `LoadXML` or `CreateMergedXmlFile`) in TAOM's crash report; the exception is the engine's. The `CreateMergedXmlFile` finalizer runs whether or not another prefix skipped the original (plan 042's fast path); never add a prefix there, and never patch the methods plan 042 watches (`ApplyXslt`, `MergeTwoXmls`, `CreateDocumentFromXmlFile`, `LoadXmlWithValidation` and the conversions).

Always on: applied for every player, whatever "Enable Load-Time Stamps" says, by `LoadTimeStampsModule` at `OnSubModuleLoad`, before the first game's XML loads; its apply time is in the `[PatchApply] phase=OnSubModuleLoad scope=total` line, and in its own `[PatchApply]` category line when the toggle is on. **PatchShield:** both targets are on `PatchShieldPolicy.ExcludedTargetMethods`. They are not hot (once per XML type per load), but a shield finalizer would swallow a `MissingMethodException`, `MissingFieldException` or `TypeLoadException` from the merge and return a null document, which `LoadXML`'s own catch turns into a silently missing ModuleData type, would switch the method's wrapper from `rethrow` to `throw`, and would add a `Harmony.Patch` per target at every player's first game start. Trade-off: PatchShield skips an excluded method for every owner, so another mod's patch on either method loses the rescue. `LoadTimeStampsBindingTests` pins the parameter names, the category, and walks both real targets through `IsExcludedTargetMethod`. Feature: [load-time-stamps.md](../features/load-time-stamps.md). **ACTIVE**, not yet seen in a real game load.

## Patch100_LoadTimeStamps_Lifecycle

**Target:** the five `CampaignEventDispatcher` lifecycle methods, one overload each, each taking `(CampaignGameStarter campaignGameStarter)`: `OnNewGameCreated`, `OnGameEarlyLoaded`, `OnGameLoaded`, `OnSessionStart`, `OnAfterSessionStart` (a prefix plus a void finalizer on each). `Main/Features/LoadTimeStamps/Hooks/CampaignEventDispatcher_Lifecycle_StampPatches.cs`.

The `[Lifecycle]` stamps (plan 040). Two stretches of a 50 s new-campaign load, 3.37 s and 3.18 s, had no line in any log; both sit inside the new-game event fan-out, where every campaign behaviour's handlers run. With "Enable Load-Time Stamps" on (default off), each prefix asks `LifecycleTimingService` to swap every listener of the dispatch's `CampaignEvents` events (`OnNewGameCreated` also covers its 100 `PartialFollowUp` rounds and `PartialFollowUpEnd`) for a wrapper that times the original call in a `try/finally` with no catch (`CampaignListenerAdapter`, through `MbEvent`'s private `_nonSerializedListenerList` and each record's private `Action` setter). The void finalizer logs a line for each handler at or over 10 ms, a total per event with the TAOM and other split, and the dispatch line (`ms` runs from the end of the swap to the finalizer, read before any line is written; `ms - listeners_ms` is the non-listener time: the `QuestManager` receiver in v1.5.3, any receiver a mod adds, the wrappers' per-call cost and any listener added mid-dispatch), then puts every original delegate back, whether or not the dispatch threw. Order, arguments and exceptions are unchanged; only an exception's stack gains the wrapper's frame while the toggle is on, and, toggle on or off, a crash thrown through one of these dispatches shows a TAOM-patched frame (the observe-only replacement method) in TAOM's crash report. Every owner's handler is timed, each line naming its assembly. Every dispatch gets its dispatch line whatever the toggle: with the toggle off the prefix reads the clock, swaps nothing and asks the adapter nothing, and the finalizer writes that one line (`listeners_ms=none`), so every player's log says what the new-game fan-out took. A listener-record member missing after an engine change turns per-handler timing off with one WARNING (toggle on only) and keeps the dispatch totals.

Applied for every player at `OnSubModuleLoad` by `LoadTimeStampsModule`, toggle on or off, in its own category so engine drift here never costs the XML stamp (the `Patch89` precedent); its apply time is in the `[PatchApply]` lines. Applying it only when the toggle is on (option B of the plan 040 review; plan 040's Maintenance note "Lifecycle only when on") no longer fits: the always-written dispatch line needs these patches for every player. **PatchShield:** all five targets are on `PatchShieldPolicy.ExcludedTargetMethods`. They are not hot (once per event per load), but a shield finalizer would swallow a missing-API exception from any campaign handler, which propagates today, and end the dispatch early: the remaining listeners, PartialFollowUp rounds and receivers would be skipped and the load would continue half-initialized. Trade-off: PatchShield skips an excluded method for every owner, so another mod's patch on one of these five methods loses the rescue. `LoadTimeStampsBindingTests` pins the single overloads, the event accessors' types, the category, and walks all five real targets through `IsExcludedTargetMethod`; `CampaignListenerAdapterTests` pins the swap on real `MbEvent` instances; the reflected members are catalogued in [reflection-sites.md](taleworlds-api-snapshot/reflection-sites.md). Feature: [load-time-stamps.md](../features/load-time-stamps.md). **ACTIVE**, not yet seen in a real game load.

## Patch101_MapFrameProfiler

**Target:** core category `Patch101_MapFrameProfiler`: `protected override void MapState.OnTick(float dt)` (prefix `Priority.First`, the frame boundary then a stamp; postfix `Priority.Last`), `internal void Campaign.RealTick(float realDt)` and `internal void Campaign.Tick()` (prefix `First` and postfix `Last` each, bound by `TargetMethod`), `public override void CampaignEvents.Tick(float dt)` (transpiler), `protected override void MapScreen.OnFrameTick(float dt)` (prefix `First`, postfix `Last`) and TAOM's own `protected override void SubModule.OnApplicationTick(float dt)` (prefix `First`, postfix `Last`, `TargetMethod`); views category `Patch101_MapFrameProfiler_Views`: every TAOM `MapView` subclass's own override of `OnFrameTick`, `OnMapScreenUpdate`, `OnMenuModeTick` or `OnIdleTick` (`TargetMethods()`, found by reflection over TAOM.dll and TAOM.Client.dll through `ClientComposition.Assembly`: the three `OnMapScreenUpdate` overrides of `FieldCampMapView`, `MomentumIndicatorMapView` and `RealmBordersMapView` at v1.5.3), prefix `First` and postfix `Last` with `__instance`. menu category `Patch101_MapFrameProfiler_Menu` (#1019, applied only with the toggle on): `GameMenuVM.OnFrameTick()`, the `GameMenuVM.ContextText` setter and `ServiceStatusService.RefreshIfChanged()`, each a prefix `First` and a postfix `Last` bound by `TargetMethod()` (`MapProfilerTargets.MenuTargets()`; the two `GameMenuVM` classes are `[ClientOnly]`), entries `GameMenuVM`, `Patch64_MenuLinkColors` (counts toward `taomMs`) and `ServiceStatusService`, nested and not additive. `Main/Features/MapPerf/Hooks/Patch101_MapFrameProfiler.cs`, `Patch101_MapFrameProfilerViews.cs`, `Patch101_MapFrameProfilerMenu.cs`.

**Dedicated server (ADR-013, B6c):** `MapFrameProfilerInstaller.OnGameInitialized` is not called (an inline server check in `OnGameInitializationFinished`).

The campaign map profiler (2026-10-03, plan 039): it splits each map frame into `mapStateMs` (with `realTickMs` and `campaignTickMs` inside it, and `tickEventMs` inside `campaignTickMs`), `mapScreenMs`, TAOM's `appTickMs` and `otherMs`, attributes every `CampaignEvents.TickEvent` listener by its owner's type and every TAOM map view by its type, and writes `[MapProfile]` every 5 s of wall clock and `[MapProfileSummary]` per campaign session; the speed class reads `Campaign.GetSimplifiedTimeControlMode()` through `ITimeControlAdapter.SimplifiedTimeControlMode`, so a Stoppable mode with the main party waiting reads `Stop`. Prefix and postfix pairs only, never a finalizer, so no exception path changes; each pair passes its start stamp through `__state`. The transpiler makes one stack-identical swap: `CampaignEvents.Tick`'s one `callvirt MbEvent<float>::Invoke(float)` becomes `call MapFrameProfilerHooks.TimedTickEvent(MbEvent<float>, float)`, through plan 028's `TickProfilerTranspiler.Rewrite`, which soft-fails: a site not found exactly once leaves the method vanilla with one `[TickProfiler]` warning, and the installer adds a `[MapProfiler]` line saying `tickEventMs` reads 0. The helper calls `Invoke` unchanged unless measuring with the listener walk bound; the walk (`TickEventListenerWalker`) reproduces `MbEvent<T>.InvokeList` statement for statement (head read once, `Action` and `Owner` read per record, the call timed in `try`/`finally`, `Next` read AFTER the call, nothing caught), so order, exception propagation, a listener removing itself and a listener added during the dispatch behave exactly as vanilla; `TickEventListenerWalkerTests` compares the two call sequences and `MapFrameProfilerBindingTests` pins the IL of `Invoke` (one read of the non-serialized list, one call of `InvokeList`, no branch, no handler) and of `InvokeList` (no handler, `Next` read after the call), and compiles every Patch101 method against the installed engine.

Threads: every target runs on the main thread. `MapFrameProfilerInstaller.OnGameInitialized` is called on every `OnGameInitializationFinished`, before the once-per-process guard, because a later game init only reports (restart needed, or the install failed); it applies both categories at most once per process, only when the MCM toggle "Enable Map Profiler" (Battle Load Diagnostics, Map Performance, default OFF, `RequireRestart = true`) reads on at the first game init, and creates the profiler only when the core category applied and all six core targets carry a `*_MapProfiler_Patch`. Game init precedes every map frame. `SubModule.cs` never names either category. Off, no patch exists. Under decision D13 (option B, the maintainer, 2026-10-03) only `Campaign.Tick`, `CampaignEvents.Tick` and (#1019) `GameMenuVM.OnFrameTick`, which no other TAOM patch targets, are on `PatchShieldPolicy.ExcludedTargetMethods`; `MapState.OnTick` (Patch43), `Campaign.RealTick` (Patch89) and `MapScreen.OnFrameTick` (Patch36, Patch89) are patched for every player and keep the shield, so a player with the profiler off sees no change. With the profiler on, a MissingMethod, MissingField or TypeLoad exception thrown anywhere inside `Campaign.Tick` or `CampaignEvents.Tick` is no longer swallowed there: it skips the rest of `Campaign.Tick` and of `MapState.OnTick` (`AfterWaitTick`, `SaveHandler.CampaignTick`) and, from a process's second game start (or the first, when another mod patched `MapState.OnTick` at load), is swallowed by the shield on `MapState.OnTick`, which strips Patch43's and Patch101's patches there (no more frames close). Otherwise it reaches `Module.OnApplicationTick`, where Patch37 runs first (priority 800 against PatchShield's 400): with crash capture on it writes a crash report and swallows; with it off it hands the exception back and PatchShield's own finalizer on that method swallows it and strips its non-protected patches, never the one that threw. A swallow on one of the three kept methods also strips Patch101's pair there, which ends that measurement for the process: the session hooks look at the six core targets again (`MapSessionHooks.LostHooks`, the installer's own patched check) at each session start, at each window start and before the summary of a measuring session and, on a lost hook, write one `[MapProfiler]` warning naming every lost one and a `reason=hooksLost` summary, then stop measuring; a strip on `MapState.OnTick` takes the boundary that runs the window check, so there the lines stop, the other hooks and the views keep measuring until the session ends, and the session end's look names it. The exclusion is by name for every owner, so another mod's patch on `Campaign.Tick`, `CampaignEvents.Tick` or `GameMenuVM.OnFrameTick` loses the shield too. `Module.OnApplicationTick`, `ScreenManager.Tick` and `ScreenManager.Update` (Patch37), the three kept methods, and Patch89's `SceneView` patches inside `mapStateMs` carry PatchShield's finalizer once per frame (plan 034). PatchShield writes one diag.log line per excluded method it skips. `MapFrameProfilerBindingTests.ProfilerOnlyTargets_AreOnPatchShieldsExclusionList` and `SharedMapTargets_StayUnderPatchShield` walk the real targets. Composes with Patch36's and Patch89's postfixes on `MapScreen.OnFrameTick`, Patch89's heartbeat on `Campaign.RealTick` and Patch43's postfix on `MapState.OnTick`, all enclosed by the brackets. Feature: [map-perf-profiler.md](../features/map-perf-profiler.md). **ACTIVE** (off by default), not yet smoked in game.

## Patch102_SiegeForces

**Target:** two classes in `Main/Features/SiegeForces/Hooks/Patch102_SiegeForces.cs`, category `Patch102_SiegeForces`: `Patch102_StartSiegeMissionPicker`, a bool prefix on the public static `PlayerSiege.StartSiegeMission(Settlement settlement = null)` (may skip); `Patch102_SpawnTotalsFit`, a void prefix on `DefaultBattleMissionAgentSpawnLogic.InitWithSinglePhase(int defenderTotalSpawn, int attackerTotalSpawn, int defenderInitialSpawn, int attackerInitialSpawn, bool spawnDefenders, bool spawnAttackers, in MissionSpawnSettings spawnSettings)` taking `object __instance` and the four ints by `ref`.

Siege Forces (#734, 2026-10-05). The picker prefix asks `SiegeForcesService.TryOfferPicker` to open vanilla's "Manage Troops" screen before a wall battle and returns false when it opened; on Done the service arms its plan and calls the public `PlayerSiege.StartSiegeMission(settlement)` again under a `[ThreadStatic]` latch in a try/finally, so the prefix steps aside and nothing vanilla does is copied. `MenuHelper.EncounterAttackConsequence` is the only caller in the campaign and SandBox sources, for an assault and a defence alike; the lord's hall, sally-out and relief-force branches never reach it. Any fault before the screen opens returns true: vanilla is the unpatched game, the safe default there. The fit prefix shrinks the player side's total and initial spawn by the troops `TaomTroopSupplierProbabilityModel` dropped from the ready list, capped at the side's final ready-list size, so deployment never waits for reserved troops that never come (v1.5.3 `CheckDeployment` :539-547). It runs for every battle that calls `InitWithSinglePhase` (campaign wall and field battles, sally-outs, Custom Battle and Custom Siege), so its guard order is the contract: a pending-record check that reads only TAOM state first, so with nothing pending no engine state is read (every Custom Battle and field battle); then a null-safe map event read (`Campaign.Current?.MainParty?.MapEvent`, never `MapEvent.PlayerMapEvent` or `MobileParty.MainParty`, which throw without a campaign); a map event or side mismatch discards the record; the whole body sits in a try that leaves the four ints untouched. Harmony binds the four ints by name.

**Why the prefix bodies hold no engine member references.** A member that stops resolving fails when the method that names it is compiled, before any try inside that method can run, so a try in the prefix itself would not catch it (lessons/harmony-il.md). Both prefix bodies therefore name only TAOM code and `object`/`ref int` parameters; the engine work sits in `[MethodImpl(MethodImplOptions.NoInlining)]` helpers (`OfferPicker`, `Resume`, `HasPending`, `Fit`) that compile on their first call. `OfferPicker`, `HasPending` and `Fit` compile inside the prefix's try, where the fault is caught and logged. `Resume` compiles later, in the Done callback (the picker screen's button), outside the prefix's try: a fault there reaches that callback, after the latch is cleared. For `InitWithSinglePhase` an escaped fault would skip the original, leave the spawn logic with no phases and throw every tick of every battle.

**Order and the attached check.** The fit class is declared first in the file. `PatchCategoryIndex.Apply` patches a category's classes in assembly order and a class that fails stops the rest, so with the fit first a failed fit leaves the entry patch unapplied; `SiegeForcesWiringTests` reads the index's own order. As a second line, `SiegeForcesService` asks `ISiegeForcesAdapter.IsSpawnTotalsFitAttached` (`Harmony.GetPatchInfo` on `InitWithSinglePhase`, looking for a prefix declared on `Patch102_SpawnTotalsFit`) once per process at the first offer and offers nothing when it is absent. A runtime fault inside the fit leaves the four totals untouched and logs "spawn totals not fitted ... deployment may stall": the ready list was already filtered by then, so the battle is not vanilla's.

**PatchShield:** neither target is on `PatchShieldPolicy.ExcludedTargetMethods`, and neither namespace is excluded, so PatchShield's finalizer wraps both. Patch102 applies at TAOM's first `OnGameInitializationFinished` (`ApplyPhase.GameInit`), after TAOM.Dependencies' shield pass 2 for that game init, so the finalizer attaches from a process's second game start, as with Patch23. It swallows only `MissingMethodException`, `MissingFieldException` and `TypeLoadException` escaping the patched method, returns the method's default (the original's remaining work does not run: no wall battle opens on that Attack, or no spawn phases for that battle) and strips the throwing owner's patches on that method, so the next call is vanilla. Both targets run once per assault or battle, never per frame. The NoInlining helpers keep such a fault inside TAOM's own try, so the shield is the second line, not the first.

Classed ReviewedSafe for co-op (`CoopVetoClassificationTests`): under a live co-op session, a host-decided peer (`ShouldDeferToHost`) or a dedicated server the service offers nothing and the prefix returns true. Overlap census (RG-K, 2026-10-05, in `SiegeForcesModule`'s summary): no other TAOM code names the targets, and no installed module patches them. Bannerlord Coop calls `InitWithSinglePhase`, and its postfix on the supplier model's method returns at once when `includePlayer` is true, the only case TAOM's filter acts on, so no priority ordering is needed. TOR_Core competes only for the supplier model slot. Applied at GameInit through `SiegeForcesModule`. `SiegeForcesBindingTests` pins both targets, the prefixes' parameter names and every engine member the helpers, the adapters and the model reference; `Patch102PrefixTests` covers the latch, the fall-backs to vanilla and the totals guard order. Feature: [siege-forces.md](../features/siege-forces.md). **ACTIVE**, not yet smoked in game.

## Patch103_MissionStartGuard

**Target:** `public void Mission.AfterStart()` (v1.5.4; non-virtual, no parameters), one class `Mission_AfterStart_MissionStartGuard_Patch` in `Main/Features/MissionStartGuard/Hooks/`, category `Patch103_MissionStartGuard`: a transpiler, a void prefix (`object __instance`, `out bool __state`, `Priority.First`), a void finalizer (`Exception __exception`, `bool __state`, `Priority.First`) and a void `[HarmonyCleanup]` method (`Exception ex`).

Mission-start guard (2026-10-08, adapted from yotthani's VanillaTuning `mission-start-guard`, MIT). `Mission.AfterStart` (`Mission.cs:3815-3852` in the v1.5.4 dump) sets `CurrentState = Continuing` last; a throw from any start call leaves the mission `Initializing`, `MissionState.FinishMissionLoading` has already cleared `_missionInitializing`, and `TickLoading` calls `LoadMission`, `Mission.Initialize` and `AfterStart` again on the next frame, for ever (#699). The transpiler is `TickProfilerTranspiler.Rewrite`, the profiler patches' soft-failing swap: each of six `callvirt` sites becomes a static `call` of the same-named helper on `MissionStartGuardCalls` (in `Hooks/`, with the swap table `MissionStartGuardSwaps`) with the same stack (instance first): `MBSubModuleBase.OnBeforeMissionBehaviorInitialize(Mission)`, `MissionBehavior.OnBehaviorInitialize()`, `MBSubModuleBase.OnMissionBehaviorInitialize(Mission)`, `MissionBehavior.EarlyStart()`, `MissionBehavior.AfterStart()` and `MissionObject.AfterMissionStart()`. Each occurs exactly once in the installed v1.5.4 body (`MissionStartGuardBindingTests`, the real IL); any other count leaves the whole method vanilla and `MissionStartGuardModule` logs one `[MissionStartGuard] OFF: wrapped N of 6` warning (the swap runs with no logger so the `[TickProfiler]` warning does not repeat it). The spawn-path selector, the deployment plan and the weather model, called by the same method, stay unwrapped (a mod can supply the last two), and so does a behaviour's `OnMissionScreenPreLoad`, called earlier by `MissionState.LoadMission`. Each helper is `try { call } catch (Exception ex) when (Survives(ex)) { report }`; the filter asks `IMissionStartGuardService.ShouldSurvive`, which is false when the toggle is off, with no service and for `OutOfMemoryException`, so a refusal leaves the exception uncaught with its original stack. A survived throw is one ERROR line (call, owning type, assembly, full exception; the first 10 per mission), at most 3 on-screen messages per mission (none on a dedicated server), and one summary WARNING when the start ends. VanillaTuning, when installed, has its own per-method finalizers that catch first, so this wrap only ever catches what they did not, and the message is then the player's only notice of it; TAOM never stands aside for it. The prefix passes the `Mission` as a weakly held token so a reload of the same mission keeps its budgets. The finalizer is void and observes only: it logs an exception that still leaves `Mission.AfterStart` (an unwrapped engine call, or any call with the toggle off), at most three per process, and passes the transpiler's latest swap count to the lost-guard check below. The service takes no lock: every caller runs on the main thread. Applied at GameInit (`MissionStartGuardModule`), which precedes every mission; the install line is written from the module's `OnPhase(GameInit)`, after the category.

**Callers of the target.** One: `MissionState.FinishMissionLoading` (`MissionState.cs:345`, v1.5.4 and the 1.4.8 and 1.5.3 dumps), once per mission, main thread. The 2026-10-08 review checked ten module assemblies and found no other caller.

**PatchShield and Patch43.** Patch43 (`Mission_AfterStart_BattleLoad_Patch`, a prefix and a postfix) already patches the method, so PatchShield's second pass attaches `ShieldFinalizerVoid` to it from a process's second game start (TAOM's late batch is patched after pass 2, so in the first game the method is unshielded). `Mission.AfterStart` is on neither exclusion list and stays shielded: once per mission, main thread (decision D13). The finalizers compose by priority, highest first, against one exception slot: this patch's void finalizer (800) runs first and changes nothing; PatchShield's (no attribute, filed as 400) then swallows a `MissingMethodException`, `MissingFieldException` or `TypeLoadException` and hands any other exception back through `RethrowStackPreserver`. Because PatchShield's finalizer returns a value, Harmony ends the wrapper with `throw` rather than `rethrow` once the shield is attached, and the engine's trace survives only through the preserver. With the toggle on a wrapped call is caught before it reaches the shield, so the shield sees only what the wrap does not cover; with the toggle off a missing-API throw from another mod's behaviour is swallowed by the shield and `AfterStart` returns without `Continuing`; `FinishMissionLoading` then runs `OnMissionLoadingFinished`, every behaviour's `OnAfterMissionLoadingFinished` and `ResumeLoadingRenderings` on the half-started mission (the unpatched game runs none of them), and the next tick loads the mission again. Patch43's postfix does not run when the original throws.

**A lost guard, and the canary.** When the shield swallows a missing-API throw it calls `Unpatch` for each owner on the method, and it does so for the prefix, the postfix and the transpiler together, never for the finalizers (`PatchShield.TryUnpatchOffendingPatches`). TAOM's owner `com.taom.mod` is not on the protected list (FOLLOW-UP L1 in the PatchShield review record), so a stripped Patch103 would lose the wrap and the prefix and keep this finalizer. The prefix is therefore the canary: it sets Harmony's `__state` to true (one value per call) before calling `BeginMission`, and the finalizer calls `EndMissionStart(__state, MissionStartGuardSwaps.LastSwapped)`, which writes ONE WARNING per process when this call's prefix did not run (the prefix, and with it the transpiler, is gone) or when the live swap count is not 6 (a later Harmony rebuild of the method soft-failed, and `LastSwapped` is the count of the transpiler's latest run). Until the 2026-10-08 review the flag was a process-wide field the finalizer cleared; Harmony reruns every finalizer when a later one throws, and the rerun read false, a false warning that also spent the latch. The prefix runs at `Priority.First`, ahead of every prefix at a lower priority (a foreign prefix at First that registered earlier, one at a higher raw priority, or one ordered before it with `HarmonyBefore` still runs first). `LogInstall`'s soft-fail warning sets the same once-per-process latch, so the install warning is not repeated by the canary. `Cleanup` (`[HarmonyCleanup]`) sets `LastSwapped` to 0 when the class fails to apply after the transpiler ran, because Harmony then registers nothing and the install line would otherwise claim six wrapped sites. `MissionStartGuardServiceTests` pin the canary cases (a call whose prefix did not run, a live count of five, a normal start, a second failure in the same process), `MissionStartGuardHookTests` pin that the finalizer passes `__state` and `LastSwapped`, and `MissionStartGuardCanaryTests` drive the real prefix and finalizer through real Harmony with a later finalizer that throws.

**Overlap census (2026-10-08).** All 242 assemblies under the installed Modules folder (TAOM's own, `0Harmony`, `System.*` and `Microsoft.*` skipped) were scanned for both a Harmony reference and the string `AfterStart`, as UTF-8 metadata and as UTF-16 string literals, which is where an attribute argument, a `nameof` or a reflection lookup of the method name would sit. Two matched, Bannerlord Coop's `GameInterface.dll` and `Missions.dll`, and a decompile of both shows only `MissionBehavior.AfterStart` overrides (`CoopBattleController`, `CoopBattleMissionSpawnHandler`, `CoopHideoutAmbushController`, `CoopHideoutMissionLogic`, `CoopTournamentBehavior`, `CoopTournamentMissionView`), no patch on `Mission.AfterStart`. So no other installed module patches the method, and no other transpiler can lose its anchor to the six `callvirt` to `call` swaps. A mod installed later that transpiled the method would meet a `call` to a TAOM helper where the engine has six `callvirt`.

**Co-op.** No bool prefix, so `CoopVetoClassificationTests` (which scans bool prefixes) has no entry to take. Classified here as reviewed safe: the patch changes nothing unless a start call throws, which otherwise loops the mission on that peer alone, and the toggle (`Survive Mission Start Failures`) is listed as instrumentation in `CoopSettingsRelevance`.

Threads: `Mission.AfterStart` and every call it makes run on the main thread. `MissionStartGuardBindingTests` pins the target, the six call signatures and counts, the rewrite on the real IL (six swaps, idempotent) and the parameter names; the real class applied to the real method builds a replacement the JIT accepts. `MissionStartGuardServiceTests`, `MissionStartGuardCallsTests` and `MissionStartGuardHookTests` cover the decisions, budgets, lines, the helpers and the prefix and finalizer. Feature: [mission-start-guard.md](../features/mission-start-guard.md). **ACTIVE**, not yet smoked in game (CrashReport's "Throw On Next Mission AfterStart" is the check).

## Patch104_NameplateCull

**Target:** `public void SandBox.ViewModelCollection.Nameplate.SettlementNameplatesVM.Update()` (v1.5.4; non-virtual, no parameters), one class `SettlementNameplatesVM_Update_NameplateCull_Patch` in `Main/Features/NameplateCull/Hooks/`, category `Patch104_NameplateCull`: one bool prefix (`SettlementNameplatesVM __instance`), no postfix, no finalizer.

Settlement nameplate cull (2026-10-08, adapted from yotthani's VanillaTuning `nameplate-cull`, MIT). Vanilla `Update` reads the map camera, stores it in `_cachedCameraPosition`, runs `UpdateNameplateMT(camera)` for every plate in a `TWParallel.For` (default grain size 16), then `RefreshBindValues()` for every plate in list order. TAOM_Map has 1,040 settlements (a hideout gets a plate only once it is visible) and most plates are hidden, so most of that work changes nothing a widget shows. The prefix hands the frame to `NameplateCullService.TryUpdate`, which has `NameplateCullAdapter.UpdateCulled` do the same three steps over only the plates `NameplateCullRule.MaySkip` does not clear, and returns false so the engine's loop does not run a second time. The plate list is reused, the three private members are `AccessTools.FieldRefAccess` delegates built once at install (`_mapCamera` on the view model, `_bindIsVisibleOnMap` and `_worldPos` on the plate), and the per-frame path allocates nothing. The cull does not store `_cachedCameraPosition`: only `UpdateNameplateAuxMT` reads it and only `Update` reaches that method through its predicate (`NameplateCullBindingTests.CachedCameraPosition_IsReadOnlyInUpdateNameplateAuxMT_...` reads the IL), so the camera goes to the parallel loop through the adapter's own field.

A plate is skipped only when every one of these holds: its pushed `IsVisibleOnMap` and its private `_bindIsVisibleOnMap` are false; its pushed `Position` is already the parked (-1000, -1000); `IsTracked`, `IsInRange` and `IsTargetedByTutorial` are false; the game's own height-and-distance test cannot show it (camera z above 400 shows towns only, above 200 towns and castles, otherwise distance below z + 100, the distance half of `SettlementNameplateVM.IsVisible`, constants pinned against the IL; a non-finite input answers "can be visible"); its settlement's range state is false (`IsVisible` for a hideout, `IsInspected` otherwise, as `RefreshDynamicProperties` computes it); its party is not `IsVisualDirty`; and `VisualTrackerManager.CheckTracked` does not hold it. Two of those are TAOM's additions to the upstream rule. (1) Parked: `Initialize` builds every plate with Position (0, 0) and every pushed property at its default, and only its first update parks it; upstream would skip such a plate from frame 1 and leave its widget at (0, 0) for good, so only a plate already parked is skippable and every plate's first update runs. (2) `IsTargetedByTutorial`: `IsTracked` reads the raw `_bindIsTargetedByTutorial` field, but the widget reads the pushed property, and a skipped plate never pushes it.

**What a skipped plate does not do.** `UpdateNameplateMT` is `CalculatePosition`, `DetermineIsInsideWindow`, `DetermineIsVisibleOnMap`, `RefreshPosition`, `RefreshDynamicProperties` and nothing else (`NameplateCullBindingTests` reads the installed IL, so a new step fails a test). For a plate that meets every condition above those are bookkeeping for a plate that stays hidden (screen position, window test, distance, `_bindWPos`, `_bindWSign`, `_bindIsInside`), or guarded by a condition (name re-read: `IsVisualDirty`; `_bindIsTracked`: `CheckTracked`; `_bindIsInRange`: the settlement's range; faction colour, banner, port and ferry are read in `RefreshDynamicProperties` only when `_bindIsVisibleOnMap` is true). `RefreshBindValues` copies the bind fields to the pushed properties, so a skipped plate keeps its last pushed values. The frame the rule lets a plate through, `UpdateNameplateMT` and `RefreshBindValues` both run for it, before the widget can read it, so a plate that becomes visible is complete within one frame of vanilla: the widget's visibility test reads the pushed `WPos`, which a skipped plate last pushed on its last unskipped frame.

**Callers of the target.** One: `GauntletMapSettlementNameplateView.OnMapScreenUpdate` (`SandBox.GauntletUI`), reached once per map frame from `MapScreen.OnFrameTick` through every `MapView.OnMapScreenUpdate` (`MapScreen.cs:1366-1369`), on the main thread. A full-assembly decompile of `SandBox.GauntletUI`, `SandBox.View` and `SandBox.ViewModelCollection` found no other caller; other installed modules were not decompiled (UNVERIFIED for them). The same method then reads each plate's `IsInside` and `IsVisibleOnMap`, which agree with the skip.

**Event registration.** `RefreshBindValues` ticks `SettlementNotifications` and `SettlementEvents` only when their `IsEventsRegistered` is true. Both, and `SettlementParties`, register only from the `IsInRange` setter (`SettlementNameplateVM.cs:344-358`) and unload when it turns false or in `OnFinalize`; an IL scan of the whole assembly finds no other caller of any of the three `RegisterEvents` (`NameplateCullBindingTests.IsInRangeSetter_IsTheOnlyPlaceAPlateRegistersItsEvents_InTheNameplateAssembly`). A plate with pushed `IsInRange` false has therefore nothing registered and no tick to lose, and a plate whose settlement just came in range is not skipped (the rule reads the settlement's own state), so registration is not delayed.

**Install timing (GameInit).** `Update` reads the instance fields `_mapCamera`, `_cachedCameraPosition`, `UpdateNameplateAuxMTPredicate` and `_allNameplates`, `Camera.Position`, `TWParallel.For` and `SettlementNameplateVM.RefreshBindValues`; no static of campaign, mission or MCM state. `TWParallel` has two static fields (`_parallelDriver = new DefaultParallelDriver()` and `_mainThreadId`) and the engine initialises it at start-up. `NameplateCullHookTests.PatchClass_AppliedToTheInstalledEngine_BuildsAReplacementTheJitAccepts` applies the real class to the real method and JIT-compiles the result in a test host with no campaign. yotthani installed at the first map frame because that patch compiles the method; TAOM found no static that needs that wait. Applied at GameInit through `NameplateCullModule`, whose `OnPhase` then binds the engine members (`NameplateCullAdapter.Initialize` builds the delegates and JIT-compiles its own three methods, so a member the build lost fails there, not at the first frame) and writes the install line. Not yet checked in game.

**Error path.** Toggle off, adapter not bound, or cull switched off after an error: the prefix returns true and the whole vanilla body runs. If the culled update throws, the service logs one `[NameplateCull] OFF after an error` line with the exception, switches the cull off for the rest of this game launch (the service is a process singleton) and the prefix returns true, so vanilla re-runs the whole update that frame. That re-run repeats work the cull may have half done (some plates updated, the camera stored); it is safe because the update is idempotent per frame (every value is recomputed from the camera and the settlements, then pushed), and if the fault is the engine's own (inside `UpdateNameplateMT` or `RefreshBindValues`) vanilla's run raises the same exception it would with the cull off, so nothing is hidden. The prefix itself catches everything and never returns false after a throw.

**TAOM interplay.** Patch38 (`SettlementNameplateWidget.DetermineTargetAlphaValue` postfix) runs only inside the widget's `if (IsVisibleOnMap)` branch, so it reads `DistanceToCamera` only for plates the cull updated this frame; `SettlementNameplateManagerWidget.OnRender` sorts only the `IsVisibleOnMap` plates by `DistanceToCamera`, so a skipped plate's stale distance orders nothing. The relation palette (`TaomSettlementPlateWidget`, `SettlementPlatePresenter`) is widget-side: it reads the pushed `Relation`, which a hidden plate receives in its first non-skipped frame (`RefreshRelationStatus` sets `_bindRelation` at once; the push waits for `RefreshBindValues`). Realm Borders and `RealmMapAdapter` read no nameplate view model (the relation is computed from the player's faction). Other installed modules: Bannerlord.Diplomacy adds a UIExtenderEx mixin to `SettlementNameplatesVM` (it hooks `RefreshValues` and `OnFinalize`, not `Update`); Bannerlord Coop's `GameInterface.dll` reads `GetNameplateOfSettlement` and patches the `SettlementNameplateVM` constructor for village raids, not `Update` (decompiled `GameInterface.dll` only; the rest of Coop is UNVERIFIED). No installed module that was checked patches `Update`.

**PatchShield.** `Update` is on neither exclusion list (once per map frame, main thread; decision D13), so the shield's finalizer attaches to it from a process's second game start (TAOM's GameInit batch is patched after pass 2, so in the first game the method is unshielded). The finalizer swallows a missing-API throw from the original and strips the unprotected owners' patches on the method; TAOM's owner id is not on the protected list (FOLLOW-UP L1 in the PatchShield review record), so it would strip this prefix too, and vanilla then runs, which is the safe outcome here. `NameplateCullBindingTests.PatchShield_StillWrapsTheUpdate_ByNameAndByNamespace` pins that it stays shielded.

**Co-op.** A bool prefix, so `CoopVetoClassificationTests` carries an entry: ReviewedSafe, client-side UI that mutates no campaign state. The toggle is classified as presentation in `CoopSettingsRelevance`.

A second prefix from another mod runs only on frames the cull did not handle: Harmony 2.4.2 skips the remaining bool prefixes (and any prefix taking a by-ref, out or reference-type argument other than `__instance`, `__originalMethod` or `__state`) when a bool prefix returns false; a void observer prefix still runs. The cull's prefix takes no `__runOriginal` (`NameplateCullPrefixOrderTests` pins the rule). The window line comes every 18,000 culled frames: toggle-off frames, battles and screens over the map never reach it, while a game menu, a map conversation or the paused map keep the update running and count. The install line says ON only when `Harmony.GetPatchInfo` lists the Patch104 prefix on `Update`, because `ModuleRunner` calls `OnPhase` whether or not the category applied.

Threads: the prefix, `UpdateCulled` and `RefreshBindValues` run on the main thread; `UpdateNameplateMT` runs on the `TWParallel` pool as in vanilla, reading only the reused list and the camera position, both written before the loop starts. `NameplateCullRuleTests`, `NameplateCullServiceTests`, `NameplateCullLinesTests`, `NameplateCullHookTests` and `NameplateCullModuleTests` cover the rule, the service, the lines, the prefix and the wiring; `NameplateCullBindingTests` pins every engine member read or called. Feature: [nameplate-cull.md](../features/nameplate-cull.md). **ACTIVE**, not yet measured or smoked in game (A/B with the map profiler on, see the feature doc).

## Patch105_HiddenHosts

**Targets (15 methods in 10 classes, all in `Main/Features/HiddenHosts/Hooks/`, category `Patch105_HiddenHosts`, GameInit):** the phase 1 pair `public virtual void SandBox.ViewModelCollection.Nameplate.PartyNameplateVM.RefreshBinding()` and `public override void SandBox.View.Map.Visuals.MobilePartyVisual.OnOpenEncyclopedia()` (both v1.5.5, no parameters), then the eleven tooltip targets of phase 2, the orders target of #848 and the field parley target of #1037, listed below. `patch-targets.md` lists one target per `TargetMethods` class (a limit of `tools/snapshot_api_surface.ps1`), so `HarmonyPatchBindingTests` is the gate for the rest. Phase 1 classes: `PartyNameplateVM_RefreshBinding_HiddenHosts_Patch` is a void prefix (`PartyNameplateVM __instance, out VanillaBinds __state`) plus a finalizer (`PartyNameplateVM __instance, VanillaBinds __state`); `MobilePartyVisual_OnOpenEncyclopedia_HiddenHosts_Patch` is a bool prefix (`MobilePartyVisual __instance`). No postfix on either.

Hidden Hosts, phase 1 (#846, 2026-10-18): another party's nameplate troop count blurs with its distance from the player's main party. Vanilla `RefreshBinding` copies the plate's bind fields to its bound properties once per map frame (`Count = _countBind; FullName = _fullNameBind; ExtraInfoText = _extraInfoTextBind`; v1.5.5 lines 765, 769, 773), and it is the only reader of those three fields (`HiddenHostsBindingTests` scans the whole assembly's IL). `RefreshDynamicProperties` decides its own writes from int caches (`_latestTotalCount`, the wounded and prisoner amounts) and a cached name object, never from the field text, and each property setter returns early on an equal value. So the prefix lays the mask over the three fields for that one call and returns vanilla's values as `__state`; the finalizer, which runs even when the original throws, puts them back. Nothing outlives the call: no per-plate table, nothing to lift when a party comes close or the toggle goes off. `HiddenHostsService.Evaluate` decides the mask from a `PartyIntelSnapshot` of raw facts (distance over the main party's seeing range against per-digit thresholds, widened by Scouting skill and by a same-realm party); a masked plate also loses its wounded and prisoner text, and at the horizon (past nine tenths of the party's own spotting reach from `GetPartySpottingRatioForMainPartySeeingRange`; besiegers and raiders of a settlement the player can see stay visible farther out, so for them the band is wider) the count is "..." and the name "Unknown party". `_fullNameBind` is rewritten by vanilla when the name changes, on every forced refresh and when a pooled plate is reused (`_latestNameTextObject = null`), which is why the name needs the same per-call treatment as the count. The four reflected fields (`_countBind`, `_extraInfoTextBind`, `_fullNameBind`, and `_isVisibleOnMapBind`, read so a plate is masked on its first visible frame; the `IsVisibleOnMap` property lags the bind by a frame) are `AccessTools.FieldRefAccess` delegates bound once in static fields inside a try that returns null on a miss. The encyclopedia prefix returns false only when the service reports the party at its horizon. It guards a hotkey, not a click: `MapScreen` calls `OnOpenEncyclopedia` when game key 67 (`MapGoToEncylopedia`) is pressed with the cursor on a party (`MapScreen.cs:1649-1651`); the keyboard default is unbound and Left Option opens it on a controller, and a left click engages or escorts as in vanilla.

**Never written:** `MobileParty.IsInspected`, `_isInspected` and `IsInfoHidden` are saved engine fields; the adapter reads `IsInfoHidden` (a party the engine already hides is left to vanilla) and writes nothing. `TaomMapVisibilityModel` is untouched; the spotting ratio is read through `Campaign.Models.MapVisibilityModel`, cached per party for half a second of real time. The adapter keeps no campaign object between calls (`PartyIntelAdapterTests`).

**Thread:** both prefixes and the finalizer run on the main thread. `RefreshBinding` is called from `PartyNameplatesVM.Update` after `RefreshDynamicProperties` (which may run on `TWParallel` workers at 32 or more plates), so the prefix reads the bind fields after the workers finish. The encyclopedia prefix runs on the hotkey press.

**Fallback and fault budget:** the toggle off returns every hook at once with no work.
