A missing service or adapter, a plate that is not on the map, a missing engine field (the bind stays null; reported once) and any exception leave the plate as vanilla wrote it for that frame, because the prefix writes nothing until everything that can throw has run; the encyclopedia prefix returns true. A fault is logged once per hook and exception type. The 32nd fault (`HiddenHostsCalls.FaultBudget`) stops both hooks for the rest of the process (one "from now on" line): the nameplate hook then does no work and the encyclopedia prefix returns true.

**PatchShield:** neither target is on `PatchShieldPolicy`'s exclusion lists (`SandBox.ViewModelCollection.Nameplate` and `SandBox.View.Map.Visuals` are not hot-layer namespaces), so both are shielded from a process's second game start. A strip removes the prefix but not the finalizer, and an unset `__state` makes the finalizer a no-op, so no mask is left behind; vanilla then runs unmasked.

**Phase 2 (hover tooltips, 2026-10-19).** Six more patch classes, same category, all on the main thread: no bool prefix, so no co-op row.

- `TooltipRefresherCollection_Subject_HiddenHosts_Patch` (`TargetMethods`): prefix (`object[] args, out ScopeToken __state`), postfix (`PropertyBasedTooltipVM propertyBasedTooltipVM`) and finalizer (`ScopeToken __state`) on `RefreshMobilePartyTooltip`, `RefreshArmyTooltip`, `RefreshSettlementTooltip`, `RefreshMapEventTooltip` and `RefreshSiegeEventTooltip` (`public static void (PropertyBasedTooltipVM, object[])`, registered by `SandBoxViewSubModule.RegisterTooltipTypes` for `MobileParty`, `Army`, `Settlement`, `MapEvent`, `SiegeEvent`). Triggers: the tooltip view model's constructor, every key-toggle rebuild (`OnIsExtendedChanged`), from the map hover, the map tracker, the settlement and encounter menu rows, army management, conversations, the kingdom, clan and town management items and the encyclopedia. The prefix opens a `TooltipFrame` for `args[0]`; the postfix, inside the frame, renames the title row (row 0, `Title` flag) of a party or army at the horizon, rewrites a settlement's defenders row, and (phase 3) appends a reason row, "Counted ... ago", when the total row actually showed a remembered "~" count (`TooltipFrame.ShowedMemory`); the finalizer closes the frame with no toggle or budget gate.
- `PropertyBasedTooltipVM_OnPeriodicRefresh_HiddenHosts_Patch`: prefix (`PropertyBasedTooltipVM __instance, out ScopeToken __state`) and finalizer on `protected override void PropertyBasedTooltipVM.OnPeriodicRefresh()`. Vanilla evaluates the troop count rows' functions here every two seconds, outside the builders, driven by `TooltipBaseVM.Tick` from the UI tick; the prefix reads `TooltipBaseVM._invokedType` and `_invokedArgs` (two `AccessTools.FieldRefAccess` delegates, bound once in a try that returns null; see `reflection-sites.md`) and opens a fresh frame for a subject of one of the five types.
- `TooltipRefresherCollection_AddPartyTroopProperties_HiddenHosts_Patch`: prefix (`out TrimToken __state`) and postfix (`PropertyBasedTooltipVM propertyBasedTooltipVM, TextObject title, TrimToken __state`) on the private `AddPartyTroopProperties(PropertyBasedTooltipVM, TroopRoster, TextObject title, bool isInspected, Func<TroopRoster>)`. While the frame is masked or at the horizon the postfix removes every row after the title this call added (rows are only appended; the token carries `IsSet`, so a stripped prefix trims nothing). Phase 3: for a masked party or army frame (with or without a remembered count yet), and only when `title.ToString()` equals the game text `str_map_tooltip_troops` (the id both builders pass, pinned), the postfix replaces the title row (index start + 1) with a `TooltipProperty` of the same label, height, flags and extended settings whose function sets `TooltipFrame.InTotal` while vanilla's original row function runs (cleared in a finally). Public API only; the original row keeps vanilla's function. A stripped postfix leaves vanilla's row.
- `TooltipRefresherCollection_AddEncounterParties_HiddenHosts_Patch` (`TargetMethods`, both private overloads): prefix with `ref bool isExtended`, set false while masked, so the per-troop rows are not built.
- `PartyBaseHelper_GetPartySizeText_HiddenHosts_Patch`: postfix on `Helpers.PartyBaseHelper.GetPartySizeText(int healtyNumber, int woundedNumber, bool isInspected)` (pinned by parameter types: a `PartyBase` overload exists). Reads the open frame; vanilla's own `isInspected == false` fog is left alone. Phase 3: while the frame's `InTotal` flag is set (the wrapped total row) it prints the verdict's remembered "~" count when there is one.
- `CampaignUIHelper_GetPartyNameplateText_HiddenHosts_Patch`: postfix on `CampaignUIHelper.GetPartyNameplateText(MobileParty party, bool includeAttachedParties)` (`Postfix(MobileParty party, bool includeAttachedParties, ref string __result)`); acts only inside a settlement frame. Callers (v1.5.5): the settlement tooltip passes `false` (the party's own roster), the army menu overlay has no frame. The mask follows the number vanilla printed: the own roster when `includeAttachedParties` is false, the army total only when true.

**Fault and toggle (phase 2).** The prefixes open nothing, and the readers keep vanilla text, with the toggle off or the fault budget (shared with the nameplate hook) spent. A fault in a prefix leaves no frame; in a reader it keeps vanilla's value for that call. The finalizers close any open token regardless. **PatchShield (phase 2).** None of the targets is on `PatchShieldPolicy`'s exclusion lists (`TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.ViewModelCollection`, `TaleWorlds.Core.ViewModelCollection` and `Helpers` are not hot-layer namespaces), so all are shielded from a process's second game start. A strip removes the prefix and not the finalizer; the unset `__state` makes the finalizer a no-op, so no frame stays open and the postfix finds none. `HiddenHostsTooltipHarmonyPairTests` runs the strip, a later finalizer that throws, and nesting through real Harmony.

**Phase 3 (memory, 2026-10-19).** No new patch class and no new reflection site: the memory is a campaign behavior (`HiddenHostsMemoryBehavior`, events `TickEvent`, `OnBeforeSaveEvent`, `HourlyTickEvent`, `PartyVisibilityChangedEvent`, `MobilePartyDestroyed`) that ages and forgets rows the nameplate and tooltip hooks record and read, and a second one (`HiddenHostsPlainSightBehavior`, events `GameMenuOpened` and `TickEvent`) that stamps what is in plain sight (`TickEvent` never fires while a plain game menu stops time, so the menu event is what stamps a town or encounter visit). The memory behavior's events are `TickEvent` and `OnBeforeSaveEvent` (the Scouting XP grant), `HourlyTickEvent`, `PartyVisibilityChangedEvent` and `MobilePartyDestroyed`. The hooks' changes are the T7 wrapper, the T10 `InTotal` branch, the reason row in the T1 to T5 postfix and the `includeAttachedParties` parameter of T11, all described above; none is a bool prefix, so no co-op row. Triggers: the visibility event is raised by `MobileParty.IsVisible`'s setter (only on a change) through `PartyBase.OnVisibilityChanged`, from `CampaignTickCacheDataStore.RealTick`'s sequential loop after the parallel passes; `MobilePartyDestroyed` by `DestroyPartyAction.ApplyInternal` before `RemoveParty`; `HiddenHostsBindingTests`-style pins are in `HiddenHostsMemoryBindingTests`. The XP grant is gated on `ICoopSessionProvider.IsAuthority` and `CoopSessionPolicy.MayWriteSaveBackedState`. Fault behavior: a handler that throws logs once per kind and carries on; the reason row's function catches its own faults and returns a blank row. The troop-total wrapper does not catch: `ReadTotal` is a try/finally that clears the frame flag and lets the exception propagate, the same as vanilla's own row function would: at build the wrapper's constructor runs inside T7's postfix try, so T7 catches and reports it; at a refresh it runs from `TooltipBaseVM.Tick`, which has no catch, and nothing above the view is verified to catch. A masked party or army total is always wrapped (with or without a memory yet), so a memory that appears while the tooltip is open shows at the next refresh; the reason row and the drawn flag follow what actually reached the screen (`TooltipFrame.ShowedMemory`), not the verdict alone. The frame slot is `[ThreadStatic]`.

**Far Watch (#847, 2026-10-20).** No new patch class, target, parameter or reflection site. Three decisions the existing patches read changed, all inside the service they already call: the nameplate prefix passes `"Unknown party"` only when `NameplateIntel.HidesName` (the horizon and no scout named the party) and `VanillaBinds.LayMask` swaps the name on that same flag; the encyclopedia bool prefix returns false only when `HiddenHostsService.IsNameHidden` (the same predicate); the subject postfix's title rename skips a frame whose verdict is `Named`. The prefix and finalizer pair still links through `__state` alone; `HiddenHostsHarmonyPairTests` runs the named-horizon plate through real Harmony, with the prefix stripped as well. The scout seam is read once per horizon evaluation (`IsNamedByScout`, a scan of at most six ids, no allocation), so the per-plate cost off the horizon is one bool read for `IsScoutAway`. See [far-watch.md](../features/far-watch.md).

**Hidden party orders (#848, 2026-10-20).** One more patch class, same category: `MobileParty_GetBehaviorText_HiddenHosts_Patch`, a postfix on `public TextObject MobileParty.GetBehaviorText()` (v1.5.5, instance, non-virtual, no overloads), binding only `ref TextObject __result`. Inside an open party or army frame whose verdict is not `Vanilla` (`TooltipFrame.HidesOrders`: masked, horizon, a remembered "~" count or a scout-named host) it sets `__result` to `{=taom_hhost_orders_unknown}Orders unknown.`; with no frame, another thread or the fault budget spent it returns at once (one `[ThreadStatic]` read, no allocation), and a fault keeps vanilla's text. It never evaluates the party and writes no state. Callers (v1.5.5, pinned from the IL in `HiddenHostsTooltipBindingTests`): `CampaignUIHelper.GetMobilePartyBehaviorText` (a 12-byte wrapper over this method) from `RefreshMobilePartyTooltip`, `RefreshArmyTooltip` (inside the Patch105 frame, as a plain-string row) and `GameMenuPartyItemVM` (no frame), and `CampaignUIHelper`'s town patrol tooltip (no frame); `SandBox.ViewModelCollection` calls neither. Patching the inner method rather than the wrapper removes the wrapper's JIT inlining question. Postfix, not a bool prefix, so no `CoopVetoClassificationTests` row. **PatchShield:** the target is on neither of `PatchShieldPolicy`'s exclusion lists (`ExcludedTargetNamespacePrefixes`: `TaleWorlds.GauntletUI`, `TaleWorlds.TwoDimension`, `TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade.GauntletUI`, `ManagedCallbacks`; `ExcludedTargetMethods`: three `Formation` members, three `Agent` members, `Mission.CanAgentRout`, `Mission.TickAgentsAndTeamsImp`, `Mission.WaitTickCompletion`, `ManagedScriptHolder.TickComponents`, `Campaign.Tick`, `CampaignEvents.Tick`, `GameMenuVM.OnFrameTick`, `MBObjectManager.LoadXML` and `CreateMergedXmlFile`, and five `CampaignEventDispatcher` session events), so it gains the value-returning finalizer from a process's second game start: Harmony 2.4.2 stores vanilla's `TextObject` in the result local before any postfix runs and returns it after the shield swallows a fault, so a missing-API fault in this postfix (its text call is direct, inside its try) keeps vanilla's orders; only a fault raised before that store (another owner's prefix or transpiler) returns null, and the wrapper's `ToString()` would then throw in the tooltip rows. `HiddenHostsTooltipHarmonyPairTests` runs it through real Harmony on a bare `MobileParty`, and the wrapper compiled before and after the patch.

**Field parley (#1037, 2026-10-27).** One more patch class, same category: `DefaultEncounterModel_CanMainHeroDoParleyWithParty_HiddenHosts_Patch`, a postfix on `public override bool TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.GameComponents.DefaultEncounterModel.CanMainHeroDoParleyWithParty(PartyBase partyBase, out TextObject explanation)` (v1.5.6; the abstract is on `EncounterModel`), binding `PartyBase partyBase, ref bool __result, ref TextObject explanation`. It refuses a mobile party that `HiddenHostsService.MasksParley` (the name verdict) names: `__result` becomes false and `explanation` becomes null, dropping vanilla's text, which names the party (the click then prints vanilla's nameless "Cannot parley with this party."). The verdict also needs the party drawn on the map (`IsVisible`). Callers (v1.5.6): `TooltipRefresherCollection.AddTooltipHintProperties`, the party and settlement `CanParley` flags in `SandBox.GauntletUI` and `MapScreen.TryParleyWith`. A refusal with no text, a settlement, the toggle off, a fault or the spent budget keep vanilla's answer. It brings the category to 15 methods in 10 classes. No `CoopVetoClassificationTests` row (it is a postfix, so a row would be stale).

**Co-op:** the encyclopedia bool prefix carries a `CoopVetoClassificationTests` entry (ReviewedSafe, client-side UI that mutates no campaign state); the toggle is presentation in `CoopSettingsRelevance`. Feature: [hidden-hosts.md](../features/hidden-hosts.md). **ACTIVE**, not yet checked in game.
## Patch106_BondsOfBattle

**Target:** `public bool PartyScreenLogic.DoneLogic(bool isForced)` (v1.5.5; non-virtual, public), one class `PartyScreenLogic_DoneLogic_Patch` in `Main/Features/BondsOfBattle/Hooks/`, category `Patch106_BondsOfBattle`: a void prefix (`PartyScreenLogic __instance`, `out HandoverCapture __state`) and a void postfix (`bool __result`, `HandoverCapture __state`); no finalizer, no skip.

Troop hand-over (2026-10-18, [bonds-of-battle.md](../features/bonds-of-battle.md)). On success `DoneLogic` pays `CurrentData.PartyInfluenceChangeAmount.Item2` through `GainKingdomInfluenceAction.ApplyForLeavingTroopToGarrison` and then calls `SetInfluenceChangeAmount(0, 0, 0)`, so the prefix copies the left party's leader (id, name, and whether he is the player or of the player's clan) and that number into Harmony's `__state`, and the postfix hands them to `TroopHandoverService.OnHandover` only when the call returned true. A failed Done (not enough gold, a refusing delegate) leaves the influence in place and grants nothing; a second Done after success reads 0. The service decides what the two flags mean.

**Callers and actors.** The influence is non-zero only on a donating screen (`PartyState.IsDonating`): Donate Troops to another clan's party (`PartyScreenHelper.OpenScreenAsDonateTroops(MobileParty leftParty)`, donating when `leftParty.Owner.Clan != Clan.PlayerClan`), or donate to a garrison (`OpenScreenAsDonateGarrisonWithCurrentSettlement`). Only Donate Troops has a left owner party, the receiving party's own (`CreateBasicInitDataWithMainPartyAndOther` sets `LeftOwnerParty = party.Party`); the garrison screen builds its data with `CreateBasicInitDataWithMainParty` and no left owner party, so it captures no lord. VERIFIED (deep review, 2026-10-18): the only caller of `OpenScreenAsDonateTroops` is `GameMenuOverlay.ExecuteTroopAction`. The patch infers no actor: the lord is the screen's own left party's leader, and the capture returns the default (do nothing) for a null leader.

**State.** Harmony's `__state` is one zeroed value per call, so a stripped prefix leaves the default (no lord) and the postfix does nothing. Both halves catch and log their own faults, so a relation bonus can never keep a party screen from closing.

**Install timing.** GameInit, through `BondsOfBattleModule`. The target is a campaign party screen, nothing renders it before a campaign starts, and the patch's parameter types have no Mission or Campaign dependent static initializer. Not on `PatchShieldPolicy.ExcludedTargetMethods`; the method runs once per party screen confirm, on the main thread.

**Overlap and co-op (2026-10-18).** No other TAOM code patches `PartyScreenLogic.DoneLogic`. The installed co-op module replaces `DoneLogic` with its own bool prefix, so in a co-op session the capture may read 0 and nothing is granted. `TroopHandoverService.OnHandover` also returns false when `ICoopSessionProvider.ShouldDeferToHost`. The patch has no bool prefix, so `CoopVetoClassificationTests` (which scans bool prefixes) has no entry to take. The dedicated-server rule lives in `RelationGrantService`.

Tests: `TroopHandoverServiceTests` covers the gain and every gate (feature, switch, enlisted, co-op, player clan, player hero); `BondsOfBattleWiringTests` pins the category, the target, the parameter names and the fault handling of both halves; `BondsOfBattleBehaviorTests` covers the sibling battle handler; `HarmonyPatchBindingTests` resolves the target. Feature: [bonds-of-battle.md](../features/bonds-of-battle.md). **ACTIVE**, not yet smoked in game.

## Patch107_PersonalRelationNotice

**Target:** `private void SandBox.CampaignBehaviors.DefaultNotificationsCampaignBehavior.OnRelationChanged(Hero effectiveHero, Hero effectiveHeroGainedRelationWith, int relationChange, bool showNotification, ChangeRelationDetail detail, Hero originalHero, Hero originalGainedRelationWith)` (v1.5.5), resolved by name (`AccessTools`), one class `DefaultNotificationsCampaignBehavior_OnRelationChanged_Patch` in `Main/Features/Relations/Hooks/`, category `Patch107_PersonalRelationNotice`: one bool prefix taking the first four parameters by name, no postfix, no finalizer.

Relation notice that names the lord (2026-10-19, [personal-relations.md](../features/personal-relations.md)). The handler is a `HeroRelationChanged` listener. For a change that involves the player it shows `str_your_relation_increased_with_notable` / `_decreased_` (which names the hero) only when the other hero has no clan or the player's clan; for every other clan it shows `str_your_relation_*_with_clan`, naming the clan. With personal relations on a change belongs to one lord, so the prefix replays vanilla's own "notable" branch for every hero (`StringHelpers.SetCharacterProperties("HERO", ...)`, VALUE = the lord's relation with the player, MAGNITUDE = the change's size, the notable portrait rule, `event:/ui/notification/relation`) through `IPersonalRelationNoticeAdapter` and returns false. It returns true, and vanilla runs untouched, when `showNotification` is false or the change is 0 (vanilla's own first test), when personal relations are off, when the adapter reports vanilla would show nothing (the change does not involve the player), and on any fault. No new strings.

**Callers of the target.** Two routes reach the handler. The `HeroRelationChanged` event it is registered on in `RegisterEvents`, raised by `ChangeRelationAction` (`ApplyInternal` and `ApplyInternalBySet`) on the main thread; and the installed co-op module, which has a postfix of its own and a direct client call (both verified in the convergence pass of the #862 review). In both cases the prefix first resolves its collaborators from the container, once per process and cached in statics, before `IsActive` is read; after that it reads only its arguments, then asks `IPersonalRelationService.IsActive` (a volatile bool and the cached switch) before touching the engine. A co-op session is never active (the seeding behavior disarms the service there), so the prefix returns true and the co-op module's direct call reaches vanilla's handler untouched.

**Install timing.** GameInit, through `RelationsModule`. The target is a campaign behavior and nothing renders it before a campaign starts. The listener delegate is built per campaign and points at the patched method, so applying before or after the behavior registers is the same. Not on `PatchShieldPolicy.ExcludedTargetMethods`; the event fires per relation change, not per frame.

**Overlap and co-op (2026-10-19).** No other TAOM code patches the handler or names it. The installed co-op module's postfix and its direct client call (above) both reach the original handler: they are untouched because the service is disarmed in a co-op session. The prefix is a bool prefix that may skip the original: it only ever replaces a message with a message, and it changes no state, so it is classed ReviewedSafe in `CoopVetoClassificationTests`. A fault in the prefix is caught there and returns true.

Tests: `PersonalRelationNoticeTests` (the gate: inactive means vanilla runs, an unrelated change, the container missing), `PersonalRelationNoticeBindingTests` (the seven-parameter signature and the four bound names), `RelationsWiringTests` (the category and phase), `HarmonyPatchBindingTests` (target resolution). Feature: [personal-relations.md](../features/personal-relations.md). **ACTIVE**, not yet smoked in game.

## Patch108_TaomBuildings

**Target:** two classes in `Main/Features/TaomBuildings/Hooks/Patch108_TaomBuildings.cs`, category `Patch108_TaomBuildings`: `Patch108_DevelopmentSprite`, a postfix on the private `DevelopmentItemVisualButtonWidget.DetermineSpriteImageFromSpriteCode(string spriteCode, bool useSmallVariant)` returning `string`, taking `__instance`, both parameters and `ref string __result`; `Patch108_LevelExplanation`, a postfix on the public `BuildingType.GetExplanationAtLevel(int level)` returning `TextObject`, taking `__instance`, `level` and `ref TextObject __result`.

TAOM building dressing (#855, 2026-10-18). The sprite method maps a building type id to a sprite name and returns "" for an id it does not know, which the widget (`OnLateUpdate`) turns into `building_default`; it appends `_t` for the small variant inside the method. The postfix changes only an empty result for a TAOM id, asks `ITaomBuildingUiHook.SpriteFor` for the name and sets it only when `UIContext.SpriteData.GetSprite(name)` is not null (`GetSprite` returns null for an unknown name), so art that is not baked yet leaves the default icon instead of a blank one. The explanation method builds the per-level line from the type's `BuildingEffectEnum` list, which a TAOM building leaves empty; the postfix supplies `ITaomBuildingUiHook.LevelText` for a TAOM type at level 1 to 3 and leaves every other result alone. Each body is in a try that leaves the result untouched and logs one `[TaomBuildings]` warning per class on the first fault (a `_warned` latch, the Patch96 shape), so a fault repeated on every town screen cannot flood the log. The hook is set by `TaomBuildingsModule.InitializeStatics` as a factory and resolved on first use, so a config read or duplicate-id throw cannot escape the fail-closed load step; a factory that throws is logged once and not retried, and the hook then stays null. Harmony binds the parameters by name.

**PatchShield:** neither target is on `PatchShieldPolicy.ExcludedTargetMethods`; the finalizer wraps both. The sprite method runs once per widget and sprite code on the main thread, `GetExplanationAtLevel` once per level line shown on the town screen. Applied at GameInit through `TaomBuildingsModule`. Not a bool prefix, so `CoopVetoClassificationTests` has no entry to take. Feature: [taom-buildings.md](../features/taom-buildings.md) (first user: [postern-gate.md](../features/postern-gate.md)). **ACTIVE**, not yet smoked in game.

## Patch109_TelcharsMark

**Lives in TAOM.Client** (`Client/Features/TelcharsMark/Hooks/`, ADR-013, B5). `PatchCategoryIndex.Build(ClientComposition.IsDedicatedServer(), ClientComposition.LoadedTaomAssemblies())` reads TAOM.dll, then TAOM.Client when it loaded, so `TryPatchCategory("Patch109_TelcharsMark")` applies it on a client and applies nothing on a dedicated server.

**Targets (all v1.5.5, all private, all Postfix, no prefix, no finalizer):** `SPInventoryVM.RefreshInformationValues()` (`SPInventoryVM.cs:3968`), `SPInventoryVM.AfterTransfer(InventoryLogic, List<TransferCommandResult>)` (`:3838`) and `SPInventoryVM.UpdateFilteredStatusOfItem(SPItemVM item)` (`:5133`). Three classes in `Client/Features/TelcharsMark/Hooks/`, category `Patch109_TelcharsMark`, each a few lines that call the screen's `UpgradeScreenMixin` and log through `TelcharsMarkLog` (first failure per source and exception type, repeats suppressed).

Inventory upgrade tint (see [telchars-mark.md](../features/telchars-mark.md)). The feature for one screen lives on `UpgradeScreenMixin`, which UIExtenderEx builds before the `SPInventoryVM` constructor returns and which runs the open-time rescore itself; during the constructor the mixin does not exist, so the postfixes find nothing. The two rescore postfixes call `UpgradeScreenMixin.Rescore`. The filter postfix runs after vanilla has set `IsFiltered` and sets it true for a list row vanilla left visible that has no verdict, when "show only better items" is on. The parameter must be named `item` (pinned by `Patch109WiringTests`). A private open delegate on the same method (`InventoryUpgradeScreen.cs`) re-runs the filters when the checkbox changes, so that call also reaches the postfix.

**Callers.** `RefreshInformationValues`: `InitializeInventory` (`:4533`), the `EquipmentMode` setter (`:1647`), `UpdateCurrentCharacterIfPossible` (`:4315`), the equip, unequip, buy, sell, slaughter, donate and transfer-all handlers (`:3482-3727`, `:4838`), since #917 Quick Actions' `InventoryVMAdapter.RefreshDisplay`, and since #924 EquipPresets' `InventoryScreenAdapter.RefreshActiveVM` (both through `InventoryVMAdapter.RefreshPanel`). `AfterTransfer`: registered on `InventoryLogic.AfterTransfer` (`:3087`), raised once per transfer command; the postfix returns while `IsRefreshed` is false, which every vanilla handler sets around its commands, so it rescores only for transfers no vanilla handler follows: EquipPresets loads, Quick Actions Unequip All and `InventoryLogic.Reset`'s empty transfer (`InventoryLogic.cs:734-739`). `UpdateFilteredStatusOfItem`: `AfterTransfer` (`:3888`), `InitializeInventory` (`:4520`, `:4528`), `ProcessFilter` (`:5120`, `:5127`) and `OnSearchTextChanged` (`:5154`). None carries an actor the patch infers.

**Timing.** The inventory screen opens long after `OnGameInitializationFinished`, so the category applies in the late batch beside `Patch34_QuickActions`. No parameter type has a Mission or Campaign dependent static initializer.

**Hot path.** The filter postfix runs once per row per filter pass: a row vanilla already hid costs one field read, the box off costs one weak-table lookup, and it allocates nothing. Filter passes include every rescore while the box is ticked, and a Quick Actions batch sell rescores once per sold stack (each sell runs vanilla's own handler); cost not yet measured in game.

**Threads.** Main thread: view model code reached from the inventory screen's construction and commands (screen tick and Gauntlet dispatch), with no Mission or asynchronous agent-tick caller in the list above.

**Number.** Patch105 was taken on trunk by Hidden Hosts, and other uncommitted batch branches claim 105 to 107, so this category took 109; re-check at every rebase. **ACTIVE**, not yet checked in game.

## Patch110_BardTownGuard

**Target:** `private bool SandBox.CampaignBehaviors.TavernEmployeesCampaignBehavior.conversation_talk_bard_on_condition()` (v1.5.5, no parameters, resolved by name with `AccessTools.Method`), one transpiler class `TavernEmployeesCampaignBehavior_BardCondition_BardTownGuard_Patch` in `Main/Features/BardTownGuard/Hooks/`, category `Patch110_BardTownGuard` (GameInit). #824, 2026-10-19.

Vanilla (`TavernEmployeesCampaignBehavior.cs:415-435`) reads `.Name` of `Settlement.All.GetRandomElementWithPredicate(town of the same culture, not the current one)`, which is null in a town whose culture owns no other town, so the condition throws. The transpiler rewrites that one call in place to `BardTownPickHook.PickTown` (same signature): the engine's pick first, then the same engine pick over every other town, else the current settlement. Exactly one engine call must match; any other count returns the original stream with one warning. The lyrics are not touched. `BardTownGuardBindingTests` (`BindingVerification`) pin the site against the installed SandBox.dll and the JIT-compiled replacement.

**Callers and thread:** the condition delegate registered by the behavior, evaluated by the conversation system at every conversation start that reaches the `talk_bard` line (`ConversationManager.GetSentenceOptions`), on the main thread; only a musician reaches the pick.

**PatchShield.** `SandBox.CampaignBehaviors` is on neither exclusion list (`PatchShieldPolicy.ExcludedTargetNamespacePrefixes` and `ExcludedTargetMethods`), so the shield's finalizer attaches to the condition from a process's second game start (TAOM's GameInit batch is patched after pass 2, so in the first game the method is unshielded), and does not install at all while a co-op module is active (`PatchShieldPolicy.ShouldInstall`). The finalizer swallows only a missing-API throw (`MissingMethodException`, `MissingFieldException`, `TypeLoadException`), in which case the condition returns false for that call, and strips the unprotected owners' patches on the method; TAOM's owner id `com.taom.mod` is not on the protected list (FOLLOW-UP L1 in the PatchShield review record), so the transpiler would be stripped too and vanilla then runs. Any other exception, a `NullReferenceException` included, is rethrown with its stack kept.

**Co-op.** A transpiler with no bool prefix, so `CoopVetoClassificationTests` needs no entry: the condition only builds conversation text and mutates no campaign state. Feature: [bard-town-guard.md](../features/bard-town-guard.md). **ACTIVE**, not yet checked in game.

## Patch111_TavernSpecialities

**Target:** `private bool SandBox.CampaignBehaviors.TavernEmployeesCampaignBehavior.conversation_tavernmaid_offers_food_on_condition()` (v1.5.5, no parameters, resolved by name with `AccessTools.Method`), one prefix class `TavernEmployeesCampaignBehavior_OffersFood_TavernSpecialities_Patch` in `Main/Features/TavernSpecialities/Hooks/`, category `Patch111_TavernSpecialities` (GameInit). #937, 2026-10-19.

Vanilla (`TavernEmployeesCampaignBehavior.cs:485-535`) sets the `DRINK` and `FOOD` text variables inside seven culture branches (`vlandia`, `empire`, `khuzait`, `aserai`, `sturgia`, `battania`, `nord`; each sets both) and nothing for any other culture, then returns true, so a TAOM town offers the previous town's dishes or the line with both dish names blank. The void prefix pre-sets `DRINK` to a mug of ale and `FOOD` to a bowl of stew before vanilla runs; vanilla's branches overwrite both for exactly their ids, so no culture list is kept here. Nothing else reads the two globals. The strings carry the keys `taom_tavern_drink_default` and `taom_tavern_food_default`, registered in the module strings and not yet translated. `TavernSpecialitiesBindingTests` (`BindingVerification`) pin that the target resolves, that the class is a prefix with no postfix, and that the category applies and JIT-compiles.

**Callers and thread:** the conversation condition delegate registered by the behavior, evaluated by the conversation system on the main thread at every conversation start that reaches the maid's offer line.

**PatchShield.** `SandBox.CampaignBehaviors` is on neither exclusion list (`PatchShieldPolicy.ExcludedTargetNamespacePrefixes` and `ExcludedTargetMethods`), so the shield's finalizer attaches to the condition from a process's second game start (TAOM's GameInit batch is patched after pass 2, so in the first game the method is unshielded), and does not install at all while a co-op module is active (`PatchShieldPolicy.ShouldInstall`). The finalizer swallows only a missing-API throw (`MissingMethodException`, `MissingFieldException`, `TypeLoadException`); a swallowed throw from the prefix skips vanilla, so that call returns false and the maid line is skipped once, and the shield then strips the unprotected owners' patches on the method. TAOM's owner id `com.taom.mod` is not on the protected list (FOLLOW-UP L1 in the PatchShield review record), so the prefix would be stripped too and vanilla then runs. Any other exception is rethrown with its stack kept.

**Co-op.** A void prefix, so `CoopVetoClassificationTests` needs no entry: it only sets conversation text variables and mutates no campaign state. Feature: [tavern-specialities.md](../features/tavern-specialities.md). **ACTIVE**, not yet checked in game.

## Patch112_CrownGuard

**Target:** `public static SceneNotificationData.SceneNotificationCharacter TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.SceneInformationPopupTypes.CampaignSceneNotificationHelper.GetBodyguardOfCulture(CultureObject culture)` (v1.5.5), one postfix class `CampaignSceneNotificationHelper_GetBodyguardOfCulture_CrownGuard_Patch` in `Main/Features/CrownGuard/Hooks/`, category `Patch112_CrownGuard` (GameInit). #825, 2026-10-20.

Vanilla switches on its own culture ids and returns `new SceneNotificationCharacter(MBObjectManager.Instance.GetObject<CharacterObject>(id))`, with `fighter_sturgia` for any other culture. The one caller a TAOM campaign reaches is `BecomeKingSceneNotificationItem`, which asks for two guards of the new ruler's kingdom culture; the engine's other, `EmpireConspiracySupportsSceneNotificationItemBase`, is raised only by Story Mode's `DefeatTheConspiracyQuestBehavior`, whose new game TAOM hides, and would get the same mapped guards. The postfix reads `culture.StringId` at the boundary, asks `CrownGuardMap.GuardTroopId` through `CrownGuardConfigProvider` for the mapped troop id (`crown_guard/crown_guard_cultures.json`, one row per kingdom culture), and replaces `__result` with a `SceneNotificationCharacter` of that troop carrying the crowned culture's `Color` and `Color2` (the scene otherwise tints a guard with its own troop culture's colours, which differ for the three borrowed rows). A culture with no row, or a mapped troop that is not loaded, keeps vanilla's result; the missing troop logs one warning per culture id. The provider is resolved lazily from IoC and cached. The scene's net for a visual that fails to build is Patch56_SceneNotificationVisualGuard.

**Callers and thread:** the become-king scene notification, which `DefaultCutscenesCampaignBehavior.OnKingdomDecisionConcluded` raises only when the player wins a king-selection vote and the dev command `taom.play_coronation` (cheat mode) raises on demand, which also calls the target once for its report; `GauntletSceneNotification.OpenScene` reads the characters on the main thread, from the global layer's `OnTick`.

**PatchShield.** `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.SceneInformationPopupTypes` is not an excluded namespace and `GetBodyguardOfCulture` is not on `ExcludedTargetMethods`, so the shield's finalizer attaches to the method from a process's second game start (TAOM's GameInit batch is patched after pass 2, so in the first game the method is unshielded), and does not install at all while a co-op module is active (`PatchShieldPolicy.ShouldInstall`). The finalizer swallows only a missing-API throw (`MissingMethodException`, `MissingFieldException`, `TypeLoadException`) and strips the unprotected owners' patches; TAOM's owner id `com.taom.mod` is not on the protected list (FOLLOW-UP L1 in the PatchShield review record), so the postfix would be stripped and vanilla's guard shown from then on. Any other exception is rethrown with its stack kept.

**Co-op.** A postfix on a pure lookup, no bool prefix and no campaign state written, so `CoopVetoClassificationTests` needs no entry. Feature: [crown-guard.md](../features/crown-guard.md). **ACTIVE**, not yet checked in game.

## Patch113_CoopRecruitAttribution

**Target:** `GameInterface.Services.TroopRosters.Interfaces.TroopRosterInterface.HandleOnRecruitmentDone(string mobilePartyId, TroopInfo[] troopsInCart)` in BannerlordCoop (internal class, v1.5.x source `TroopRosterInterface.cs:320-364`), resolved by reflection through `ReflectionUtils.FindTypeAcrossLoadedAssemblies`. One class `CoopRecruitAttributionPatch` in `Main/Features/CoopInterop/Hooks/`, category `Patch113_CoopRecruitAttribution` (GameInit). #966 (Batch B of #964), 2026-10-21. **ACTIVE**: applied in the GameInit batch at `Main/SubModule.cs:1866` (`TryPatchCategory("Patch113_CoopRecruitAttribution")`); not yet checked in game.

`HarmonyPrepare` returns true only when the Coop type and method resolve, so the patch is skipped without Coop; when Coop is active but the method is missing, `Prepare` logs one warning (`[CoopRecruitAttribution] Patch113 not applied`), since the patch would otherwise silently never apply. `HarmonyPatchBindingTests` lets this one patch pass on `Prepare() == false` through an explicit `ThirdPartyTargetPatches` set; every other patch still has to resolve. The prefix saves `RecruitAttributionScope.Current` into `__state` and sets it to the party id (`__0`); a void finalizer restores `__state`, which keeps the scope correct after a throw and leaves Harmony's rethrow and stack untouched. With a stripped prefix `__state` is null and the finalizer clears any outer scope; PatchShield is never installed while a co-op module is active, so this does not happen in practice. The scope is `[ThreadStatic]` and spans one synchronous call.

**Why:** `CampaignEvents.OnUnitRecruitedEvent` (`CharacterObject, int`) carries no party. Under Coop only the server raises it for a guest's recruitment, inside this method (`:359-360`), so `SpecialResourceEventAdapter.SnapshotRecruit` reads the scope (the behavior's `OnUnitRecruited` forwards it to `SpecialResourceEarningService.ChargeRecruit`) to charge the recruiting party's leader through `RecruitChargePolicy` instead of `Hero.MainHero`.

**Key prefix:** the string argument is Coop's object-manager key, `"MobileParty_" + StringId` (`AutoRegistryBase`), not the bare `StringId`. `CoopRecruitAttributionPatch.ToPartyStringId` strips the prefix (`CoopRecruitAttributionPatch.cs:48-51`; the prefix is `:59-64` and the finalizer `:66-70`) and `SpecialResourceEventAdapter.SnapshotRecruit` resolves the party with `CampaignObjectManager.Find<MobileParty>`.

**Callers and thread:** the only caller of the target is `ServerTroopRosterHandler.HandleOnRecruitmentDone` (`ServerTroopRosterHandler.cs:41-45`, a `ClientRequestRecruitment` message handler, so a guest's recruit request). It runs the call through `GameThread.Run`, so the target executes on the game thread, and the target raises `OnUnitRecruited` synchronously inside it; the `[ThreadStatic]` scope therefore spans exactly that call and no handler on another thread sees it. **Known limitations (#967):** the guest's client-side affordability gate (Patch51) reads a stale balance and the server-side charge floors at zero until balances are server-owned; Coop's prisoner and mercenary recruit paths never raise `OnUnitRecruited`, so their `recruit_cost` is never charged under co-op, the hosting player included. Feature: [coop-interop.md](../features/coop-interop.md).

**Co-op.** A pure bracket: no skip, no campaign state written, so `CoopVetoClassificationTests` needs no entry. **Tests:** `CoopRecruitAttributionPatchTests` (real Harmony on a local fake), `RecruitChargePolicyTests`.

## Patch114_AlignmentRecruitmentScoring

**Target:** `private (int, float) TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors.AiBehaviors.AiVisitSettlementBehavior.GetApproximateVolunteersCanBeRecruitedDataFromSettlement(Hero hero, Settlement settlement)` (v1.5.5, `AiVisitSettlementBehavior.cs:396-425`), one postfix class `Patch114_AiVolunteerEstimate_Postfix` in `Main/Features/AlignmentRecruitment/Hooks/`, category `Patch114_AlignmentRecruitmentScoring` (ProcessLoad, declared by `AlignmentRecruitmentModule`). #887, 2026-10-23.

Vanilla counts filled volunteer slots (4 own faction, 2 foreign) with no alignment check, and the result feeds the AI lord's recruiting score. `TaomVolunteerModel` blocks the actual recruit across the Free/Evil divide, so a lord could plan a trip that yields nothing. The postfix returns when the service is null, the count is 0 or an argument is null, then skips a settlement of the lord's own map faction (the same kingdom on both sides, or no kingdom, which resolves Neutral: never blocked; most of the calls); otherwise it asks `IRecruitmentAlignmentService.IsRecruitmentBlocked` with the recruiter's `Clan.Kingdom.StringId`, the settlement's `MapFaction.StringId` and `hero == Hero.MainHero`, and sets `__result = (0, 0f)` on a block. A throw is caught and logged once.

**Callers and thread:** `AiHourlyTick` :294, once per candidate settlement passing the gates at :269 and :292, for every AI lord party; only foreign candidates (a kingdom lord far from home scanning towns and villages within half a day, a landless kingdom's fallback villages) can be zeroed; own-faction candidates return after one reference compare. Main thread. Food and trade scoring for the same settlement is separate and unchanged.

**PatchShield.** `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors.AiBehaviors` is not an excluded namespace and the estimate is not on `ExcludedTargetMethods` (`Dependencies/Foundation/PatchShieldPolicy.cs`), so the shield's finalizer wraps the method from a process's first game start (the category applies at OnSubModuleLoad, before PatchShield's pass 2); it does not install while a co-op module is active (`PatchShieldPolicy.ShouldInstall`). The postfix catches what its body throws at run time. A missing-API failure in JIT-compiling the postfix after an engine update reaches the finalizer, and the target returns vanilla's estimate (stored before the postfix runs). A missing-API failure in vanilla's own body returns the default `(0, 0f)`.

**Co-op.** A void postfix on a pure estimate, no skip and no campaign state written, so `CoopVetoClassificationTests` needs no entry. Feature: [alignment-recruitment.md](../features/alignment-recruitment.md). **Tests:** `AiVolunteerEstimateBindingTests`, `AlignmentRecruitmentWiringTests`. **ACTIVE**, not yet checked in game.

## Patch115_RedArrow

**Targets:** `public void TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.ViewModelCollection.ArmyManagement.ArmyManagementItemVM.UpdateEligibility()` and `public ArmyManagementItemVM(Action<ArmyManagementItemVM> onAddToCart, Action<ArmyManagementItemVM> onRemove, Action<ArmyManagementItemVM> onFocus, MobileParty mobileParty)` (v1.5.5, `ArmyManagementItemVM.cs:401-430,487-509`). Two postfix classes in `Main/Features/RedArrow/Hooks/`, `ArmyManagementItemVM_UpdateEligibility_RedArrow_Patch` and `ArmyManagementItemVM_Constructor_RedArrow_Patch`, both `[ClientOnly]`, category `Patch115_RedArrow` (GameInit, declared by `RedArrowModule`). #873, 2026-10-23. **ACTIVE**, not yet checked in game.

Vanilla refuses to call or release army members during any siege: `UpdateEligibility` asks the army-management model (whose first branch is the player's siege) and then `CampaignUIHelper.GetMapScreenActionIsEnabledWithReason` (two siege blocks), and the constructor sets `IsTransferDisabled = IsMainHero || PlayerSiege.PlayerSiegeEvent != null` (and ends with `RefreshValues()`). No model override is involved: the army-management model is untouched, and the `UpdateEligibility` postfix redoes the party check itself. The constructor postfix sets `IsTransferDisabled` to `IsMainHero` when `RedArrowService.Applies` holds (singleplayer, switch on, the player's party in a besieger camp and leading its army). The `UpdateEligibility` postfix returns at once on vanilla's own skip branch (`CanJoinBackWithoutCost`, or `IsInCart && !IsAlreadyWithPlayer`); otherwise, when the feature applies, it reads the candidate through the adapter and asks the service for the party check (vanilla's text on a refusal), then the map-action check (the player's own besieged settlement allowed; a foreign one, a raid, a map event and every other block kept), and writes the private `_eligibilityReason` through an `AccessTools.FieldRef` bound in `Prepare`, plus the public `IsEligible`.

**Fail safe.** The eligibility patch's `Prepare` returns false, with one logged error, when `_eligibilityReason` is missing, and the constructor patch's `Prepare` defers to it, so a missing field never leaves the remove buttons on while the add buttons stay refused. That coupling covers only that field: if one target alone moved, the other postfix would still apply, and `HarmonyPatchBindingTests` is the guard for a moved target. A fault in a postfix or the boundary is caught, logged once, and leaves vanilla's value, which is the block. In a postfix the original's result is already stored, so a PatchShield swallow of a JIT-time member fault also leaves vanilla's value.

**Callers and thread:** `UpdateEligibility` runs from the constructor, `OnAddToCart` (twice), `OnRemove` and `ArmyManagementVM.ExecuteReset` (`ArmyManagementItemVM.cs:427,461,467,472`, `ArmyManagementVM.cs:1354`); the constructor from `ArmyManagementVM` (:994 for each lord party of the player's faction, :997 for the player's own row). Main thread, UI only. The actor is always the local player: the adapters read `Campaign.Current?.MainParty`, `Hero.MainHero` and `MobileParty.MainParty`.

**Co-op.** UI only, no campaign state written by the patches; the service stands down in any session, for a deferring client and on a dedicated server, and the classes are `[ClientOnly]`. Feature: [red-arrow.md](../features/red-arrow.md). **Tests:** `RedArrowWiringTests`, `RedArrowServiceTests`, `RedArrowArmyBoundaryTests`, `RedArrowPostfixTests`, `RedArrowBindingTests`.

## Patch116_SwornAnew

**Target:** `private bool TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors.LordDefectionCampaignBehavior.conversation_player_start_defection_with_prisoner_on_condition()` (v1.5.5, `LordDefectionCampaignBehavior.cs:369-384`), one postfix class `Patch116_PrisonerDefectionLine_Postfix` in `Main/Features/SwornAnew/Hooks/`, category `Patch116_SwornAnew` (ProcessLoad, declared by `SwornAnewModule`). #869, 2026-10-23.

The condition of the prisoner line "I have an offer for you: join us, and be set free", shown in a conversation with a prisoner of the player's party and with one in a dungeon of the player's clan. Vanilla checks that the player leads a kingdom, the lord leads their own clan, is a prisoner, is not of the ruling clan, has no blood feud, and is held by the main party or in a settlement the player's clan owns. It never asks `DefectionModel`, so the new `TaomDefectionModel` (which opens the diplomacy-menu option for a prisoner) would leave this line unrestricted. The postfix returns when vanilla's answer is false, or when the service or adapter is missing; otherwise it asks `SwornAnewService.HidesPrisonerLine(adapter.ReadConversationPartner())` and sets `__result = false` when the service refuses the prisoner. A free hero, the switch off and an unreadable partner leave vanilla's answer alone. The full verdict applies, not the alignment alone, so a mercenary clan's lord is hidden too (joining through the line raises `JoinAsMercenary` and never arms the party-screen commit). The veto runs on every peer, a co-op peer and a dedicated server included, because it only hides a line locally. A throw is caught, logged, and vanilla's answer kept.

The service verdict repeats vanilla's own `DefaultDefectionModel` faction checks, so the postfix can also hide the line for a prisoner whose clan is under mercenary service, and for a prisoner already in the player's own faction; vanilla's line offers it for both. Alignment (a Free lord is never asked to join an Evil realm, nor an Evil lord a Free one) and the lore heroes (`IUncapturableRegistry`: Sauron and the Nine, by identity) are the point of the patch.

**Callers and thread:** the only reference to the target is the condition delegate of the `player_prisoner_talk` line in `AddLordDefectionPersuasionOptions` (`:141`); the method is private, so nothing outside the behavior calls it. The conversation system evaluates the delegate on the main thread when it builds the player's options for the `hero_main_options` state. Once per option build, not per frame.

**PatchShield.** `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors` is not an excluded namespace and the condition is not on `ExcludedTargetMethods`, so the shield's finalizer wraps the method from a process's first game start (the category applies at OnSubModuleLoad, before PatchShield's pass 2). The postfix catches what its body throws at run time. A missing-API failure in JIT-compiling the postfix reaches the finalizer; a postfix fault leaves vanilla's stored value (`harmony-il.md`, 2026-10-20 correction). If the shield ever strips the postfix, the prisoner line goes back to vanilla's unrestricted answer; the diplomacy option stays gated by the model, which the shield does not touch.

**Co-op.** A void postfix on a pure condition: no skip, no campaign state written, so `CoopVetoClassificationTests` needs no entry. The veto is not gated on co-op or a dedicated server (the model and the commit are, through `IsDedicatedServer || ShouldDeferToHost`; see the `SwornAnewBehavior` row in [coop-interop.md](../features/coop-interop.md)). Feature: [sworn-anew.md](../features/sworn-anew.md). **Tests:** `Patch116PrisonerDefectionLineTests`, `SwornAnewBindingTests` (target shape, parameter name `__result`, the line still registered with this condition, the category applying and the JIT accepting it, both diplomacy conditions still asking the model). **ACTIVE**, not yet checked in game.
## Patch117_MessengerSessionRules

**Targets:** six private instance conditions, all `bool` with no parameters (v1.5.5), resolved by name with `AccessTools.Method` in `[HarmonyTargetMethods]`: `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors.LordConversationsCampaignBehavior` `conversation_lord_join_army_on_condition` (`:2055`, line `main_option_discussions_1`), `player_ask_to_join_players_army_on_condition` (`:2634`, `main_option_discussions_2`), `conversation_clan_member_manage_troops_on_condition` (`:3842`, `clan_member_manage_troops`) and `conversation_clan_member_manage_inventory_on_condition` (`:3852`, `clan_member_manage_inventory`); `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors.CompanionRolesCampaignBehavior.companion_rejoin_after_emprisonment_role_on_condition` (`:503`, line `companion_rejoin_after_emprisonment_role`); and `SandBox.CampaignBehaviors.BoardGameCampaignBehavior.conversation_lord_talk_game_on_condition` (`:585`, line `lord_player_play_game`). One postfix class `MessengerSessionRulesPatch` in `Main/Features/Messengers/Hooks/`, category `Patch117_MessengerSessionRules` (GameInit, applied by `MessengerSessionRulesModule`). #898, 2026-10-23.

**Why:** while `IMessengerConversationState.IsOpen`, the join-army consequences (`lord_considers_army_on_consequence`, `lord_considers_joining_player_army_on_consequence`) call `Army.AddPartyToMergedParties`, which sets `AttachedTo = LeaderParty` and snaps the attached party across the map; the board game starts after `close_window`, when the messenger has already ended the mission, so the line is a dead end; the clan member troop and equipment inspections and the companion recall are physical hand-overs that a messenger cannot do. The postfix is `__result = Filter(__result, state)`: `Filter` only ever turns true into false, and has no error path to fail (the state's only implementation reads one volatile bool). It is a postfix, not a prefix, so it runs after Enlistment's Patch66 prefix on the join-army condition (`LordConversationsConditionPatches.cs`). The "gathering" answer `lord_tell_gathering_player_answer` is unreachable in v1.5.5 (no line outputs the `lord_tell_gathering` token); its consequence would only set `MainParty.Army`. The enlist oath and the Tokens of Esteem gift line are TAOM lines and read the flag themselves (`EnlistmentDialogBehavior`, `GiftConversationFlow`), not through this patch. Wanderer hiring stays on purpose.

**Callers and thread:** each condition is a delegate registered with the conversation starter and run by `ConversationSentence.RunCondition`, called from `ConversationManager.GetSentenceOptions` (`ConversationManager.cs:515-536`) whenever the sentence options are rebuilt, on the main thread; the postfix reads one volatile bool.

**PatchShield.** None of the targets is on `PatchShieldPolicy.ExcludedTargetNamespacePrefixes` or `ExcludedTargetMethods`, so the shield's finalizer attaches to each condition from a process's second game start (TAOM's GameInit batch is patched after pass 2, so in the first game they are unshielded), and does not install at all while a co-op module is active (`PatchShieldPolicy.ShouldInstall`). The finalizer swallows only a missing-API throw from the original and strips the unprotected owners' patches on the method.

**Assembly:** `SandBox.dll` is not in `ClientAssemblies.Names`, so the patch lives in core, as `BardTownGuardModule` does. `MessengerSessionRulesPatchTests` pin the targets, the postfix-only shape and the JIT-compiled replacement against the installed DLLs (`BindingVerification`).

**Co-op.** A postfix on local conversation predicates that skips nothing and writes no campaign state, so `CoopVetoClassificationTests` needs no entry. Feature: [messengers.md](../features/messengers.md). **ACTIVE**, not yet checked in game.

## Patch118_SeasonedHostsStewardXp

**Target:** `public void TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CharacterDevelopment.DefaultSkillLevelingManager.OnSettlementProjectFinished(Settlement settlement)` (v1.5.5, `DefaultSkillLevelingManager.cs:150`, the only overload), one skipping prefix class `Patch118_ProjectStewardXp` in `Main/Features/SeasonedHosts/Hooks/`, category `Patch118_SeasonedHostsStewardXp` (GameInit, declared by `SeasonedHostsModule`). #890, 2026-10-24.

Vanilla grants 1000 Steward XP through `OnSettlementSkillExercised` each time a settlement building project finishes: to the settlement's governor, or to the owning clan's leader when he is in it. No model seam reaches it, so the prefix is the only way to remove the grant. `return !service.SkipsProjectStewardXp();` skips the original when the MCM switch "No Steward XP From Finished Projects" (default off) is on and this peer does not defer to the co-op host. A null service or a fault returns true, so vanilla grants the XP and the first fault is logged once.

**Callers and thread:** the single caller is `BuildingsCampaignBehavior.OnBuildingLevelChanged` (`:155`), after every building level gain in any settlement, the player's own fiefs included, so the skip is global and the setting says so. Main thread, a few calls per campaign day at most. `SeasonedHostsBindingTests` pins that the behavior still calls the target.

**PatchShield.** `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CharacterDevelopment` is not an excluded namespace and the target is not on `ExcludedTargetMethods`, so the shield's finalizer can wrap it (`PatchShieldPolicy.ExcludedTargetNamespacePrefixes` lists GauntletUI, TwoDimension and ManagedCallbacks only). The prefix catches what its body throws. If the shield ever strips the prefix, the grant goes back to vanilla's.

**Co-op.** A skipping prefix on a replicated mutation (XP on a hero's developer) whose condition is a per-peer MCM value, so it is classified `GatedForCoop` in `CoopVetoClassificationTests` (listed in `GatedVetoes_AreExactlyThePinnedSet`) and `SeasonedHostsService.SkipsProjectStewardXp` returns false while `ShouldDeferToHost`. Feature: [seasoned-hosts.md](../features/seasoned-hosts.md). **Tests:** `SeasonedHostsBindingTests` (11: the single overload, the category attaching one bool prefix and the JIT accepting it, the caller), `Patch118ProjectStewardXpTests` (5: the prefix verdict through the module's statics handshake, the fault path and the log-once), `SeasonedHostsServiceTests` (27: the verdict and the host deferral), `SeasonedHostsWiringTests` (16). **ACTIVE**, off by default (the switch), not yet checked in game.
## Patch119_EncyclopediaHeroFilters

**Targets:** `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.Encyclopedia.Pages.DefaultEncyclopediaHeroPage.InitializeFilterItems()`, a `protected override IEnumerable<EncyclopediaFilterGroup>` with no parameters (v1.5.5, `DefaultEncyclopediaHeroPage.cs:175-212`), one Postfix in `Main/Features/Encyclopedia/Hooks/DefaultEncyclopediaHeroPageInitializeFilterItemsPatch.cs`, category `Patch119_EncyclopediaHeroFilters`, applied by `EncyclopediaFiltersModule` at ProcessLoad (OnSubModuleLoad). #907, 2026-10-24.

**Why:** the hero page has no Race or Age filter. The postfix copies `__result`, appends the groups from `IEncyclopediaHeroFilterAdapter` and assigns the copy back; a throw leaves the incoming list. ProcessLoad, not GameInit, because SandBox builds the encyclopedia pages in its own `OnGameInitializationFinished` (`SandBoxSubModule.cs:117,125`), which runs before TAOM's, so a GameInit patch would miss the first campaign. The co-op module postfixes the same method with a lazy AddItem, so `__result` is never cast to a `List`.

**Callers and thread:** `EncyclopediaPage` runs `InitializeFilterItems` once, in its constructor (`_filters = InitializeFilterItems()`), on the main thread; `IsFiltered` then runs each group predicate (`EncyclopediaPage.IsFiltered`, no catch), which is why every predicate the adapter builds catches and lets the hero through.

**PatchShield.** PatchShield wraps this vanilla method (it skips only TAOM-declared methods, `Dependencies/Foundation/PatchShield.cs:200-209`), installs nothing when a co-op module is active (`:150-162`), and returns the original's value after a postfix fault (`:299-307`). This postfix catches everything itself, so the finalizer never fires for it.

**Co-op.** A postfix on a UI list builder: nothing skipped, no campaign state written, so `CoopVetoClassificationTests` needs no entry. Config: `ModuleData/encyclopedia/hero_filters.json`. **ACTIVE**, not yet checked in game.

## Patch120_HarvestRoad

**Targets:** `public static void TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.Actions.SellGoodsForTradeAction.ApplyByVillagerTrade(Settlement settlement, MobileParty villagerParty)` (v1.5.5, `SellGoodsForTradeAction.cs:70`) and `private void TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors.PartiesBuyFoodCampaignBehavior.BuyFoodInternal(MobileParty mobileParty, Settlement settlement, int numberOfFoodItemsNeededToBuy)` (`PartiesBuyFoodCampaignBehavior.cs:56`), both resolved with pinned argument types. Two nested prefix and postfix classes in `Main/Features/HarvestRoad/Hooks/HarvestRoadPatches.cs`, `VillagerSalePatch` and `FoodPurchasePatch`, category `Patch120_HarvestRoad` (GameInit, declared by `HarvestRoadModule`). #912, 2026-10-26.

**Why:** vanilla raises `OnCaravanTransactionCompleted` only for caravans (`SellGoodsForTradeAction.cs:64-66`). The prefix keeps a snapshot of the party's item roster in `__state` (null when the gates say no), the postfix diffs it against the roster after the call and raises the event for the lines that left (villager, positive counts) or arrived (food buyer, negative counts). The notice reads the sign of the first line, so each list carries one sign. Every body is in try/catch with a one-time warning; the prefix assigns `__state = null` before anything can throw.

**Callers and thread:** `ApplyByVillagerTrade` has one caller, `VillagerCampaignBehavior.OnSettlementEntered` (`:324-330`, towns only). `BuyFoodInternal` is reached from `TryBuyingFood` (settlement entry and the hourly party tick, `doParallel: false`, so the main thread) and `BuyFoodForArmy`; it is called hourly with an amount of 0 for stocked parties, which the prefix skips without a snapshot. A castle is neither `IsTown` nor `IsVillage`, so neither seam reaches one in v1.5.5. Patch68's diagnostics prefix and postfix sit on `ApplyByVillagerTrade` too and coexist.

**PatchShield.** Neither target is on `PatchShieldPolicy.ExcludedTargetNamespacePrefixes` or `ExcludedTargetMethods`, so the shield's finalizer attaches from a process's second game start. A postfix that throws leaves vanilla's trade untouched either way.

**Co-op.** Prefix and postfix write no campaign state and skip nothing, so `CoopVetoClassificationTests` needs no entry. The event is the one Coop already forwards from the server to every client, so the server's switch decides for all peers. Feature: [harvest-road.md](../features/harvest-road.md). **Tests:** `HarvestRoadBindingTests` (targets, parameter names, dispatcher shape, engine members, category and JIT), `HarvestRoadPatchTests`, `HarvestRoadWiringTests`. **ACTIVE**, not yet checked in game.

## Patch121_ManyHammers

**Lives in TAOM.Client** (`Client/Features/ManyHammers/Hooks/`, ADR-013): `SmeltingVM` and `RefinementVM` are in the client-only `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.ViewModelCollection` assembly. Category `Patch121_ManyHammers` (GameInit), declared by `ManyHammersModule` in core, so `FeatureModules.All` is the only apply line and `SubModule.cs` and `IoC.cs` are untouched. `PatchCategoryIndex.Build` reads TAOM.dll, then TAOM.Client.dll when it loaded, so a client applies the three classes and a dedicated server applies none. #911, 2026-10-26.

**Targets (v1.5.5, three classes):** prefix on `public void SmeltingVM.TrySmeltingSelectedItems(Hero currentCraftingHero)` (`SmeltingVM.cs:229`); prefix on `public void RefinementVM.ExecuteSelectedRefinement(Hero currentCraftingHero)` (`RefinementVM.cs:136`); postfix on `public void SmeltingVM.RefreshList()` (`SmeltingVM.cs:189`). Classes `Patch121_SmeltingVMTrySmeltingSelectedItems`, `Patch121_RefinementVMExecuteSelectedRefinement` and `Patch121_SmeltingVMRefreshList`, plus the non-patch helpers `ManyHammersCalls` and `SmeltingListFilter`. Every target is pinned with its argument types, and `ManyHammersWiringTests` pins the parameter names Harmony injects by (`currentCraftingHero`) against the installed DLLs.

**Prefixes never skip vanilla.** Both are void prefixes, so they cannot. With Shift or Ctrl held (Ctrl wins) and the MCM switch on, each runs the extra units through `Campaign.Current.GetCampaignBehavior<ICraftingCampaignBehavior>().DoSmelting` or `DoRefinement`, the instance the view model itself holds, and leaves exactly one unit to vanilla, which then does its own `RefreshList`, sort, reselect and `CraftingVM.UpdateAll`. Before each extra unit the numbers are read again (stamina, the model's cost for this unit, the nine materials, the stack) and the unit runs only while it and vanilla's last still fit. A unit needs stamina above 10 and at least the model's cost, and no material may go negative; both are the smithy button's own rules, plus the cost. The cost and the charcoal come from `Campaign.Current.Models.SmithingModel` at each unit, so `TaomSmithingModel`'s cultural feats and any cap on the smelt output reach bulk through the model slot, which this feature never edits. A throw in an extra unit is logged once and ends the loop. Because the opening plan reserves a unit, a half-finished `DoSmelting` still leaves at least one weapon for vanilla's own call.

**The postfix hides locked rows.** `RefreshList` runs in the `SmeltingVM` constructor, after every smelt, on each switch to the Smelting tab and in BannerlordCoop's client refresh; the postfix drops the rows whose `IsLocked` is set. When no row is left it clears any selection (vanilla's reselect after a smelt would hand a null to `OnItemSelection`, `SmeltingVM.cs:273-277`). When the selection was locked it selects, through the public `ExecuteSelection()`, the first row left of the same weapon, else the first row left. A row locked inside the smithy is not touched by the click: vanilla records the lock (`ProcessLockItem` only edits its lock list, `SmeltingVM.cs:249-263`), and the next `RefreshList` hides the row. The filter never rebuilds the list: it runs inside `RefreshList`'s own postfix, so a rebuild would re-enter it. No private field or method of either view model is reflected into, and no private method is a patch target.

**Callers and thread:** `TrySmeltingSelectedItems` and `ExecuteSelectedRefinement` are called only by `CraftingVM.ExecuteMainAction` (`CraftingVM.cs:1144-1153`), from the smithy's button click on the main thread. `RefreshList` is called from the constructor (`SmeltingVM.cs:169`), after each smelt (`:271`), on each switch to the Smelting tab (`CraftingVM.cs:1267`), and on a BannerlordCoop client from `SmithingVMsHandler.Handle_NetworkRefreshSmelting`. Shift and Ctrl are read with `Input.IsKeyDown`, left and right keys together.

**PatchShield.** `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.ViewModelCollection` is not an excluded namespace and none of the three is on `ExcludedTargetMethods`, so the shield's finalizer attaches from a process's second game start (TAOM's GameInit batch is patched after pass 2, so in the first game they are unshielded), and not at all while a co-op module is active (`PatchShieldPolicy.ShouldInstall`). Every patch body catches what it throws and logs it once (`ManyHammersCalls.Error`).

**Co-op.** The prefixes are void, so `CoopVetoClassificationTests` (which scans only bool prefixes) needs no row. A guest (`ShouldDeferToHost`) plans one unit and keeps the locked-row filter, which is a view filter. Feature: [many-hammers.md](../features/many-hammers.md). **Tests:** `BulkCraftRulesTests`, `ManyHammersServiceTests`, `ManyHammersSettingsProviderTests`, `BenchUnitAdapterTests`, `SmeltingListFilterTests`, `ManyHammersWordingTests`, `ManyHammersWiringTests`. **ACTIVE**, not yet checked in game.
## Patch122_BanditVoices

**Target:** `private bool TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors.BanditInteractionsCampaignBehavior.bandit_neutral_greet_on_condition()` (v1.5.5, `BanditInteractionsCampaignBehavior.cs:213`, the only overload, no parameters), resolved by name. One Postfix, `BanditGreetingPatch` in `Main/Features/BanditVoices/Hooks/BanditGreetingPatch.cs`, category `Patch122_BanditVoices` (GameInit, declared by `BanditVoicesModule`). #900, 2026-10-26.

**Why:** vanilla's condition for the bandit's opening line sets the two conversation text variables `BANDIT_NEUTRAL_GREETING` and `BANDIT_ULTIMATUM` and returns true when the player attacked a bandit party on land or at sea. For a party the player has not met it picks a line with `MBRandom.RandomInt(8)`; the "harmless" line (case 4) and the "dozens more of us hiding" line (case 7) carry the faction-specific variants, and vanilla knows only its five original bandit factions. The postfix runs only when `__result` is true, asks `BanditVoicesService.Apply` and, on a first meeting with one of the eight talking TAOM bandit clans, overwrites both variables together on a roll of 0.25 from TAOM's own `IRandomSource`: the clan's own greeting from `str_taom_bv_greeting.<clanId>_harmless` or `_brothers`, and vanilla's own paired ultimatum (`{=A5IJmN0X}` or `{=ASRpFaGF}`) set verbatim. A fault is caught and logged once; vanilla's text stands.

**Callers and thread:** a dialogue-line condition (`AddDialogLine("bandit_start_attacker", "start", ...)`, `:114`), evaluated by the conversation manager on the main thread when the conversation opens. Not a hot path. The first-meeting test reads vanilla's private `_interactedBandits` (`:49`) through one `AccessTools.FieldRefAccess` typed as `object` and read as `IDictionary` (catalogued in `reflection-sites.md`); a party missing from it is a first meeting, and the greeting's consequence (`:361-367`) records it, so the clan speaks at most once per party.

**PatchShield.** The target is on `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.CampaignBehaviors`, not an excluded namespace or method, so the shield's finalizer can wrap it. The postfix catches everything itself, so a fault never reaches the finalizer.

**Co-op.** A postfix that skips nothing and writes only two local conversation text variables, so `CoopVetoClassificationTests` needs no entry; the module declares no campaign behavior, so `CoopGateDocCoverageTests` asks for no row. It stands down on a dedicated server (`IDedicatedServerProvider`) and while a messenger conversation is open (`IMessengerConversationState`). The roll never touches the engine's shared random stream. `EnableBanditGreetings` is classified as presentation in `CoopSettingsRelevance`. Feature: [bandit-voices.md](../features/bandit-voices.md). **Tests:** `BanditVoicesBindingTests` (target shape, parameter name, the two variables and the two vanilla ultimatum texts still in the method body, the field binding, one postfix attached and the JIT accepting it), `BanditVoicesServiceTests`, `BanditVoicesPatchTests` (the postfix's two paths, one warning on a throw, the GameText node), `BanditVoicesWiringTests`, and the `_interactedBandits` row of `ReflectionSiteBindingTests`. **ACTIVE**, not yet checked in game.

## Patch124_TurboClick

**Target:** `private bool SandBox.View.Map.MapScreen.IsParleyClickReleased()` (v1.5.6, the only overload, no parameters, not virtual), resolved by name. One Postfix, `MapScreen_IsParleyClickReleased_TurboClick_Patch` in `Main/Features/TimeAcceleration/Hooks/MapScreen_IsParleyClickReleased_TurboClick_Patch.cs`, category `Patch124_TurboClick` (ProcessLoad, declared by `TimeAccelerationModule`), `[ClientOnly]`. #1040, 2026-10-27.

**Why:** v1.5.6 `MapScreen.HandleMouse` sends a released click on a party or settlement to `HandleParleyClick` when this predicate is true (Left Ctrl, `MapParleyModifier`, on a keyboard), and to `HandleLeftMouseButtonClick` otherwise, which is the whole v1.5.5 click path. TAOM's turbo is Ctrl + Space, so a Left Ctrl turbo click on a party or settlement went to the parley handler instead of moving: a friendly or neutral target printed "Cannot parley with this party." (or "...settlement."), and an at-war target printed vanilla's refusal or opened a parley. The postfix sets `__result = false` while `ITimeAccelerationService.IsTurboActive`, so the else branch handles the click. Without turbo, or when vanilla answered false, vanilla's answer stands. A fault is caught and logged once, and vanilla's answer stands.

**Callers and thread:** `HandleMouse` only (pinned by `TurboClickBindingTests`), evaluated every campaign map frame on the main thread. The postfix returns on a false result before it touches the service, so the steady-state cost is one branch. The service is resolved lazily on the first true answer.

**Inlining.** Harmony 2.4.2 marks a patched method not inlinable, and the category applies at ProcessLoad (`OnSubModuleLoad`), before the installed Coop's `PatchAll` patches `HandleMouse` (`MapSiegeHoverCursorPatch` postfix, `DisableGameMenuPausePatches` transpiler) on a co-op join or host start, and before any later mod. A foreign mod that patches `HandleMouse` even earlier could still cause an inlined copy of the 61-byte original, and then the postfix would never run; the in-game check is the proof. Other ClientOnly MapScreen patches (Patch89) already apply in `OnSubModuleLoad`.

**Known limitations.** (1) Frame order: `MapScreen.HandleMouse` runs inside the game tick (`Module.OnApplicationTick` calls `GameManagerBase.Current.OnTick`, which reaches `MapState.OnMapModeTick`, `IMapStateHandler.BeforeTick` and `HandleMouse`) before the submodules' `OnApplicationTick`, where TAOM updates the turbo latch; so a click released on the exact frame Space is pressed still parleys, and one released on the exact frame Space is released moves. Codex rated it MEDIUM; it was not fixed because the fix would duplicate `OnTick`'s gates in a second predicate for a one-frame window (simplicity criterion). (2) Gamepad: while the latch is open the postfix also cancels a `MapParleyClick` (ControllerRUp) press, reachable only while keyboard Ctrl (either side) and the turbo key are held at the same time.

**In-game check.** Hold Left Ctrl + Space and click a friendly town, a friendly party and an enemy lord party; each must move or act as an ordinary click. It must be Left Ctrl: `MapParleyModifier` is LeftControl plus ControllerRUp only, while TAOM's latch accepts either Ctrl. Control step: with Right Ctrl + Space a town click moves even without the patch, so it proves nothing.

**Co-op.** Local UI only. Turbo never latches on a co-op client that defers to the host, and a void postfix is outside `CoopVetoClassificationTests`. Feature: [time-acceleration.md](../features/time-acceleration.md). **Tests:** `TurboClickPatchTests`, `TurboClickBindingTests`, and the `IsTurboActive` rows of `TimeAccelerationServiceTests`. **ACTIVE**, not yet checked in game.

## Patch125_HideoutClanGuard

**Target:** two prefixes in `Main/Features/BanditManagement/Hooks/Patch125_HideoutClanGuard.cs`, category `Patch125_HideoutClanGuard`, applied in the standard batch beside Patch86 (`SubModule.cs`). (1) `private int BanditSpawnCampaignBehavior.GetInfestedHideoutCount(Clan banditFaction)`, resolved by name, injecting the private field `_hideouts` (`Dictionary<CultureObject, List<Hideout>>`, as `____hideouts`). (2) `public MobileParty BanditSpawnCampaignBehavior.AddBanditToHideout(Hideout hideoutComponent, PartyTemplateObject overridenPartyTemplate = null, bool isBanditBossParty = false)`. v1.5.6. #1063, 2026-10-29.

**Why:** a bandit clan lives in TAOM's `characters/clans.xml` and the hideouts of its culture live in TAOM_Map's `settlements.xml`, so either can load without the other. (1) A clan whose culture owns no hideout (TAOM with an older TAOM_Map): `GetInfestedHideoutCount` reads `_hideouts[banditFaction.Culture]` with no guard (`:499`), and `CacheHideouts` fills that dictionary only from `Hideout.All`, so new-game stage 11 (`SpawnBanditsAroundHideoutAtNewGame`) throws `KeyNotFoundException`. The prefix returns 0 for a culture with no entry, so the clan spawns nothing and fills no den. (2) A hideout whose culture has no clan (a save made before the clan existed: `SandBoxManager.InitializeSandboxXMLs` loads Settlements on a saved campaign but not Factions): `AddBanditToHideout` finds no clan and dereferences null at `clan.DefaultPartyTemplate` (`:331`). The prefix returns null, the value vanilla already returns for a non-bandit hideout, and runs vanilla in every other case. Both report once per culture to the file log. Found by the Codex pre-review of the Feral Orcs (`docs/reviews/rca-feral-orcs-2026-10-29.md`).

**Callers and thread:** (1) `SpawnBanditsAroundHideoutAtNewGame` (new-game stage 11), `SpawnBanditsAroundHideout` (hourly clan tick at night) and `AddNewHideouts` (daily), campaign main thread. In `AddNewHideouts` a count of 0 gives the den-less clan the heaviest weight (`1 - n/max`) in that day's single pick; when it wins, `SelectANonInfestedHideoutOfSameCultureByWeight` finds no den and nothing is filled, so while a map mismatch lasts it takes a share of the other clans' daily refills (size unmeasured). (2) `FillANewHideoutWithBandits` (same-culture dens only, ignores the result), `CampaignIncidentEffects.InfestNearbyHideout`, the Gold Rush incident (ignores it; it still fires `OnHideoutSpotted`, a "hideout spotted" message and Scouting XP for a den that stays empty), and `AddBossParty` (`:211`, dereferences it). `AddBossParty` runs from `OnSettlementEntered` when a bandit party enters a visible den at any time in the campaign, not only at a new game, and a bandit party only enters a den of its own culture (`AiVisitSettlementBehavior`), so a clan of that culture exists and the guard never returns null there. Stage 10's new-game fill picks dens per clan, so it never reaches a clanless den. Both prefixes are a dictionary lookup or a walk of `Clan.BanditFactions`; no hot path.

**Known gap:** the guard checks only that some bandit clan carries the den's culture; vanilla also requires that clan to be a hideout or looter faction (`IsBanditFaction || IsLooterFaction`). A naval bandit clan of a den's culture would still reach the null dereference; TAOM ships none. Applied from `OnGameInitializationFinished` (`SubModule.cs`), which precedes `OnNewGameCreated` and every follow-up stage. Verified against the v1.5.6 DLLs by the deep-review engine lens, 2026-10-29.

**Tests:** `HideoutClanGuardTests` (the decisions), `Patch125HideoutClanGuardBindingTests` (targets, parameter names, the field's type). **ACTIVE**, not yet checked in game. Feature: [bandit-management.md](../features/bandit-management.md).

## Patch126_StagedSiege

**Target:** two classes in `Main/Features/StagedSiege/Hooks/`, category `Patch126_StagedSiege` (GameInit, declared by `StagedSiegeModule`). `StagedSiegeSpawnSettingsPatch`: a void prefix, Low priority, on the public `DefaultBattleMissionAgentSpawnLogic.InitWithSinglePhase(int defenderTotalSpawn, int attackerTotalSpawn, int defenderInitialSpawn, int attackerInitialSpawn, bool spawnDefenders, bool spawnAttackers, in MissionSpawnSettings spawnSettings)`, taking `object __instance` and `ref MissionSpawnSettings spawnSettings`. `StagedSiegeSpawnFramePatch`: a void postfix on the public `Mission.GetFormationSpawnFrame(Team team, FormationClass formationClass, bool isReinforcement, out WorldPosition spawnPosition, out Vec2 spawnDirection, bool useDefaultClassIfNotFound = true)` (v1.5.6, the only overload), taking `Mission __instance`, `Team team`, `bool isReinforcement` and both outs by `ref`. Staged Siege (#1054), 2026-10-29.

**Why:** in a castle assault on a recorded scene, reinforcements follow the stages instead of the global timer. The prefix replaces the incoming settings with the Balanced method on a custom timer (batch 0.05 and desired 0.166 by default, wave count 0), keeps the incoming initial method, wave percentage, defender advantage and maximum side ratio, writes the larger of the incoming global interval and the staged trickle as the interval (under a custom timer its only reader is `BattleEndLogic`'s rout check, interval plus 3 s), and installs `StagedReinforcementTimer` through `SetCustomReinforcementSpawnTimer`. The timer is installed before the settings change, so a fault between the two leaves the global timer in charge. The postfix overwrites the frame a reinforcement formation spawns on: attackers on the previous point facing the current one, defenders on the recorded hold frame; stage A has neither and keeps vanilla's frame.

**Callers and thread:** `InitWithSinglePhase` runs once per battle from the spawn handlers (the siege handler's `AfterStart`, field battles, sally-outs, Custom Battle), on the main thread; the first guard is a pure read of the staged slot (`StagedSiegeSlot.HasArmed`), so a battle that is not an armed staged siege reads no engine state. `GetFormationSpawnFrame` is called by the troop spawn path (v1.5.6 :3964-3972, :4266); the postfix returns on `isReinforcement` false and on an unarmed slot, then reads only the immutable `StageSnapshot`, so its thread does not matter. Both bodies name no engine member: the engine work sits in `[MethodImpl(NoInlining)]` helpers called inside the try, and a fault leaves vanilla's settings or frame.

**Coexistence.** Patch102 (Siege Forces) is Normal priority on the same method and writes only the four spawn-total ints; this prefix writes only `spawnSettings` and reads none of the ints, so order cannot change either result, and Low priority runs it second. The mission behavior arms the service in `EarlyStart`, before the siege spawn handler's `AfterStart` calls `InitWithSinglePhase`, which the prefix needs.

**Verified by IL; owed in game: the S2 settings-live line.** (1) The ref write into the `in` argument reaches `Init`: the vanilla siege handler passes its local by address (`SandBoxSiegeMissionSpawnHandler.cs:18-19`), `InitWithSinglePhase` forwards the same pointer (`DefaultBattleMissionAgentSpawnLogic.cs:199`) and `Init` copies it (:397); the `S2 deployment over: settings live` line confirms it in the first game run.

**UNVERIFIED until the first game run.** (2) Whether a navmesh-less `WorldPosition` resolves in the troop spawn (the spawn projects onto the navmesh, :3983-3995): the `S2 reinforcement ... m from the staged frame` lines carry the distance.
