**Tests:** `StagedSiegeBindingTests` pins both targets, the parameter names, the Low priority, the twelve-parameter settings constructor and every engine member the adapter, patches, timer, tactic and behavior reference; `StagedSiegeSpawnSettingsPatchTests` runs the prefix body; `StagedSiegeWiringTests` pins the category and the two classes. Feature doc: [staged-siege.md](../features/staged-siege.md). **ACTIVE**, not yet checked in game.

## Patch127_ShadowfaxClan

**Target:** three classes in `Main/Features/ShadowfaxScreens/Clan/Hooks/`, category `Patch127_ShadowfaxClan` (`ClanFastOpenCalls.PatchCategory`; GameInit, through `ShadowfaxScreensModule`). `ClanPartiesVM_RefreshValues_ClanFastOpen_Patch`: a transpiler (plus a `[HarmonyCleanup]`) on `public override void ClanPartiesVM.RefreshValues()` (v1.5.5). `ClanPartiesVM_RefreshPartiesList_ClanFastOpen_Patch`: a void prefix (`out long __state`, `Priority.First`) and a void finalizer (`long __state`, `Priority.First`) on `public void ClanPartiesVM.RefreshPartiesList()`, counting and timing only. `ClanManagementVM_Ctor_ClanFastOpen_Patch`: a void prefix (`out ScreenOpenWindow? __state`) and a void finalizer (`Exception? __exception`, `ScreenOpenWindow? __state`) on the only `ClanManagementVM` constructor, `(Action, Action<Hero>, Action<Hero>, Action)`.

Clan fast open (2026-10-19, issue #851; feature: [shadowfax-screens.md](../features/shadowfax-screens.md#clan-fast-open)). Vanilla builds the clan screen's party lists three times per open: the `ClanPartiesVM` constructor calls `RefreshPartiesList` and then `RefreshValues` (which builds again, `ClanPartiesVM.cs:414-415, 428`), and the `ClanManagementVM` constructor reaches `RefreshValues` again through `UpdateKingdomRelatedProperties`, `UpdateBannerVisuals` and `_clanParties.RefreshValues()` (`ClanManagementVM.cs:1166, 1325, 1269, 1212`). The transpiler swaps the one `RefreshPartiesList()` call inside `RefreshValues` for `ClanPartiesVM_RefreshValues_ClanFastOpen_Patch.RefreshPartiesListInRefresh(ClanPartiesVM)` through the soft-failing `TickProfilerTranspiler.Rewrite` with a `CallSwap`, as Patch103 does: a site count other than one leaves the IL vanilla, and `ClanScreenFastPathService.LogInstall` writes one install or bail line from `ShadowfaxScreensModule.OnPhase`. The helper skips when `ClanFastOpen` is on and co-op is off, rendering `CaravansText` and `GarrisonsText` as `RefreshPartiesList` does at `ClanPartiesVM.cs:500-503` (`RefreshValues` just reset them with whatever `{CURRENT}` was last set to, which after build 1 is the garrison count, so the Caravans header briefly shows that number); a header fault falls back to the full build. Every other caller builds: the constructor's direct call, `ClanManagementVM.RefreshCategoryValues` (`:1372`) and any co-op handler. The two callers that pair a rebuild with `RefreshValues` are `OnChangeClanNameDone` (`ClanManagementVM.cs:1389-1390`) and the protected `GauntletClanScreen.OnActivate` (`:226-239`); party changes reach `RefreshCategoryValues` through `_onRefresh`.

**Harmony fact.** A bool prefix that returns false does not stop postfixes, so the earlier prefix-based skip still ran every postfix of `RefreshPartiesList` for the skipped call. With the call-site swap, the skipped call never reaches the method and its postfixes run only on real builds. TroopWeight patches the rows' `UpdateProperties`, not this method.

**Brackets.** The finalizers are void, so Harmony keeps `rethrow` and the engine's stack, and run at `Priority.First` so PatchShield's finalizer cannot swallow the exception before it is seen. Each prefix and finalizer pair links only through `__state`: Harmony reruns every finalizer when a later one throws, and PatchShield strips prefixes but never finalizers. `ClanFastOpenHookTests` drive the real prefixes, finalizers and a swap transpiler through Harmony 2.4.2 on dummy classes; `ClanFastOpenBindingTests.RefreshValuesPatch_AppliedToTheInstalledEngine_BuildsAReplacementTheJitAccepts` applies the real transpiler to the real method and prepares it.

**Co-op.** The helper asks `ICoopPresenceProvider.IsCoopActive` (the service's one remaining catch guards that probe), so under co-op it always builds and the screen is vanilla. No bool prefix is left, so `CoopVetoClassificationTests` has no Clan entry. The toggle is presentation in `CoopSettingsRelevance`.

**PatchShield decision.** None of the targets is on a PatchShield exclusion list: it wraps them from a process's second game start and swallows only missing-API exceptions. The patches stay shielded; a swallow on a once-per-open view-model path costs one open at worst and is safer than an exclusion that would also unshield third-party patches on those methods.

**TAOM interplay.** `RefugeClanScreenPatch` (Patch75, postfix on `RefreshPartiesList`) runs after each build that runs, so its rows are added in the first build and stay; the header render counts them.

**Timer.** The constructor pair opens a `[ScreenOpen] Clan open` window through `ScreenOpenTiming` (null while "Log Screen Open Times" is off, which also keeps the service's count window closed) and writes the build counts as its detail: `(fast open on; list builds: 1 run, 2 skipped, 38.1 ms)`, where `on` or `off` is the path the open ran with. The span is the VM build and not the movie load. Main thread. `ClanFastOpenBindingTests` pin the targets and parameter names, the three-build chain in the installed IL, the single swapped call, the header tail of `RefreshPartiesList` and the one constructor. **ACTIVE**, not yet measured in game; kept only if the A/B in the feature doc clears 16 ms.

**In-game correctness checklist:** headers show their counts and refuge rows appear once; disbanding a party or changing a leader updates the lists; renaming the clan updates lists and headers; three reopen cycles show no stale rows; the open line reads `1 run, 2 skipped` with the switch on and `3 run, 0 skipped` off.

**Removal checklist** (when the A/B misses the 16 ms rule): delete `ClanScreenFastPathService.cs`, `ClanFastOpenCalls.cs` and the three Clan hook files; in `ShadowfaxScreensModule.cs` delete the Clan category line, the `ClanScreenFastPathService` registration, the `ClanFastOpenCalls.Initialize` line and the `LogInstall` line in `OnPhase`; delete `TAOM.Tests/Features/ShadowfaxScreens/Clan/` and the Clan rows of `ShadowfaxScreensModuleTests`; delete the "Clan Fast Open" setting in `BattleLoadDiagnosticsSettings`, its provider property, the interface member, its `CoopSettingsRelevance` entry (`Main/Features/CoopInterop/CoopSettingsRelevance.cs`) and their tests; delete the three `ShadowfaxScreens.Clan.Hooks` rows of `taleworlds-api-snapshot/patch-targets.md` and fix its count; delete this section, the Clan section of `shadowfax-screens.md` and the `feature-map.md` clause.

## Patch128_ShadowfaxInventory

**Target:** two classes in `Main/Features/ShadowfaxScreens/Inventory/Hooks/`: the private `SPInventoryVM.InitializeInventory()` (transpiler, plus a prefix and void finalizer that time it) and the one public constructor `SPInventoryVM(InventoryLogic, bool, Func<WeaponComponentData, ItemObject.ItemUsageSetFlags>)` (prefix plus void finalizer, which reads the private `_usageType` field and the `inventoryLogic` argument).

Inventory and trade fast open (issue #852, part of Shadowfax Screens). The category is `Patch128_ShadowfaxInventory`; it applies at `GameInit` through `ShadowfaxScreensModule`, and the module and both hooks name it through `ShadowfaxInventoryCalls.PatchCategory`. The `InitializeInventory` transpiler swaps the two `OrderBy<ItemRosterElement,string>` calls (each followed by `ToArray<ItemRosterElement>`) for a helper that passes the source through when "Inventory Fast Open" is on. It rewrites the existing instruction in place, so labels stay and no branch moves, and applies only when it finds exactly two sites; any other shape returns the IL unmodified and the service logs one line with the reason, so a re-applied category (whose second pass finds no vanilla call) is a no-op. Row order: rows land where vanilla puts them unless they tie on every key the active comparer reads, because the only two callers of `InitializeInventory` (the constructor and `AfterReset`) sort the rows right after it with an `ItemDescription` tie-break; `MBBindingList.Sort` uses the unstable `List<T>.Sort`, so fully tied rows (same name, type and value) can appear in a different relative order. Loot mode sorts the other side by cost. The earlier constructor transpiler on `SPItemVM` (a concept-lookup memo) was deleted: it measured about 0.7 us per row. `IInventoryOpenAdapter` is deleted too: mode and counts come from the patched constructor.

The constructor prefix starts the `[ScreenOpen] Inventory open` window and the void finalizer ends it (also on a throw, which Harmony rethrows with its stack); the link is `__state`, so a stripped prefix leaves a no-op finalizer. The span is the view-model build only, not the movie load in `GauntletInventoryScreen.OnReady`. The `InitializeInventory` prefix and finalizer link through a nullable `__state` and count time only inside a timed open. Toggle off or no service wired: every helper is vanilla's own call. Threads: the view-model constructor and `InitializeInventory` run on the UI main thread. `NameSortRewriteTests`, `InventoryOpenFastPathServiceTests` and `ShadowfaxInventoryCallsTests` cover the logic; `InventoryOpenPatchShapeTests` runs the brackets through real Harmony; `InventoryBindingTests` feeds the installed IL through the rewrite, applies the transpiler to the real `InitializeInventory` and runs `RuntimeHelpers.PrepareMethod` on it (the JIT accepts the result), and pins the targets, parameter names, field type, callers and tie-break. Feature: [shadowfax-screens.md](../features/shadowfax-screens.md). **ACTIVE**, not yet measured in game (A/B in the feature doc; the change is deleted if it misses the 16 ms rule).

**Co-op:** BannerlordCoop's `GameInterface` carries an `SPInventoryVMPatches` class naming `SPInventoryVM`; the co-op module's inventory patches only add postfixes to `SPInventoryVM.SaveItemLockStates` and `SaveItemSortStates`, so they do not touch the constructor or `InitializeInventory`. These hooks observe the constructor and rewrite two calls inside `InitializeInventory`; they write nothing to the network or the save.

**PatchShield:** stays shielded. The targets are in `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.ViewModelCollection.Inventory`, not on the exclusion lists, and each runs once per screen open, so the shield's cost is nil. It swallows only missing-API exceptions inside the wrapped method, and the hooks read no member that can go missing without the binding tests failing first.

**In-game correctness checklist:** sort a merchant list by each column and reopen (the saved sort holds); trade an item (order holds); open a loot screen and a stash (rows sorted); switch hero and Reset (rows stay sorted); compare against the switch off.

**Removal checklist** (if the A/B misses the 16 ms rule): delete `NameSortRewrite.cs`, `InventoryOpenFastPathService.cs`, `ShadowfaxInventoryCalls.cs` (or keep its timer calls) and the two files in `Inventory/Hooks/`; delete the five `TAOM.Tests/Features/ShadowfaxScreens/Inventory/` test files; in `ShadowfaxScreensModule.cs` delete the inventory category line, the `InventoryOpenFastPathService` registration and the `ShadowfaxInventoryCalls.Initialize` line; drop the `InventoryFastOpen` setting from `IShadowfaxScreensSettingsProvider`, its provider, its `CoopSettingsRelevance` entry (`Main/Features/CoopInterop/CoopSettingsRelevance.cs`) and the MCM page; delete the two `ShadowfaxScreens.Inventory.Hooks` rows of `taleworlds-api-snapshot/patch-targets.md` and fix its count; delete this registry section, the feature doc's "Inventory and trade fast open" section and Key Files rows, and the inventory clause of the `feature-map.md` row.

## Patch130_ShadowfaxQuests

**Targets (v1.5.5), six classes in `Main/Features/ShadowfaxScreens/Quests/Hooks/`, category `Patch130_ShadowfaxQuests`, applied at GameInit:**

| Class | Target | Shape |
|---|---|---|
| `QuestsVM_Ctor_QuestLogPatch` | `QuestsVM..ctor(Action)` | void prefix with `out __state`, void finalizer |
| `QuestItemVM_JournalCtor_QuestLogPatch` | `QuestItemVM..ctor(JournalLogEntry, Action<QuestItemVM>, QuestsVM.QuestCompletionType)` | void prefix with `out long __state`, void finalizer |
| `QuestItemVM_PopulateQuestLog_QuestLogPatch` | private `QuestItemVM.PopulateQuestLog(JournalLog, bool)` | bool prefix (`QuestItemVM __instance`) |
| `QuestItemVM_UpdateIsUpdated_QuestLogPatch` | `QuestItemVM.UpdateIsUpdated()` | postfix |
| `QuestItemComparerBase_GetJournalLogAt_QuestLogPatch` | protected `GetJournalLogAt(QuestItemVM questItem, JournalLogIndex logIndex)` of the private abstract nested type `QuestItemSortControllerVM.QuestItemComparerBase`, resolved in `TargetMethod` | postfix (`int logIndex`, `ref JournalLog __result`) |
| `QuestsVM_SetSelectedItem_QuestLogPatch` | private `QuestsVM.SetSelectedItem(QuestItemVM quest)` | void prefix |

Quest Log Fast Open (#854, docs/features/shadowfax-screens.md). Vanilla's `QuestsVM` constructor builds a `QuestItemVM` for every finished quest, and each one builds a `QuestStageVM` per journal log (`PopulateQuestLog`, called only from the three `QuestItemVM` constructors; `QuestLogEntry` is set only by the journal constructor, so it tells a finished quest from an active quest or an issue). The bool prefix skips `PopulateQuestLog` and marks the item as deferred only when all of these hold: the `QuestsVM` constructor prefix opened the scope on this thread, the toggle was on and the adapter bound when it opened, the item has a journal entry, the service is not replaying, and no earlier rebuild threw. A mid-open toggle flip changes nothing for that open.

**Why the screen stays correct.** The `SetSelectedItem` prefix builds a deferred item before vanilla copies its stages: it replays `PopulateQuestLog(entry, false)` for each `JournalLogEntry.GetEntries()` entry through the cached `MethodInfo` (so another mod's patch on that method still runs), then `UpdateIsUpdated` and `RefreshValues`, under a replay flag that makes the deferral prefix step aside. It builds whatever the toggle says now. Finished-quest rows bind `IsUpdated` (vanilla `QuestsScreen.xml:277`), vanilla sets it from the stages, so the `UpdateIsUpdated` postfix sets it for a deferred item from "any journal entry is in `IViewDataTracker.UnExaminedQuestLogs`", the same data `QuestStageVM.IsNew` reads. The sort reads a quest-less item's date from `Stages` (`GetJournalLogAt` lines 15-39 in the decompile) and returns null for an item without stages, which would sort it as undated; the postfix replaces only that null, only for a deferred item, with the first or last journal log the `PopulateQuestLog` prefix recorded on the item's mark (the journal constructor feeds `GetEntries()` in order, so the first and last `log` seen are the first and last entries). `JournalLogIndex` is `nested family` in the private comparer type, so the postfix declares `int logIndex` (the enum's underlying type; `First` is 0 and `Last` is 1, pinned by `QuestLogFastOpenBindingTests`, as is the parameter name `log`). The giver portrait stays vanilla's real `HeroVM` (built in the constructor), so there is no placeholder to show.

**Callers of the target.** `PopulateQuestLog`: the three `QuestItemVM` constructors (pinned by IL in `QuestLogFastOpenBindingTests`), plus the adapter's replay. `SetSelectedItem`: item selection (the item's `_onSelection` delegate), the `QuestsVM` constructor's own selections (saved selection and first item) and the later list-switch calls. `GetJournalLogAt`: the comparers derived from `QuestItemComparerBase`, run by `QuestsVM.OnSortOptionChanged`, which the `SelectorVM` constructor fires inside the `QuestsVM` constructor and a sort change fires later. All on the UI thread.

**Install and fail-closed rules.** `OnPhase(GameInit)` calls `QuestLogDeferService.Install`, which binds the adapter: it resolves `PopulateQuestLog` and requires the `Harmony.GetPatchInfo` lists of `PopulateQuestLog`, `SetSelectedItem`, `UpdateIsUpdated` and `GetJournalLogAt` to carry this feature's patch (the module runner ignores whether a category attached, and a skipped build with no on-demand build would leave quests empty). Any miss leaves the adapter unbound, so nothing defers, and the log says `[QuestLog] fast open unavailable (reason)`. The same four checks run again behind `IsBound` at the start of every open (`QuestLogDeferService.BeginOpen` snapshots the live answer), so a patch removed after the install stops deferral from the next open (`QuestJournalAdapterTests`). A rebuild that throws is logged once (`[QuestLog] building a finished quest on demand threw ...`), disarms deferral for the rest of the process, and leaves that item marked so the sort fallback still works; the item is not retried and the other deferred items still build when selected.

**Coexistence.** The `PopulateQuestLog` prefix is a bool prefix: Harmony 2.4.2 skips later bool prefixes on the method when this one returns false, and the adapter's replay runs the whole prefix chain again with the replay flag set. The two constructor patches are void and observe only; a void finalizer keeps Harmony's rethrow, and `__state` links each pair, so a finalizer whose prefix PatchShield stripped closes nothing (`QuestLogPatchTests`). Co-op: client-side UI only, no campaign state written; the toggle is presentation in `CoopSettingsRelevance`, and the bool prefix has a `CoopVetoClassificationTests` entry.

**Disarm path.** A rebuild that throws part way can leave stages already added to the item. The item stays marked and is never retried, so the sort fallback keeps reading its recorded first and last logs and the dot postfix answers for any marked item, so a later `UpdateIsUpdated` call (a stage's examination callback) derives the dot from the journal entries' unexamined logs, not from the partial stages. Deferral is off for the process, and the next open line reads `fast open off;`.

**Open line.** The detail starts `fast open on;` only when the open ran with the toggle on, the adapter bound and no disarm; otherwise `fast open off;`. `updated=` skips the calls made inside a replay.

**Co-op.** Nothing in BannerlordCoop references the quest view models, and these patches write no campaign state, so a co-op session is unaffected.

**PatchShield decision: stays shielded.** The targets are on neither exclusion list and run once per open or per selection on the main thread; the shield swallows only missing-API exceptions, and a stripped patch is the safe outcome here because the per-open attachment check then turns deferral off.

**In-game correctness checklist.** (1) Open the Quest log with the switch on: finished quests list, select one, and its stages appear. (2) Sort by date started and by last updated with the switch on and off: the order is the same. (3) A finished quest with an unexamined log shows the updated dot before it is built. (4) Turn the switch off while the log is open and select a deferred quest: it still builds. (5) The open line reads `fast open on;` with the switch on and `fast open off;` with it off.

**Removal checklist** (to delete this screen): delete `Main/Features/ShadowfaxScreens/Quests/` and `TAOM.Tests/Features/ShadowfaxScreens/Quests/`; delete `Main/Adapters/IQuestJournalAdapter.cs`, `Main/Adapters/NullQuestJournalAdapter.cs`, `Client/Adapters/QuestJournalAdapter.cs` and `TAOM.Tests/Adapters/NullQuestJournalAdapterTests.cs`, the `IQuestJournalAdapter` registration in `Client/TaomClientComposition.cs` and its rows in `ClientSubModuleTests` and `TaomClientCompositionTests`; in `ShadowfaxScreensModule.cs` the quest category line, the adapter and service registrations, the `QuestLogDeferCalls.Initialize` line and the `OnPhase` bind; the `QuestLogFastOpen` member in `IShadowfaxScreensSettingsProvider`, `ShadowfaxScreensSettingsProvider`, `BattleLoadDiagnosticsSettings`, `CoopSettingsRelevance` and `ShadowfaxScreensSettingsProviderTests`; the `QuestItemVM_PopulateQuestLog_QuestLogPatch` entry in `CoopVetoClassificationTests`; the `PopulateQuestLog` and `SetSelectedItem` rows in `ReflectionSiteBindingTests` and `reflection-sites.md`; the six `ShadowfaxScreens.Quests.Hooks` rows of `taleworlds-api-snapshot/patch-targets.md` and its count; in `ShadowfaxScreensModule.OnPhase`, reword the server gate's comment (one install line is left) and keep its server test for that line; the Quest log section of `shadowfax-screens.md`; this registry section; the module wiring test cases that name the quest service.

## Patch_MainRosterLossDiag

**Targets:** `TroopRoster.AddToCountsAtIndex(int, int, int, int, bool)` and `TroopRoster.SetElementNumber(int, int)` (both Prefix, observe-only, never skip the original). **ACTIVE, TEMPORARY diagnostic.** Code: `Main/Features/TroopWeight/Diagnostics/` (`TroopRoster_AddToCountsAtIndex_Patch`, `TroopRoster_SetElementNumber_Patch`, `MainRosterLossDiagnostics`).

**Feature:** [troop-weight-system.md](../features/troop-weight-system.md#main-roster-loss-diagnostic-temporary-2026-10-17).

Player report (2026-10-17): about 60 non-hero troops vanished from the main party right after an Elite Emissary purchase. No TAOM path found removes a whole roster at once: `AlignmentDesertion` removes daily and shows a popup, and special-resource desertion takes 10% per type per day. `AddToCounts`, `Clear`, `RemoveIf`, `RemoveTroop`, `RemoveNumberOfNonHeroTroopsRandomly` and `Add` all reach `AddToCountsAtIndex`; `SetElementNumber` is the one public write that skips it and updates no cached total, so it has its own prefix.

**One line:**

```
[RosterLoss] tick=1234 -3 gondor_recruit (row 10 -> 7, regulars 60 -> 57) via <caller> <- <caller> ...
```

It is a DEBUG line (queued by the logger, no synchronous flush on the casualty path), written to `Logs/taom_debug_<timestamp>.log`. The chain is up to 8 managed frames with TAOM diagnostic, Harmony, MonoMod and `System.` frames dropped. Only the main party's `MemberRoster` and only non-hero troops are logged. There is no toggle and no MCM switch. On a line whose first frame is `TroopRoster.SetElementNumber_Patch<N>` (Harmony's replacement method), `regulars X -> Y` is the row sum after the write: in single player the cached `TotalRegulars` stays at X until the next load, so later lines on that roster read too high by the amount removed (BannerlordCoop recomputes the cache after its own calls).

**`tick=`** is `Campaign.CurrentTickCount`, which `Campaign.Tick` advances once per call, and `MapState` calls it only while active. One tick is therefore one map frame, or one whole party-screen session, or one whole mission. Group lines by `tick=` within one load: the counter is not saved and restarts at 0 on every load and new campaign, so split loads by the line timestamps. One tick can hold several actions (the main party's daily listeners run in the same tick); the chain tells them apart.

**Read the lines.** Benign sources and their chains:

| Source | Chain contains |
|---|---|
| Battle deaths | `MapEventParty.OnTroopKilled` -> `RemoveTroop` |
| Routs | `MapEventParty.OnTroopRouted` |
| Autoresolve | `MapEventSide.ApplySimulationDamageToSelectedTroop` |
| Party-screen upgrade | `PartyScreenLogic.UpgradeTroop` (remove, then add the upgraded troop) |
| Transfers, dismissals | `PartyScreenLogic` transfer paths |
| Party-screen Esc or Cancel | `PartyScreenData.ResetUsing` (`Clear` then re-add, net zero) |
| Daily desertion | `AlignmentDesertionBehavior` |
| Vanilla desertion (low morale, wage limit, or members over `PartySizeLimit`, which TroopWeight lowers for the main party) | `DesertionCampaignBehavior.CheckDesertionForParty` |
| Special-resource desertion | the special-resource daily tick |

A culprit is a same-tick run that drives `regulars` toward the surviving count with no matching re-add. Never filter `ResetUsing` out by name: a reset from a stale snapshot (`ResetToLastSavedPartyScreenData`) is itself a candidate.

**Companion lines to collect** from `Logs/taom_debug_<timestamp>.log`: `[TroopCountDiag]`, `[EliteEmissary] PURCHASE`, `[SpecRes]`, `[AlignDesert]`, and the engine's `[High Desertion Alert]` in the rgl log.

**Not covered:** a loss across a save and reload (the cached totals are recomputed on load), over-removals (the engine's `TroopRosterElement.Number` setter throws on a negative value, so nothing leaves the roster and nothing is logged) and writes by reflection.

**Threads:** removals can arrive from `Mission.OnAgentRemoved` (battle deaths via `MapEventParty.OnTroopKilled` -> `RemoveTroop`), possibly off the main thread. The patch holds no mutable state except a log-once flag, and `FileLogger` is thread-safe. Logging only.

**PatchShield:** from the second game start in a process, PatchShield wraps every patched method (`Dependencies/Foundation/PatchShield.cs`). While this diagnostic is on, a `MissingMethodException`, `MissingFieldException` or `TypeLoadException` raised inside a roster change on these two methods is swallowed and the method returns its default instead of propagating. These methods are not excluded, because exclusion would remove the shield from third-party patches on them.

**Co-op:** BannerlordCoop also prefixes both methods. Its only skip is a duplicate hero addition, which this diagnostic ignores.

**Apply timing:** the `OnGameInitializationFinished` batch, after `Patch17_TroopWeight`, with `TroopRoster_AddToCountsAtIndex_Patch.Initialize(logger)` called just before it.

**Removal checklist** (when the report is root-caused): delete both patch files, `MainRosterLossDiagnostics.cs` and `MainRosterLossDiagnosticsTests.cs`; delete the two `SubModule.cs` lines and their comment; delete this registry section; delete the `troop-weight-system.md` section and its Key Files and test-list mentions; delete the two `patch-targets.md` rows (and fix its count); delete the `feature-map.md` clause.

**Tests:** `MainRosterLossDiagnosticsTests` (formatter, caller-chain filter, `SubModule.cs` wiring and the category on both patch files).

## Patch129_ShadowfaxParty

**Target:** six classes in `Main/Features/ShadowfaxScreens/Party/Hooks/`, category `PartyFastOpenCalls.PatchCategory` (`Patch129_ShadowfaxParty`), applied at GameInit through `ShadowfaxScreensModule`. All v1.5.5 `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.ViewModelCollection.Party`: `PartyVM(PartyScreenLogic)` constructor (prefix and finalizer), `PartyVM.OnFinalize` (prefix), private `PartyVM.InitializeTroopLists()` (prefix and finalizer, timing only), `PartyVM.RefreshValues()` (prefix and finalizer, timing only), private `PartyVM.TransferAllTroops(PartyScreenLogic.PartyCommand)` (prefix and finalizer, timing only) and private `PartyCharacterVM.GetCharacterCode(CharacterObject character, TroopType type, PartyRosterSide side)` (a bool prefix that sets `__result` on a hit, plus a postfix that stores on a miss).

Party Fast Open (#853, [shadowfax-screens.md](../features/shadowfax-screens.md) "Party fast open"). The constructor prefix opens a `PartyScreenFastPathService` session and a `[ScreenOpen] Party open` timer window; the session ends at that `PartyVM`'s `OnFinalize` or at the next `PartyVM` constructor, whose `BeginScreen` clears it (Talk deactivates and reactivates the screen and abandons the old view model without `OnFinalize`). One cache serves the row builds: the character code, for the whole session. Every prefix and finalizer pair is linked only through `__state` (a phase clock start), so a stripped prefix leaves its finalizer a no-op; every hook catches everything and falls through to vanilla. The `GetCharacterCode` prefix returns false only on a hit and then skips the whole method body: vanilla computes the equipment code and the body properties itself there, and `CharacterCode.CreateFrom` only builds the string. The open line's detail starts `fast open on;` or `fast open off;`. The item-category count cache is gone (measured 0.1 to 1 ms per open).

**Callers.** `GetCharacterCode` has two call sites, both in `PartyCharacterVM.cs` (lines 175 and 183 in v1.5.5), each passing the instance's own `Type` and `Side`; only `PartyVM` constructs `PartyCharacterVM`. `InitializeTroopLists` is called by the constructor, `Reset` and `TransferAllTroops`.

**Interplay.** BannerColorPersistence (`Patch23`) postfixes the same `GetCharacterCode` and, since #993, replaces the result with a code rebuilt in the clan colours (`ClanColoredCharacterCode.Recolor`, which returns the same instance when the colours already match). Patch23 is applied before GameInit and both postfixes have the default priority, so it runs first and the cache stores the recoloured code; on a cache hit `Recolor` returns that code unchanged. `PartyFastOpenBindingTests` pins that `CharacterImageIdentifier`, `CharacterImageIdentifierVM` and `UpgradeTargetVM` read only the shared code's `Code`.

**Co-op:** the cache holds codes for the screen's own rosters and no campaign state. Coop's roster refresher calls the screen's `OnReset`, which rebuilds rows through the same hook, so codes stay correct. The remaining bool prefix is classified ReviewedSafe in `CoopVetoClassificationTests`.

**PatchShield:** stays shielded. PatchShield wraps these targets from a process's second game start and swallows only missing-API exceptions; none of the targets is on its exclusion lists, and every target is a presentation method whose worst swallowed outcome is a missing row or timer line, never campaign state.

**In-game correctness checklist:** open the party screen with a mixed army and prisoners and check each row's portrait; expand an upgrade row and check the upgrade-target portraits; press Transfer All and Reset; Talk, return and reopen; repeat once with the toggle off.

**Removal checklist** (deleting Party Fast Open): delete `Main/Features/ShadowfaxScreens/Party/` and `TAOM.Tests/Features/ShadowfaxScreens/Party/`; in `ShadowfaxScreensModule.cs` delete the `using`, the category line, the `Register<PartyScreenFastPathService>` line and the `PartyFastOpenCalls.Initialize` line; delete `PartyFastOpen` from `IShadowfaxScreensSettingsProvider`, `ShadowfaxScreensSettingsProvider`, `BattleLoadDiagnosticsSettings` and `CoopSettingsRelevance`, and its rows in `ShadowfaxScreensSettingsProviderTests` and the module wiring tests; delete the `PartyCharacterVM_GetCharacterCode_ShadowfaxParty_Patch` entry in `CoopVetoClassificationTests`; delete the setting's translation rows, this section, the `shadowfax-screens.md` Party section and its Configuration row.

**Tests:** `PartyScreenFastPathServiceTests`, `PartyFastOpenPatchShapeTests` (real Harmony around stand-ins, every prefix/finalizer pair), `PartyFastOpenBindingTests` (IL premises tagged RequiresGameIL), `ShadowfaxScreensModuleTests` (the wiring). **Not yet measured or smoked in game.**


## Patch131_FordsOfWar

**Target:** one class, `FordsTowerHitPatch` in `Main/Features/FordsOfWar/Hooks/`, category `Patch131_FordsOfWar` (the string is `FordsOfWarModule.PatchCategory`, and the module declares it for `ApplyPhase.FirstMission`). A void prefix on `DestructableComponent.OnHit(Agent attackerAgent, int inflictedDamage, Vec3 impactPosition, Vec3 impactDirection, in MissionWeapon weapon, int affectorWeaponSlotOrMissileIndex, ScriptComponentBehavior attackerScriptComponentBehavior, out bool reportDamage, out float modifiedDamage, out float fireDamage, out float modifiedFireDamage)`, a `protected internal override` (v1.5.6 installed DLL), taking `object __instance`, `Agent attackerAgent`, `ref int inflictedDamage` and `ref MissionWeapon weapon`.

**Why:** a Fords of War tower is a destructible entity whose damage is a rule, not a hit: a tower takes nothing while another tower stands in front of it in its lane, ranged blows count 0.15, hero blows 4, siege troops 3. The prefix hands the blow to `IFordsTowerHitHook` (the mission director) and writes back the damage the match allows. A component that is not a registered Fords tower comes back unchanged, so every other destructible in the game is untouched, and with no match running the prefix is one volatile read. A ranged blow is a missile (the weapon is the arrow or bolt) or any weapon whose current usage is ranged, ammo or consumable.

**Callers and thread:** `Mission.OnEntityHit` (Mission.cs:2247) calls `OnHit` on every script of the hit entity, reached from `Mission.MeleeHitCallback` and `Mission.MissileHitCallback`, both `[MBCallback(null, false)]` natives. Native chooses their thread and no log has shown which, so the patch is written as if it may run off the main thread: it registers no blow, spawns nothing and touches no main-thread collection. The hook takes the director's gate (never held across an engine call) and the adapters' own locks. The tower's `OnDestroyed` event fires inside the same `OnHit`, and the director's handler only enqueues the work on its `DeferredCallbackQueue` (`Enqueue`, not `RunOrDefer`, which would run it at once on the main thread, inside the hit). The next mission tick's `Drain` then marks the tower fallen, tells the match (`OnTowerDestroyed`) and fades the gunners (`KillGunners`).

**Never throws:** the body sits in a try and a fault logs once and leaves the vanilla damage. The engine member reads sit in a `NoInlining` helper called inside the try.

**Tests:** `FordsOfWarBindingTests` pins the target (the only declared overload, the eleven parameter names, `in MissionWeapon`, the `out` parameters), that the prefix binds by name with the damage by reference, and the category; `FordsTowerHitPatchTests` runs the prefix body. `FordsOfWarModuleTests` pins the category, its phase and that the patch names the module's constant. **ACTIVE: the module applies the category at the first mission; not yet checked in game.** The in-game check is that melee units ordered to attack a tower reduce its hit points, and that a locked tower takes none.
